# Session: Musikmatch — Rust core, multi-source library, vfs-sync integration

**Date**: 2026-08-11 20:34
**Summary**: Rename the project to Musikmatch, move the core to Rust (axum/tokio/sqlx/rodio/symphonia/lofty),
restructure `.meta` into per-track sidecars with a content-addressed image store, and adopt
`@cloudauthn/vfs-sync` as the synchronisation layer across multiple sources.

**Status**: Proposal — awaiting approval. No code changed yet.

---

## 1. Context

The project is today a zero-backend browser app (React 18 + Vite + TypeScript) that edits ID3 tags
in place through the File System Access API. Two documents describe it: [STACK.md](../STACK.md) and
[THESAURUS.md](../THESAURUS.md).

It becomes **Musikmatch**: a music player *and* a file organiser/renamer, running on web, desktop
and mobile, over several storage sources that can be synchronised with each other.

### What the audit found in the current `.meta` implementation

Reviewed in [Mp3ManagerView.tsx](../src/views/Mp3ManagerView.tsx) and [id3.ts](../src/lib/id3.ts).

Worth keeping:

- Dual checksums (`fullChecksum` over the whole file, `audioChecksum` over the audio payload with
  ID3v2/ID3v1/APE stripped) — `getAudioFrameSlice` / `calculateDualChecksums`, id3.ts:113-175.
- Fast re-hydration validated by `(size, lastModified)` without reading bytes.
- Two-phase hydration: render from the snapshot first, walk the filesystem in the background.

Must change:

1. **Cover art is base64 inside the JSON.** `MetaRecord.metadata` carries the full `TrackMetadata`
   including `coverDataUrl`. At ~700 tracks this is a multi-hundred-MB file, re-serialised whole
   (`JSON.stringify(snapshot, null, 2)`) every 10 verified files (Mp3ManagerView.tsx:341-343).
2. **The primary key is `relativePath`**, in an app whose purpose is renaming files. A rename
   discards the record, including the `audioChecksum` that exists precisely to be rename-stable.
3. **Three uncoordinated writers** to the same file — the background verifier mutating a captured
   snapshot in place (L290-343), phase 2 of `hydrateRoot`, and batch edit (L1177-1182). Last writer
   wins over the entire library.
4. **O(n) rewrite per change.**
5. **`version !== 1` silently yields an empty snapshot** (L1503) and then overwrites the file. A
   future v2 opened by an old build destroys the library.
6. **`VfsAdapter` is decorative.** Its only consumer is the `implements` clause in
   [browserVfs.ts:14](../src/vfs/browserVfs.ts#L14); the view is typed against the concrete
   `BrowserVfs` and uses six methods absent from the interface (`mountFromHandle`,
   `hasReadWritePermission`, `getRootHandle`, `getRootName`, `readTextFile`, `writeTextFile`).
7. **Unstable file ids.** `${relativePath}#${lastModified}#${size}` (browserVfs.ts:188) changes
   exactly when the file changes, which is why phase 2 has to patch `vfsFileId` after the walk.

## 2. Decisions

| # | Decision | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D1 | Rust core: tokio, axum, sqlx/SQLite, symphonia, rodio, **lofty** | symphonia cannot write tags; lofty reads *and* writes ID3v2/MP4/FLAC/Vorbis plus artwork |
| D2 | Multi-format from day one; `Mp3Track` → `Track` | lofty and symphonia make it nearly free |
| D3 | Zero-install web mode survives | Cloud sources are HTTPS+CORS from the browser; the daemon becomes *one source among several*, not a requirement |
| D4 | MusicBrainz/AcoustID: reserve schema fields now, build matching later | `mbid_recording`, `mbid_release`, `acoustid` are free to reserve, expensive to retrofit onto synced replicas |
| D5 | One tag engine everywhere: lofty compiled to wasm for browser sources | If the browser wrote ID3 with `browser-id3-writer` and desktop with lofty, byte output would differ, hashes would never match and sync would loop. This is a correctness requirement, not an optimisation |
| D6 | `@cloudauthn/vfs-sync` stays **TypeScript only** for now | Owner's call. Consequence: the sync engine runs in the JS layer on all targets (Tauri's frontend is JS) |
| D7 | SHA-256 everywhere; **drop blake3** | vfs-sync content-addresses with SHA-256. Reusing it means `full_hash` comes free from the tree entry and image filenames equal the engine's blob address |
| D8 | Two views: Player and Organiser | The Player is read-only over the index and needs no write permission on any source — which is what makes iOS viable. Organiser ships on desktop and web only |
| D9 | Canonical tags live in `.meta`; writing them into the audio files is an explicit apply/export | See §5 — this is what keeps the sync engine's two missing features from mattering |

### Deferred / open

- **O1**: whether vfs-sync eventually collapses to a single Rust implementation compiled to wasm
  (zero drift, one engine) or keeps a dual TS+Rust implementation (needs a versioned `.vfs/` spec
  and a shared conformance suite). D6 defers this; it does not settle it.
- **O2**: album key. MusicBrainz release MBID when known; otherwise a generated UUID stored in the
  record. **Not** a hash of (album_artist, album, year) — that changes when a typo is corrected.
- **O3**: default policy for keeping artwork embedded in the audio files (proposed default: keep
  embedded, with `.meta/images/` as the canonical store).

---

## 3. Target architecture

```
crates/
  core/     domain: Track, Album, identity, rename rules. No I/O.
  tags/     lofty — read/write tags and artwork over bytes. Compiles to wasm.
  index/    sqlx + SQLite: schema, migrations, queries.
  scan/     walk + (size, mtime) fast path + hashing + notify.
  audio/    symphonia (decode, duration) + rodio (native output).
  server/   axum — same handlers behind every transport.
apps/
  desktop/  Tauri v2, reuses the React UI.
  mobile/   Tauri v2 iOS/Android — Player only.
  web/      React UI; sources are FSA/OPFS/Drive directly, or the daemon over HTTP.
```

### The storage seam

`VfsAdapter` is replaced by an **operations** interface rather than a bytes interface. Shipping
whole files across an IPC or HTTP boundary just to hash or tag them is strictly worse than what
exists today.

```rust
#[async_trait]
pub trait StorageBackend: Send + Sync {
    async fn scan(&self, opts: ScanOpts) -> Result<Vec<FileStat>>;
    async fn hash(&self, id: &FileId) -> Result<Hashes>;        // full + audio
    async fn read_tags(&self, id: &FileId) -> Result<Tags>;
    async fn write_tags(&self, id: &FileId, tags: &Tags) -> Result<()>;
    async fn rename(&self, id: &FileId, to: &RelPath) -> Result<FileId>;
    async fn open_stream(&self, id: &FileId, range: Range) -> Result<ByteStream>;
    async fn watch(&self) -> Result<Receiver<FsEvent>>;
}
```

`FileId` stays opaque and, unlike today, **stable** — Android SAF hands out content URIs and iOS
security-scoped bookmarks, so paths are not universal identifiers.

### The JS/Rust boundary

- **JS owns**: FSA/OPFS handle management, the directory picker, permission prompts, byte read and
  write. Roughly what [browserVfs.ts](../src/vfs/browserVfs.ts) already is.
- **wasm (Rust) owns**: tag parse/write, hashing, audio-payload slicing, rename rules.
- **Native Rust owns**: the same crates plus the real filesystem, cloud clients, rodio/symphonia.

The index is the one component genuinely written twice: sqlx does not compile to wasm. Proposal —
SQLite in the browser via wa-sqlite over OPFS, with schema, migrations and queries kept as shared
SQL text and two thin drivers.

---

## 4. `.meta` format

```
.meta/
├── tracks/<audio_sha256>.json       # tags, current path, uuid, mbids, full_hash
├── albums/<album_key>.json          # album_artist, year, images[]
├── artists/<artist_key>.json        # images[]
├── images/<sha256>.<role>.<ext>     # content-addressed, deduplicated
└── library.json                     # root settings, schema version
.vfs/                                # sync state, owned by vfs-sync
```

**Per-track sidecars are mandatory, not a preference.** A single JSON plus a file-level syncer means
one conflict takes the whole library; split per track, the worst case is a conflict copy of one song.

**Sidecars are named by content, not by path.** If the sidecar were path-named, renaming the audio
on peer A while editing tags on peer B is a rename-vs-edit conflict on one file. With an immutable
name, renaming is just editing a field in a small JSON. Deduplication comes free. The 1:1 reflection
of audio files in `.meta` is preserved — it is logical (one record per audio) rather than positional
(the tree shape is not mirrored).

**Torn renames self-heal.** A rename touches two things (the audio file's name, the `path` field in
its sidecar), so a peer can observe an inconsistent state. No transactional support is needed from
vfs-sync: the tree entry `id` says the file moved and `audio_sha256` says which record it belongs
to, so reconciliation needs no re-hashing.

### Images

Role in the filename, as requested: `images/<sha256>.front.jpg`.

- The `<sha256>` prefix **is** the identity, and equals the vfs-sync blob address (D7).
- The role suffix is a **non-authoritative hint** for human browsing. The record is the source of
  truth. Where an image holds several roles, the suffix is picked by a fixed precedence
  (`front > back > disc > booklet > artist > label`) so every peer derives the same name.
- If two peers do write the same bytes under different suffixes, vfs-sync stores and transfers
  **one blob** — the cost is a second working-folder file, reconciled by app-level GC, not a second
  transfer.
- The suffix token must be spelled exactly like the role in the record, so hint and truth cannot
  disagree. Vocabulary follows the MusicBrainz Cover Art Archive (`front`, `back`, `disc`,
  `booklet`, `artist`, `label`), which also maps ~1:1 onto the ID3 APIC picture-type byte, making
  extraction lossless. **Note**: this replaces the `cover` token from the original sketch —
  `front` is the CAA term.
- Records carry `images: [{ hash, role, mime, w, h }]`.

Album- and artist-level records exist because a back cover belongs to a release and a photo to an
artist. Hanging them off track sidecars would have twelve songs each claiming the same artwork,
turning every artwork edit into a twelve-way conflict.

### Identity: two layers, both needed

- **vfs-sync `id`** — identity of a *file in the mesh*. Survives renames; this is what stops a moved
  album from re-transferring.
- **`audio_sha256`** — identity of the *musical content*. Survives retagging and renaming, and —
  which the mesh id cannot do — identifies the same recording arriving at two sources by independent
  paths, having never synced. Without it there is no dedup and no library merge.

`full_hash` is not computed by Musikmatch at all: it is the vfs-sync tree entry's `hash` (D7).

---

## 5. vfs-sync

Reviewed at `.tmp/vfs-sync` (v0.1.21, MIT). It is a git-shaped engine: SHA-256 content-addressed
blobs, tree snapshots, commits with parents, real ancestor negotiation, mesh topology, tombstones,
rename intent carried in the tree, logical mtime, conflict copies. Adapters: OPFS, FSA, Google
Drive, Node fs, Memory.

Consequences for the design above:

- **Ancestor bookkeeping is not our problem.** Earlier in this design I planned to record
  per-replica `last_synced_*` state; the engine's commit graph already does it. Do not duplicate it.
- `readRange` already exists, and its doc comment cites this exact use case ("parse the ID3 tag of
  a 200 MB track" for a few hundred bytes).
- Blob dedup across paths and peers is already there, which is what makes the image naming scheme
  cheap.

### Layering invariant

vfs-sync's scope is *N peer folders, content-agnostic*, and that must hold. **Test: if vfs-sync ever
needs to know what an MP3 is, the layering is wrong.** `.meta/` is, to the engine, an ordinary
folder of files it does not interpret.

### Proposed contributions (all content-agnostic)

Ordered by value to Musikmatch. Following the codebase's existing convention: optional adapter
methods with emulated fallbacks, as `fileId?()` and the streaming trio already do.

1. **Garbage collection.** Listed under "Not implemented" in `docs/architecture.md`; unreachable
   blobs are never removed. For a music library this is acute — every tag write rewrites the whole
   file, minting a new blob and orphaning the old one. A 200 GB library retagged twice would carry
   ~600 GB of `objects/`.
2. **Selective materialisation** — letting a peer hold a file's metadata without its content. This
   is what makes mobile viable: sync `.meta/` in full, browse a 200 GB library, fetch bytes on
   demand. Generic (Dropbox smart sync), and it unlocks a whole target.
3. **Delta transfer.** Also listed as not implemented: a one-byte change re-sends the whole file.
   Because ID3v2 sits at the head of the file, a tag edit shifts everything after it, so
   content-defined chunking (FastCDC) suits this better than fixed-block rsync.
4. **Backend-native transfer fast path.** When both peers share a backend, skip streaming through
   the engine — Drive→Drive is an API call with zero bytes on the wire.
5. **A resolver hook on `sync()`.** `mergeTrees` is pure and exported, but the documented recipe for
   custom resolution requires supplying your own `commonAncestorTree` — i.e. reimplementing the
   hardest part of the engine. A hook (by path pattern or mime) would let Musikmatch register
   per-field JSON merging for sidecars without leaving the engine, and would keep music semantics
   out of vfs-sync.
6. **Native digest passthrough** — Drive `md5Checksum`, Box `sha1`, Dropbox `content_hash`, to
   compare without downloading. Caveat: algorithms differ per provider, so it must be treated as an
   opaque digest comparable **only within one backend**, never across two.

### Why D9 matters

Items 1 and 3 above are the sync engine's two known gaps, and both are triggered by the same thing:
rewriting a 10 MB audio file to change 20 bytes of tag. Keeping canonical tags in `.meta` and making
"write tags into the audio files" an explicit operation means the audio blobs stop churning during
normal editing — which demotes both gaps from blocking to merely desirable.

---

## 6. Milestones

**M0 — Reshape the interface, in TypeScript, before any Rust.** Turn `VfsAdapter` into the
operations interface, implement it over FSA (accepting that `rename` is copy+delete there and
`hash` still reads bytes), migrate the view onto it and fix the concrete-class typing. Validates the
contract against the real UI at zero migration risk: if the shape is wrong, we find out in
TypeScript.

**M1 — `.meta` v2.** Per-track sidecars, `images/` store, album/artist records, migration from
`.meta/mp3-manager.json` that preserves existing hashes. Schema versioning that refuses to
overwrite what it does not understand (fixing audit item 5).

**M2 — Rust core.** `core` + `index` + `scan` + `tags`; axum daemon that scans a folder and serves
the library; importer for `.meta` v2. Frontend talks to it through an `HttpBackend` implementing the
M0 interface — no UI component changes.

**M3 — vfs-sync integration.** Two peers over `.meta` first (small, textual, conflict-friendly),
audio blobs second. GC and selective materialisation contributed as needed.

**M4 — Playback.** Keep `<audio>` on web; rodio/symphonia on the native path.

**M5 — Packaging.** Tauri v2 desktop, then mobile with the Player view only.

---

## 7. Rename

`mp3-manager` → `musikmatch` across package name, README, STACK.md, THESAURUS.md and the domain
types. Best done at the start of M1, while the surface is still small.

## Extension — 2026-08-11 21:05 — Open questions closed, in-file identity model

Resolves O1, O2 and O3, and adds D10: what may and may not be written into the audio files.

### O2 — Album key: generated UUID

Minted once, stored in the album record. Never derived from `(album_artist, album, year)`, which
changes when a typo is fixed. MusicBrainz release MBID recorded alongside it when known, as an
attribute rather than as the key.

### O3 — `.meta/images/` is a mirror of what the audio file carries

Decided: the extracted image is byte-identical to the embedded APIC payload. **Supersedes the
downscaled-thumbnail proposal** — no derived renditions, no divergence between what the file has
and what `.meta` shows.

Consequences:

- The audio file stays the source of truth for its own artwork; `.meta/images/` is a faithful,
  rebuildable projection of it.
- The role suffix comes straight from the APIC picture-type byte, so extraction is lossless in
  both directions.
- Deduplication still holds: the same artwork embedded in twelve tracks yields twelve identical
  payloads, one SHA-256, one file.
- Because it is derivable, `.meta/images/` is technically a cache — but it must still be synced,
  since a peer holding metadata without content (selective materialisation, §5 item 2) has no
  audio files to project it from. This is what lets a phone show covers for a library whose bytes
  it does not hold.
- Accepted cost: changing artwork rewrites every affected audio file. Mitigated by D9 — the
  rewrite happens on apply, not on every edit in the UI.

### O1 — Dual implementation, TS + Rust, with a versioned spec

Decided. The `.vfs/` on-disk format and the engine's semantics become a public contract between two
codebases; divergence causes silent data loss rather than an error, so the spec and a **shared
conformance suite** are the real deliverables, not the second implementation.

Every rule the docs describe as "deterministic so both peers compute it identically" is a
conformance hazard and must be pinned down in the spec:

| Area | What must match exactly |
| --- | --- |
| Trees | Canonical JSON encoding, entries sorted by `id` |
| Hashing | SHA-256, and precisely which bytes are covered |
| Identity | The five-step id resolution order (rename intent → path continuity → hash heuristic → hash cache → fresh id) |
| First encounter | Fall back to path matching; lexicographically smaller id wins |
| `storeId` | Both peers adopt the lexicographically smaller |
| Merge | Per-dimension resolution, newer `mtime` wins, hash tiebreak |
| Conflict copies | Name derived from content, so repeated merges produce the same path and id |
| Ancestor | Frontier computation, ordered by timestamp then hash |

Suite shape: scenario fixtures in a language-neutral format (initial state of N peers, operations,
expected converged tree and commit hash) executed by both implementations, plus property tests
generating random operation sequences and asserting that all peers converge with no data loss.

### D10 — What goes in the audio file

The earlier ruling ("no sync information in ID3") was drawn too broadly. The real dividing line is
not ID3-versus-sidecar, it is **peer-invariant versus peer-specific**.

- **Peer-invariant** data converges: every replica ends up with identical bytes. It can live in the
  file.
- **Peer-specific** data cannot, by construction. "Last synced with peer X at T" has three
  different values across three peers for one shared object. Its home is `.vfs/`.

`fileHash` additionally cannot be stored in its own file — writing it changes the bytes it
describes. `audioHash` can: it covers only the audio payload, so a tag write leaves it valid. It is
a fixed point under retagging.

Adopting the XMP Media Management model, which solves this exact problem for images and PDFs:

| Frame (`TXXX` / `UFID`, owner `musikmatch`) | XMP analogue | Written | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `track_id` | `xmpMM:DocumentID` | Once, at import | Identity across retag and rename |
| `origin_id` | `xmpMM:OriginalDocumentID` | On derivation | Lineage when `audioHash` breaks — transcode, normalise, re-encode |
| `rev_id` | `xmpMM:InstanceID` | Every write | Identifies this byte state without being a function of it |
| `audio_sha256` | — | Every write | Content identity; precedent: Picard stores `Acoustid Fingerprint` the same way |

`rev_id` is what the "hash of the last sync" idea was reaching for. A file cannot contain its own
hash, but it can contain a token minted at write time that serves the same purpose. Comparing it
against the index distinguishes three cases vfs-sync alone cannot:

- **Same `rev_id`** — untouched, whatever the filesystem timestamps say.
- **Different but known `rev_id`** — edited by another Musikmatch peer.
- **Missing or unknown `rev_id`** — edited by a foreign tool (iTunes, Picard, a car stereo).

Cost rule: none of these ever trigger a write on their own. They piggyback on writes already
happening — import, or applying tags — so they add no churn. On read-only or write-expensive
sources they are simply skipped, and `audio_sha256` still covers the normal case.

References: [XMP Media Management namespace](https://developer.adobe.com/xmp/docs/xmp-namespaces/xmp-mm/),
[Picard ID3 formats](https://github.com/metabrainz/picard/blob/master/picard/formats/id3.py).

## Extension — 2026-08-11 21:40 — Ignore rules

Adds contribution 7 to §5, plus a data-loss bug that must be fixed before any of it.

### What already exists

`VFSNode.open(adapter, { ignore })` takes a predicate (`WalkOptions.ignore`, walk.ts:11), and
`docs/recipes.md` already sketches reading patterns from a `.vfsignore`. The mechanism is there;
what is missing is a file format and the fix below.

### Prerequisite bug: ignoring a tracked file deletes it across the mesh

`scan()` derives what disappeared from the paths the walk returned, and the walk applies `ignore`:

```ts
const files = await walk(this.adapter, this.ignore ? { ignore: this.ignore } : {});  // vfs-node.ts:193
const vanished = prevLive.filter((e) => !livePaths.has(e.path));                      // vfs-node.ts:228
```

So a file that was already tracked and then becomes ignored is indistinguishable from a deleted
one: it enters `vanished`, gets a tombstone, and the tombstone propagates and removes it on every
peer. `docs/recipes.md` states ignored paths "never appear as deletes on the other side", which
holds only for paths that were *never* tracked.

Fix: "not walked" and "not present" must be different things — `scan()` must exclude currently
ignored paths from the `vanished` computation and leave their entries untouched. Git solves the
same problem by refusing to ignore already-tracked files. This is a prerequisite for any
file-based rule format, which makes the mistake trivially easy to trigger.

### Where the rules live

Two mechanisms, split the way git splits `.gitignore` from `.git/info/exclude`. Location does not
change *what* the rules govern — always the working folder — only whether the rules themselves
travel between peers, since everything under the control dir is skipped by the walk
(walk.ts:26).

| | Home | Travels? | Format | Written by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Shared | `.vfsignore`, working folder | Yes — it is ordinary synced content | Plain text, `#` comments | Hand or app |
| Local | `ignore?: string[]` in `.vfs/config.json` | No | JSON array | Engine only, via API |

The local half goes into `config.json` rather than a separate `.vfs/ignore`: `NodeConfig` already
evolves by optional field (`storeId?` — "Absent only in stores written before it existed"), and one
less file in the control dir is one less round trip per `open()`, which on Drive is what walking
costs. The tradeoff is that `config.json` is engine-written on every commit and sync, so hand
editing races with it — acceptable precisely because these rules are set through the API, not by
hand. A separate file would only earn its place if hand-editing were a supported workflow.

### What Musikmatch actually needs

**Only the local half, at first.** Every peer runs Musikmatch and ships the same default rules for
OS junk (`.DS_Store`, `Thumbs.db`, `._*`, `@eaDir`, `.Trashes`), so the app binary already
guarantees the symmetry that a shared file would otherwise provide. `.vfsignore` is the
general-case feature — heterogeneous peers, user-authored rules — and belongs in vfs-sync without
Musikmatch depending on it on day one.

Residual risk: version skew. If one peer ships a rule another lacks, the docs' asymmetry hazard
applies ("the ignoring peer receives the file but never reports it"). Confined to junk files, so
low stakes, and the tombstone fix keeps it from turning into data loss.

`.meta/` must never appear in any rule set — it is the metadata plane and has to sync in full.

### Not to be confused with selective materialisation

Opposite mechanisms. Ignoring makes a file invisible to the tree; materialisation (§5 item 2)
keeps it in the tree without holding its bytes. "Sync `.meta` to the phone but not the audio" is
materialisation — ignoring would leave the phone with a tree in which the tracks do not exist, and
therefore nothing to display.

### Bootstrap ordering

A shared `.vfsignore` is itself content, so the sync that delivers new rules still runs under the
old ones. It converges on the second pass, which `syncUntilStable` already provides.

---

## Extension — 2026-08-11 21:52 — Ignore: both mechanisms adopted

Supersedes "What Musikmatch actually needs" above, which proposed deferring the shared half.

`.vfs/ignore` is dropped — it was only ever shorthand for the idea. The arrangement is:

- **`.vfsignore`**, working folder, synced, plain text with `#` comments.
- **`ignore?: string[]`** in `.vfs/config.json`, local, set through the API.

Both are adopted from the start, not just the local one.

### Composition

The engine reads both at `open()` and composes them into the single predicate the walk already
takes. **Union**: a path ignored by either is ignored, exactly as git applies `.gitignore` and
`info/exclude` together. No precedence to define, because there is nothing to break a tie between.

That holds only while there is no negation. If `!pattern` un-ignore rules are ever added, order
becomes observable and has to be specified — last match wins within a file, and one file has to be
declared to outrank the other. Recommendation: ship without negation and keep union.

### Pattern syntax

Full gitignore semantics (leading `/`, trailing `/`, `**`, `!`, nested files that re-scope) is a
large surface for what this needs. Minimal subset that covers every real case:

| Form | Matches | Example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| exact path | that path | `docs/notes.md` |
| directory prefix | the subtree | `.cache/` |
| `*` glob on the basename | any directory | `*.tmp`, `.DS_Store` |

Any of the three matched anywhere in the tree, since OS junk appears at every level — `.DS_Store`
must match in all folders, not only the root.

### `.vfsignore` is not self-ignorable

It is ordinary synced content and the mesh needs it to converge, so the engine must refuse to let a
rule exclude it — the same way git always tracks `.gitignore`. Otherwise one peer can drop the
rule set out of the tree and the others read it as a delete.

## Extension — 2026-10-28 00:15 — M0 re-scoped against vfs-sync 0.2.0

Verifies the sync engine as it stands today and re-scopes M0 accordingly. Several things the
milestones set out to build already ship, and the seam M0 has to adopt is the engine's own
`VFSAdapter` — not the `StorageBackend` sketch in §3.

### What was verified

`@cloudauthn/vfs-sync@0.2.0`: present on the npm registry under that exact name (checked against the
registry, not assumed), source readable at `../vfs-sync`, changelog at `CHANGELOG.md`.

Three statements in this document are now historical, all in the direction of less work:

| Written here | Reality as of 0.2.0 |
| --- | --- |
| §5 item 2 — selective materialisation to be contributed at M3 | **Shipped.** `materialize`/`dematerialize`, policy passed to `VFSNode.open`, entries travel without their bytes |
| Extension 21:40 — "ignoring a tracked file deletes it across the mesh" | **Fixed**, and named as the headline of the release: a path counts as a delete only when this node saw it on disk *and* no current rule excludes it |
| Extension 21:52 — `.vfsignore` plus local rules, union, three forms, no negation, never self-ignorable | **Implemented exactly as specified**, in `ignore.ts` and `vfs-node.ts`, with the conformance tests |

Still open upstream: GC of unreferenced content (the remaining §5 contribution), and §5 item 1 — the
churn of rewriting a whole audio file to change a few tag bytes, which D9 already demotes from
blocking to merely desirable.

Consequence for §5's premise: of the two gaps that justified D9, one is upstream's problem now and
the other is the one D9 addresses. The decision stands; its justification narrows.

### The seam M0 adopts

`VFSAdapter` in 0.2.0 is **path-based**, not operations-based:

```
list / read / write / delete / rename / stat                    required
mkdir / fileId / readRange / readStream / writeStream / append  optional
changes                                                          optional
```

That is the opposite shape from §3's `StorageBackend` (opaque `FileId`, `hash` and `read_tags` as
operations). The two answer different questions rather than conflicting:

- **vfs-sync's `VFSAdapter` is the transport seam.** It exists, is tested, and is what the sync engine
  consumes. Every backend the plan wants already implements it — FSA, OPFS, Drive, node — and
  `FSAAdapter` ships `pick`, `fromHandle`, `hasPermission` and `ensurePermission`, which is precisely
  the surface `Mp3ManagerView` uses and `VfsAdapter` never declared.
- **`StorageBackend` remains the Rust-side seam** for M2, where a whole-file round trip across HTTP
  genuinely is the cost to avoid. It is not what the TypeScript UI talks to.

Why adopt the engine's interface at M0 instead of inventing a local one now and replacing it at M3:
one seam, validated by the code that will actually use it, and nothing written to be thrown away. It
also pulls the dependency in earlier than M3 — the milestone that read "integrate vfs-sync" becomes
"wire the mesh", which is the smaller of the two.

### Revised M0

1. `npm install @cloudauthn/vfs-sync@0.2.0`.
2. Delete `src/vfs/types.ts` and `src/vfs/browserVfs.ts`. The library replaces both; nothing in them
   survives.
3. `RootWorkspace.vfs` is retyped from `BrowserVfs` to `VFSAdapter`, and gains `rootHandle` for the
   two things that are session concerns rather than storage ones: re-authorising permission on reload
   and `saveRootSession`. The handle stops being reachable through a getter on the storage object.
4. Call-site map, all inside `Mp3ManagerView.tsx`:

| Today | After |
| --- | --- |
| `new BrowserVfs()` + `mount()` | `FSAAdapter.pick()` |
| `vfs.mountFromHandle(handle)` | `FSAAdapter.fromHandle(handle)` |
| `vfs.hasReadWritePermission()` | `adapter.ensurePermission()` |
| `vfs.getRootName()` | `adapter.name` |
| `vfs.getRootHandle()` | `root.rootHandle` (workspace field) |
| `vfs.listFiles({ extensions: ['.mp3'] })` | `walk(adapter)` + `.mp3` filter; name derived from the path, size and mtime from `WalkedFile.stat` |
| `vfs.readFile(id)` | `adapter.read(relativePath)` |
| `vfs.writeFile(id, bytes)` | `adapter.write(relativePath, bytes)` |
| `vfs.readTextFile(p)` / `writeTextFile(p, text)` | `stat` then `read` / `write`, with `TextDecoder` and `TextEncoder` |
| `vfs.getWriteOperations()` | dropped — it fed a single informational sentence, and the engine keeps its own log |

5. Drop `Mp3Track.vfsFileId`. Its only reason to exist was `${path}#${mtime}#${size}`, an id that
   changes exactly when the file does — audit item 7. On a path-based adapter the path *is* the key,
   and phase 2 no longer has to patch ids after the walk. M1 replaces the path key with `track_id`;
   this step is what turns that substitution into a change of one field instead of two.
6. `META_FILE_PATH` stays `.meta/mp3-manager.json`; the layout change belongs to M1. Note for M1:
   `walk()` skips only `.vfs`, so `.meta` is walked today and stays out of the results only because
   the extension filter rejects it. That is luck, not design — `.meta` must be excluded by a rule.

Out of scope, unchanged: `.meta` v2, the Rust crates, playback, packaging.

Expected diff: two files deleted, one view re-typed, one dependency added. `npm run build` is the
acceptance test — this repo has no test suite yet, and M0 does not add one.

### Risk

Low and reversible. Every call preserves its behaviour one-for-one except the write-operation counter
in one status message. The one thing to check is `walk()`'s per-file `stat` on FSA: it calls
`getFile()` once per entry, which is what `BrowserVfs.walk` already did, so the walk costs the same.

---

## Walkthrough — 2026-10-28 00:40 — M0 done

The re-scoped M0 landed as planned. `npm run build` (`tsc --noEmit && vite build`) passes.

### What changed

- `@cloudauthn/vfs-sync@^0.2.0` added to dependencies.
- `src/vfs/types.ts` and `src/vfs/browserVfs.ts` deleted (204 lines). The interface that described
  itself and the class that was the only implementation are both gone; the seam is now the library's
  `VFSAdapter`.
- `src/types.ts`: `Mp3Track.vfsFileId` removed.
- `src/views/Mp3ManagerView.tsx`: rewired per the call-site map above. `RootWorkspace` now carries
  `vfs: VFSAdapter` plus `rootHandle`, and `hydrateRoot` takes the handle as a required option rather
  than reading it off the storage object.

### Deliberate deviations, all small

1. The write-operation counter is gone from the batch-edit status message: the sentence now reads
   `Metadatos guardados en X.` The engine keeps its own log; the app had no use for a second one.
2. The Spanish "no FSA in this browser" message is preserved by guarding with `isFSAAvailable()`
   before `FSAAdapter.pick()`, which would otherwise throw the library's English text.
3. `src/global.d.ts` is untouched, but its `Window.showDirectoryPicker` declaration is now unused —
   the library declares its own. No conflict today (the build proves it), worth deleting in a later
   pass rather than widening this diff.

### Verification

`npm run build` passes. There is still no test suite in this repo, so the runtime assumptions M0
rests on were checked directly against the node backend of the same library, in
`.tmp/scripts/m0-smoke.mjs` (temporary, git-ignored). All nine checks pass, and two of them are worth
recording because they are load-bearing:

- `stat.mtime` is milliseconds (`file.lastModified`), matching what `lastModified` held before
  `vfsFileId` existed — so the "unchanged file" fast path still works.
- `write('.meta/…')` creates the missing parent folder, and `stat()` of an absent `.meta` is `null`.
  That is what keeps `readMetaSnapshot` returning an empty snapshot on a fresh folder instead of
  throwing.

One finding, repeated from the plan because the smoke test proved it: **`.meta/mp3-manager.json` is
returned by `walk()`** — only the `.mp3` filter keeps it out of the track list. Excluding `.meta` must
become an explicit rule in M1, not an accident of file extensions.

### Follow-ups this creates

- M1: `.meta` excluded by rule; path key replaced by `track_id` (one field now, not two).
- M2: the Rust `StorageBackend` trait keeps §3's operations shape. The TypeScript side is settled and
  no longer needs it.
- M3: becomes "wire the mesh" — `VFSNode.open` over an `FSAAdapter`, `sync()` between roots — rather
  than a migration.

Suggested commit message:

```
refactor: adopt vfs-sync's VFSAdapter as the storage seam (M0)
```

## Extension — 2026-11-04 12:44 — Re-plan: the Rust tag engine (lofty, in wasm) first, then Tauri v2 desktop

**Summary**: The owner chose, on 2026-11-04, to go ahead with two of this record's decisions now:
one tag engine everywhere, `lofty` compiled to wasm for the browser (D5), and Tauri v2 for the
native app (M5). The milestones are re-planned around them: the engine first, then the desktop app;
the axum daemon, the SQLite index and the sync mesh are deferred. The roadmap is
[ROADMAP/2026-11-04/rust-tag-engine-and-tauri.md](../ROADMAP/2026-11-04/rust-tag-engine-and-tauri.md).
The work starts in a new session and branch, once the current branch is merged.

How it was decided, in `RECORD/2026-11-04.github-actions-workflows.WIP.md` (12:20, 12:29): the
owner asked whether WebAssembly would speed up tag reading and writing (measured: no; native
WebCrypto hashing beats WebAssembly, and the slow parts are how `jsmediatags` and `id3.ts` do the
work), what risk FLAC writing carries, and, with a native app in mind, what to move to WebAssembly
and in which language, and whether to port to Flutter. The answers: the code that must give the
same bytes on every platform (the tag engine above all, the audio region the checksums cover, the
`.meta` format), in Rust, with `lofty`; and Tauri v2 rather than Flutter, since it keeps the one
jq79 interface on desktop and mobile while the core runs natively. User message (translated):
*"OK, let's go with your recommendations of lofty and Tauri v2. Write the ROADMAP and RECORD, and
summarise the plan for me here."*

### Where the project stands (what changed since this record's milestones)

- M0 is done (2026-10-28). The interface is no longer React: it is jq79 views over a Deck theme,
  with `src/helpers/` (pure, tested), `src/lib/` (stores and services) and a layer check; `npm test`
  runs layers, styles, types, unit tests and a 90% coverage floor.
- Tags: read with `jsmediatags`, written with `browser-id3-writer`, whose loss of unedited frames is
  repaired by `src/helpers/id3v2.ts` (2026-11-04, `keepUneditedFrames`). MP3 only.
- `.meta/` is still v1 (`.meta/mp3-manager.json`), SHA-1 checksums, covers inside as data URLs
  (audit item 1 of this record, still open). Writers are serialised by `flushMeta`, and the scan's
  race with a save is fixed (audit item 3).
- Storage goes through vfs-sync's `VFSAdapter` (`FSAAdapter`); no mesh yet.
- Playback: one `<audio>` element, routed through a Web Audio analyser for the display.

### Decisions

| # | Decision | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- |
| E-D1 | One Rust crate, `crates/tags`, over `lofty`: read and write tags over bytes, and the audio region of each format. No I/O. | D5 of this record: the same bytes on every platform, or checksums and sync diverge |
| E-D2 | The web loads it as wasm (`crates/tags-wasm`, wasm-bindgen), lazily: only when a file has to be read or written, never for a library that reopens from `.meta/` | the first load stays as fast as today (measured at ~1s on a phone-like profile) |
| E-D3 | The write safeguards live in the crate, for every platform: after writing, parse again; the audio region must be byte-identical (same hash); otherwise refuse and say why | the FLAC risk (2026-11-04 12:20): a writer's bug changes the person's only copy of their files |
| E-D4 | One TypeScript port, `src/lib/tags/`, with a wasm implementation (web) and a Tauri one (native, on paths); the views do not know which runs | the UI stays one, as M2 already wanted for its `HttpBackend` |
| E-D5 | Checksums keep their definitions (SHA-1, the same audio region) until `.meta` v2; in the browser the hash stays WebCrypto, which is faster than wasm (15 ms against 51 ms for 13 MB) | no migration of anyone's `.meta/` in this plan; D7 (SHA-256) moves to `.meta` v2 |
| E-D6 | Tauri v2 desktop reuses the jq79 build as it is; the native side implements vfs-sync's `VFSAdapter` over Tauri commands and runs tags and hashing on paths, in process | WebKit webviews (macOS, Linux) have no File System Access API; shipping whole files over the IPC to hash or tag them is the cost §3 already rejected |
| E-D7 | No daemon and no SQLite index in this plan | with Tauri the core runs in process; M2's daemon and index answer the multi-source mesh, which stays deferred |
| E-D8 | Native audio output (exclusive / bit-perfect) is a separate, optional step after the desktop release; `<audio>` stays the default and the fallback | browsers cannot do bit-perfect output (answered 2026-11-04); it is the one thing the native app adds for sound |
| E-D9 | Mobile starts with a spike on file access, and is Player-first (D8 of this record) | Android SAF and iOS security-scoped bookmarks decide what is possible |

### Repository layout (proposed)

```
crates/
  tags/          lofty: read, write, audio region, safeguards. No I/O. cargo test on fixtures/.
  tags-wasm/     wasm-bindgen bindings; built into a package the web app imports.
src-tauri/       Tauri v2: commands over the filesystem (list, read, write, stat, rename,
                 delete, pick a folder) and tags / hashes on paths, calling crates/tags natively.
src/lib/tags/    the port: engine.ts (the interface), wasm.ts, tauri.ts.
src/lib/platform.ts  which runtime this is (browser or Tauri).
fixtures/        conformance files and their expected results, for cargo test and npm test.
```

The wasm package is imported as a package (an npm workspace or a `file:` dependency), so the layer
check treats it as one and `src/lib/` may import it. The Makefile builds it before `up`, `test` and
`build`; Rust (stable, with the `wasm32-unknown-unknown` target) becomes a development requirement.

### Steps

**E0 — Spike (decides the rest).**
1. Build `lofty` for `wasm32-unknown-unknown` with wasm-bindgen: binary size raw and gzipped, the
   time to load it, and read / write times on the 2026-11-04 benchmark file (13 MB MP3, 1.27 MB
   cover) against today's engine.
2. Check `lofty`'s write semantics against what jackbird needs: unedited ID3 frames kept (the cases
   `src/helpers/id3v2.test.ts` covers), unedited Vorbis comments and FLAC blocks kept, padding used,
   v2.3 vs v2.4 kept.
3. A Tauri v2 shell that loads today's `dist/` on macOS, Windows and Linux: jq79 runs (safeEval),
   `<audio>` plays MP3 and FLAC from disk through the asset protocol, the Web Audio analyser works
   on it.
4. A go / no-go in this record, with the numbers. If `lofty` drops data, the options are an upstream
   contribution or keeping a merge step like `keepUneditedFrames` in the crate; if the wasm is too
   large, cargo features that build only the formats used.

**E1 — `crates/tags`.** `read(bytes) -> Tags`, `write(bytes, &Tags) -> bytes`,
`audio_region(bytes) -> Range` per format (MP3: after ID3v2, before ID3v1 / APE, as
`getAudioFrameSlice` today; FLAC: after the last metadata block), and E-D3's safeguards.
`fixtures/`: small files made with `ffmpeg` and `browser-id3-writer`, plus their expected tags and
regions as JSON. Done when `cargo test` passes on them, with every case of the TypeScript tests.

**E2 — The engine in the web app.** `crates/tags-wasm`; `src/lib/tags/engine.ts` (the interface:
read the tags and the audio region of a file's bytes, write tags into them) and `wasm.ts`, loaded
on first use; `readTrackMetadataFromBytes` and `writeTrackMetadataToBytes` move onto it;
`jsmediatags`, `browser-id3-writer` and `src/helpers/id3v2.ts` go. Covers come back as bytes and are
turned into URLs natively (2 ms, against 52 ms one character at a time). Done when `npm test` runs the
fixtures against the wasm build, a library saved before reopens without re-reading any file (the
checksums match), and the first load is no slower.

**E3 — FLAC.** The walk takes `.flac`; the player's MIME type by extension; `Mp3Track` becomes
`Track` (D2); texts that say MP3 say audio. Done when a FLAC library opens, plays, edits and saves,
and every saved file decodes with `ffmpeg` and keeps its unedited comments and blocks.

**E4 — Tauri v2 desktop.** `src-tauri/`: the folder dialog; a `TauriAdapter implements VFSAdapter`
over commands, so the rest of the app is unchanged; `src/lib/tags/tauri.ts` calling commands that
read, hash and write on paths; the session keeps paths instead of directory handles; playback from
disk through the asset protocol (no file read into memory); GitHub Actions builds for macOS,
Windows and Linux beside the web build. Signing and notarising need the owner's certificates; the
first builds are unsigned. Done when the desktop app opens a folder, plays, edits and reopens it
after a restart on the three systems.

**E5 — Native output (optional).** Decoding (symphonia) and output (cpal) in Rust, with exclusive
or hog modes where the system offers them, the analyser fed from Rust, `<audio>` as the fallback.

**E6 — Mobile.** The file-access spike first; then a Player-first app, as D8 says.

### Deferred, each a separate decision

`.meta` v2 (this record's M1: covers out of the snapshot, per-track sidecars, SHA-256), the axum
daemon and the SQLite index (M2's server half), the vfs-sync mesh (M3), the rename to Musikmatch
(§7), scanning in a Web Worker (2026-11-04 12:20, proposal 3).

### Risks

- **The wasm's size and load time** (E0 measures them): lazy loading keeps them off the first load
  of a library that reopens from `.meta/`; cargo features can drop the formats not used.
- **`lofty`'s write semantics** may not match the "keep what is not edited" rule in every case
  (E0 checks them).
- **WebKit webviews** (macOS, Linux): no File System Access API (E-D6 answers it), and differences in
  `<audio>` codecs (Linux plays through GStreamer, so FLAC needs its plugins) and in Web Audio with
  the asset protocol (E0 checks them).
- **Signing**: macOS notarisation and Windows code signing need paid certificates; until then the
  builds warn when opened.
- **Toolchain**: Rust in development and in CI; build times grow.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 18:10 — E0, points 1 and 2: how they are measured (plan)

**Summary**: The owner approved E0's first two points (lofty in wasm: size, load and speed; lofty's
write semantics). Point 3 (the Tauri shell) waits: this container has no `webkit2gtk`, and macOS and
Windows need CI or the owner's machines. This is how the two points are measured; the results
follow in the next extension, with a go / no-go for them.

User message (translated): *"Go ahead with 1 and 2."*

The spike lives in `.tmp/e0/` (ignored by git, as AGENTS.md asks for temporary work); the record
keeps what it found and enough of how to repeat it. Nothing in `src/` changes.

### 1. lofty in wasm

- A `cdylib` crate over the latest `lofty` with `wasm-bindgen`: `read(bytes)` gives the six fields
  jackbird edits (title, artist, album, year, genre, track) and the first front cover as bytes with
  its MIME type; `write(bytes, fields, cover?)` gives the new file. Built for
  `wasm32-unknown-unknown` in release with `opt-level = "z"`, LTO and one codegen unit, then
  `wasm-bindgen --target web`.
- **Size**: the `.wasm` raw, gzipped (`-9`) and brotli'd, beside today's engine (`jsmediatags` and
  `browser-id3-writer` as the build bundles them, minified and gzipped); and what cargo features can
  take out.
- **Load**: compiling and instantiating the module in Chromium (`WebAssembly.compile` of the bytes,
  so the network is out of it), first time and again.
- **Speed**: the 2026-11-04 benchmark file made again (5 min at 320 kbps, some 13 MB, with a JPEG
  cover of some 1.27 MB, tagged by today's engine so both engines start from the same bytes). In
  Chromium (Playwright), on the dev server, medians of 9 runs after one discarded: today's
  `readTrackMetadataFromBytes` and `writeTrackMetadataToBytes` from `src/lib/id3.ts`, against the
  wasm `read` (with the cover turned into an object URL, as E2 plans) and `write`, copies into the
  wasm memory included. The same crate natively (release) for reference: that is the Tauri path.

### 2. lofty's write semantics

Checked natively with `cargo test`-style checks in the spike (the wasm build runs the same code; one
case is repeated through the wasm to confirm), on files made with `ffmpeg` and tags built byte by
byte, through both of lofty's ways of editing: its generic `Tag` and the concrete `Id3v2Tag` /
`VorbisComments`.

- **MP3**: a tag with `TIT2 TPE1 TALB TYER TCON TRCK` beside `TPOS TPE2 COMM TXXX` (ReplayGain)
  `USLT UFID PRIV`, two `APIC` (front, back) and a non-standard frame; a title edit must keep every
  other frame byte for byte and in order. Also: the version kept (v2.3 in, v2.3 out; v2.4 in, v2.4
  out), the year frame for each version, the padding (a small edit into a tag with room leaves the
  audio where it was), a frame flagged "discard when altered", a v2.3 tag with unsynchronisation, a
  v2.4 tag with plain frame sizes, an ID3v1 tag at the end, and the audio region byte-identical
  (the same SHA-1 of `getAudioFrameSlice`'s region).
- **FLAC**: STREAMINFO, SEEKTABLE, APPLICATION, CUESHEET, a VORBIS_COMMENT with ALBUMARTIST,
  DISCNUMBER, REPLAYGAIN_*, MUSICBRAINZ_*, LYRICS, a repeated ARTIST and a lower-case key, two
  PICTURE blocks and PADDING; a title edit must keep every other block and comment, use the padding
  when it fits, leave the audio frames byte-identical, and decode with `ffmpeg` without errors.

### Go / no-go, as proposed

- **Go** if some way of editing in lofty keeps everything above, or a small merge step in the crate
  (as `keepUneditedFrames` does today) can make it so; and the gzipped wasm, loaded lazily, is no
  larger than a few hundred KB, with read and write no slower than today.
- **No-go**, or a changed plan, if lofty loses data that no merge step can restore, or the wasm is
  far larger than that with no feature to trim it.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 18:30 — E0, points 1 and 2: results, and a go with a merge layer

**Summary**: Measured as the 18:10 plan says. lofty in wasm reads some 15× and saves some 5× faster
than today's engine; MP3 and FLAC alone weigh 145 KB gzipped, against 17.5 KB today. Used as its
documentation leads (the generic `Tag`), lofty is not fit for jackbird; its concrete tags keep
unknown frames byte for byte but lose data in five cases; a merge layer of some 250 lines on top
of them kept everything in every case. Proposal: **go**, with that layer as part of E1. Point 3
(Tauri) is still open.

### How it was measured (to repeat it)

- `.tmp/e0/spike/` (not committed): a crate over `lofty` `=0.25.5` and `wasm-bindgen` `=0.2.130`,
  Rust 1.97; `.tmp/e0/slim/`, the same without lofty's `Probe`; `wasm-bindgen` CLI 0.2.130 and
  binaryen 124's `wasm-opt`. Fixtures made with `ffmpeg`, tags and FLAC blocks built byte by byte.
  `mutagen` 1.48.1 and `ffmpeg` as independent checks. Scripts in `.tmp/scripts/`
  (`bench-chromium.cjs`, `wasm-equiv.mjs`, `validate.py`, `make-bench.mjs`).
- Chromium (Playwright) on the dev server, in this container (4 vCPU Xeon, 2.1 GHz); medians of 9
  runs after one discarded, two runs of the whole benchmark.
- The benchmark file: 5 min of pink noise at 320 kbps (12.0 MB) and a 1.38 MB JPEG cover, tagged
  by `browser-id3-writer` with the frames `src/lib/id3.ts` writes: 13.38 MB.

### 1. Size and loading

| Build | Raw | gzip -9 | brotli -11 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Today: `jsmediatags` + `browser-id3-writer`, bundled and minified (esbuild) | 63.7 KB | 17.5 KB | 15.1 KB |
| lofty wasm through `Probe` (every format lofty knows), `opt-level = "z"`, LTO, `wasm-opt -Oz` | 933.7 KB | 308.7 KB | 221.5 KB |
| The same, MP3 and FLAC only (the format told from the first bytes, no `Probe`) | 430.5 KB | 144.8 KB | 118.7 KB |
| wasm-bindgen's JavaScript glue | 16.1 KB | 3.1 KB | 2.8 KB |

- lofty has no cargo feature per format, and 0.25.5 does not build with its default features off
  (a missing import when `id3v2_compression_support` is off), so `flate2` stays in. What trims it is
  not calling `Probe`: the linker then leaves out MP4, APE, WavPack, Ogg, AIFF, WAV, Musepack and
  AAC.
- `opt-level = 3` gives a 25% larger module and no speed (the work is copying memory), so the size
  build it is.
- Loading, network aside: compiling 3.4–4.6 ms (medians of 7 fresh pages; 3.2–10.3 ms each),
  instantiating 0.5–0.6 ms.

### 1. Speed

| Step, on the 13.38 MB file | Today (`src/lib/id3.ts`) | lofty, wasm | lofty, native |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Read the six fields and the cover (today a data URL; wasm an object URL) | 153–188 ms | 9–11 ms | 0.4 ms |
| Read the six fields only | — | 1.6–1.8 ms | — |
| Save a title edit, the cover written again (as the app does today) | 120–124 ms | 18–24 ms | 20 ms |
| Save a title edit, the cover untouched | — | 19–23 ms | 19 ms |

- The save times are the merged writer's (below), with its checks: a copy of the source, the audio
  compared, the result parsed again. lofty's concrete tags alone save in 10–12 ms in wasm.
- Every time includes copying the bytes into and out of the module's memory.
- **wasm and native give the same bytes**: the merged writer through the wasm reproduced the native
  output byte for byte in all 20 fixture saves.
- **Memory**: the module's memory grew from 1.6 to 41.1 MB reading and saving the 12.8 MiB file
  (some 3.2× the file), and stays there: wasm memory never shrinks. Further saves did not grow it.

### 2. Write semantics

Fixtures (3 s of `ffmpeg` audio, real JPEGs as covers):

- **MP3**, six cases: v2.3 with 2 KB of padding; v2.4 with padding; v2.3 unsynchronised; v2.4 with
  plain frame sizes (as old iTunes wrote them); v2.3 with an ID3v1 tag at the end; no tag. Each tag
  has 18 frames: `TIT2 TPE1 TALB TYER|TDRC TCON TRCK`, then `TPOS TPE2 COMM TXXX` (ReplayGain)
  `USLT UFID` (MusicBrainz) `PRIV`, two `APIC` (front, back), a non-standard `XQZW`, `TCMP`, and a
  `TPE4` flagged "discard when the tag is altered".
- **FLAC**, four cases: STREAMINFO, SEEKTABLE, APPLICATION, VORBIS_COMMENT (14 comments: two
  ARTIST, a lower-case `replaygain_album_gain`, ALBUMARTIST, DISCNUMBER, REPLAYGAIN_TRACK_GAIN,
  MUSICBRAINZ_TRACKID, LYRICS, COMMENT…), CUESHEET, two PICTURE, 4 KB of PADDING last; the padding
  first; no padding; an ID3v2 tag before `fLaC`.
- Two edits each: the title; the title and a new front cover. Three writers: lofty's generic `Tag`;
  its concrete tags (`Id3v2Tag`, `VorbisComments`) through their own setters; and "merged", the
  concrete tags with the steps listed under 3.
- All 72 files written were checked apart from the spike: `mutagen` parses each, `ffmpeg` decodes
  each without an error, and the decoded audio's MD5 equals the source's. All pass: no writer broke
  a file or changed its audio. The differences are in what each keeps.

**1. The generic `Tag`: unfit.** In v2.3 every text frame comes back re-encoded (ISO-8859-1 to
UTF-16, twice the size), picture descriptions too, frame flags are dropped, and the frames come out
in a different order on every run (a hash map), so the same edit gives different bytes each time:
checksums would differ between runs and platforms. In FLAC, lower-case keys are upper-cased, the
vendor string is added as an `ENCODER=` comment, and picture blocks are re-encoded.

**2. The concrete tags: unknown and unedited frames kept byte for byte**, in v2.3, v2.4,
unsynchronised tags and beside an ID3v1 tag; in FLAC, STREAMINFO, SEEKTABLE, APPLICATION, CUESHEET
and PICTURE blocks kept byte for byte, and the comments kept. The version is kept when the caller
asks for it (`use_id3v23` is a write option; the crate sets it from the tag it read). But:

- a. **v2.4 with plain frame sizes: silent loss.** lofty reads the sizes as syncsafe, cuts the
  front cover's frame from 2,165 to 1,141 bytes, and loses every frame after it (the back cover,
  `XQZW`, `TCMP`, `TPE4`); a save writes that. In strict mode lofty refuses the file instead. Today's
  `readId3v2` reads such tags (it tries plain sizes when syncsafe ones do not end cleanly).
- b. **A frame lofty cannot validate is dropped on writing, with only a log line**: a TDRC set as
  text (lofty holds dates as timestamps) vanished from v2.3 saves. jackbird's year is free text, and
  one that does not parse as a date ("c. 1959") cannot go into TDRC through lofty.
- c. lofty's own `set_date` on a v2.4 tag that has a TDRC adds a second one.
- d. **The padding is never reused**: an ID3v2 tag always gets a fresh 1,024 bytes; in FLAC the old
  PADDING stays where it was and the comments and pictures are written after it (lofty's issue
  #445). So the audio moves on every save: no cost in the browser, which writes the whole file
  anyway, but the native app could otherwise rewrite only the tag, in place.
- e. A FLAC file with an ID3v2 tag before `fLaC` cannot be saved ("failed to write Flac file").
- f. Writing the six fields on every save, as jackbird does, replaces every value of a key: the
  second ARTIST goes.

**3. Merged: everything kept, in every case.** 0 frames, comments or blocks lost or changed, the
order kept, in all 20 saves. What it adds to the concrete tags:

- Only the fields that changed are written, and in place: the first value of a key changes, the
  others stay.
- The year goes in as a timestamp frame; a year lofty cannot hold is refused with a message, not
  dropped.
- A v2.4 tag with plain frame sizes is normalised (sizes rewritten as syncsafe) before lofty reads
  it.
- The tag is padded back to its old length when the new one fits, and a FLAC's padding is moved
  last and resized, so the audio stays where it was: in 5 of the 12 MP3 saves (every title edit of
  a tagged file; the new cover, 5.4 KB, did not fit in the old one's 2.2 KB plus 2 KB of padding)
  and 6 of the 8 FLAC saves (all but the file with no padding).
- A FLAC's leading ID3v2 tag is left as it is instead of making the save fail.
- E-D3's check: the audio bytes must be identical and the result must parse in strict mode, or
  the save is refused.

Two smaller points for E1: new text in a v2.3 tag is written as UTF-16 (v2.3 has no UTF-8), so a
retitled frame grows a little; ISO-8859-1 when the text fits would keep it smaller. And lofty
keeps a frame flagged "discard when altered" (the standard asks it only of unknown frames), where
today's `keepUneditedFrames` drops it.

### Go / no-go

Against the conditions set at 18:10:

- **Some way of editing in lofty keeps everything, with a small merge step**: yes, the merged
  writer.
- **The gzipped wasm, loaded lazily, no more than a few hundred KB**: 145 KB without `Probe`.
- **Reading and saving no slower than today**: some 15× faster reading, 5× faster saving.

**Proposal: go**, with these changes to E1 and E2 (awaiting approval):

- `crates/tags` uses lofty's concrete tags only, never the generic `Tag` or `Probe`; it tells MP3
  from FLAC by their first bytes.
- The spike's merge steps become E1's code, each with tests on the fixtures above: changed fields
  only, in place; the year as a timestamp or a refusal; v2.4 plain sizes normalised; the padding
  reused (ID3v2 and FLAC); a FLAC's leading ID3v2 tag; the strict re-read and the audio check.
- lofty pinned to an exact version, with the fixtures run on every bump: the behaviours above may
  change between versions.
- E2: the module's memory freed after a batch of saves (dropping the instance and loading it again
  costs ~5 ms); covers out as bytes, shown through object URLs.
- Upstream, optional (the merge layer does not depend on it): report or contribute plain-size v2.4
  tags in lofty's default mode, the build without default features, FLAC padding (#445), and
  `set_date`'s second TDRC.
- Point 3, the Tauri shell, still to do: this container has no `webkit2gtk`, and macOS and Windows
  need CI or the owner's machines. Proposed: a workflow that builds the shell on the three systems,
  with the checks by hand (jq79, `<audio>` with MP3 and FLAC, the analyser) done by the owner.

The spike's code lives only in `.tmp/e0/` and goes when this container does; whether to keep it
(for instance as E1's starting point) is the owner's call.

Suggested commit message: `docs(record): E0 points 1 and 2, lofty in wasm measured and its writes checked; go, with a merge layer`.

**Summary**: CI's first run of `tauri-shell.yml`

and its unsigned bundles on Linux, macOS and Windows, and made the unattended check on each. jq79

the analyser sees them; Linux does not, as found here at 18:55. A second run adds the local HTTP
server to the check on the three systems, to see whether one way of serving files can do for all.

### Builds

| System | Runner | Build (cold) | Bundle |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Linux | Ubuntu 22.04, x64 | 4 min 12 s | `.deb` and `.AppImage` |
| macOS | macOS (latest), arm64 | 3 min 18 s | `.dmg` (4.4 MB zipped) |
| Windows | Windows (latest), x64 | 6 min 41 s | NSIS installer, 2.36 MiB |

Unsigned, as planned: macOS and Windows warn when they are opened. Rust 1.99.1 on the runners.

### The unattended check (MP3 and FLAC fixtures alike unless noted)

| | macOS (WKWebView) | Windows (WebView2, Edge 156) | Linux (WebKitGTK, 22.04 and 24.04) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| jq79 draws the app, no errors | yes | yes | yes |
| File System Access API | no | **yes** | no |
| Origin-private file system | yes | yes | no |
| Page origin | `tauri://localhost` | `http://tauri.localhost` | `tauri://localhost` |
| `fetch` from the asset protocol | 200 | 200 | 200 |
| `<audio>` from the asset protocol, `crossOrigin` | plays, analyser 235 / 237 | plays, analyser 233 / 236 | media error 4 |
| `<audio>` from the asset protocol, no `crossOrigin` | plays, analyser 235 / 237 | plays, **analyser 0** (cross-origin) | media error 4 |
| `<audio>` from a `blob:` URL | MP3 plays (235); **FLAC: media error 4** | plays, analyser 233 / 236 | plays, analyser 233 / 236 |
| `AudioContext` | running, 48 kHz | running, 44.1 kHz | running, 44.1 kHz |

Runners have no sound card either; the times played only say the files decoded.

### What it means

- **E-D6 stands**: the native `TauriAdapter` over paths, for all three. Windows' webview has the
  File System Access API (it is Chromium), so today's adapter could work there, but one adapter
  for all keeps the session on paths and the work out of the IPC.
- **Playback from disk**: the asset protocol, with `crossOrigin = "anonymous"`, works on macOS and
  Windows; on Windows it is cross-origin, so without `crossOrigin` the analyser is silent, as the
  player sets it today. On Linux it does not play at all. A `blob:` URL is no common fallback
  (FLAC fails on macOS) and reads files whole. So E4 needs a second way for Linux: the local HTTP
  server (18:55), or that server everywhere if it plays on macOS and Windows too (the second run
  checks it). Behind one function in `src/lib/` (a media URL for a path), the views do not know.
- Point 3 is otherwise met, unattended: the shell loads today's `dist/` unchanged on the three
  systems. What remains for the owner, by hand on real machines: open the bundles (past the
  unsigned warnings), choose files in the E0 check window, hear them, and see the bars move.

### The second run

`tauri-shell.yml` now starts `src-tauri/e0/http-server.py` (a folder on 127.0.0.1 with CORS and
byte ranges; `actions/setup-python` gives the same `python` on the three systems) and passes
`JACKBIRD_E0_HTTP`, so the check plays each fixture a fourth way.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 20:59 — Cloud sources (Google Drive, Box) and an optional account: proposal

**Summary**: The owner's idea for what comes after E4, laid out against what exists: the web and
the app, each without a backend or with an optional account (email and passkeys), and Google Drive
and Box as sources. It fits as two independent axes over one code base; the account is an optional
layer, not a condition for cloud sources. Three facts are assumed, not checked, and a spike (C0)
checks them first. The roadmap is `ROADMAP/2026-11-04/cloud-sources-and-accounts.md`. Nothing is
built yet.

User messages (translated): *"The idea would be: web without a backend, + Box.com, + GDrive? Web
with a backend: an account tied to an email (+ passkeys), + Box.com, + GDrive? App without a
backend, + Box.com, + GDrive? App with a backend: an account tied to an email (+ passkeys), +
Box.com, + GDrive? Does it fit you?"*, then *"OK, add it."* and *"Transactional emails are exempt
from GDPR, aren't they?"*

### Two axes, one code base

| | No account | Account (email + passkeys), opt-in |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Web** | local folders (FSA), Drive, Box | the same, plus what the account adds (below) |
| **App (Tauri)** | local folders (paths), Drive, Box | the same, plus what the account adds |

- Every source is a vfs-sync `VFSAdapter`, the seam this record already chose. vfs-sync 0.2.0 has a
  `GDriveAdapter` that runs in the browser with an OAuth token (Drive's REST API allows CORS). There
  is no Box adapter: one would be a content-agnostic contribution to vfs-sync, like the others in §5.
- The tag engine (E1) reads byte ranges, so reading a cloud file's tags does not download it whole
  (`readRange`).
- The version without an account stays complete: the account only adds conveniences. That keeps
  today's promise ("nothing is uploaded anywhere") for whoever wants it.

### Cloud sources without a backend: what has to be solved

1. **Playback.** `<audio>` cannot send an `Authorization` header. Web: a Service Worker answers
   requests for a private URL prefix by fetching from the provider with the token and passing the
   byte ranges through, so a track streams instead of being downloaded first. App: the same proxy
   in Rust, beside E4's local server on Linux (and its custom protocol on macOS and Windows).
2. **Tag writes.** Neither Drive nor Box can change part of a file: every save uploads the whole
   file again (10–100 MB). This makes D9 (canonical tags in `.meta/`, writing them into the audio as
   an explicit operation) a condition for cloud writes, so `.meta` v2 moves before C1's writes, or
   C1 starts read-only.
3. **Drive's scopes.** Reading the music folder a person already has needs a restricted scope
   (`drive.readonly` or `drive`), which for a public app means Google's verification with a
   third-party security assessment, renewed yearly. The alternative is `drive.file` with the Google
   Picker; to check: whether picking a folder grants access to everything in it.
4. **Sessions.** Google's model for browser-only apps (the Identity Services token client) gives no
   refresh token: access expires after an hour and is asked for again. To check for Box: whether
   its OAuth takes PKCE without a client secret; if not, Box in the web needs a backend for the code
   exchange. In the app both work with PKCE and a loopback redirect.

### What the optional account adds (a backend)

- Refresh tokens for Drive and Box, encrypted at rest, so connections last and follow the person
  across devices without asking again.
- Settings and the list of libraries across devices (the library itself already travels with its
  folder, in `.meta/`).
- A relay peer for the vfs-sync mesh (M3), for devices that are not on at the same time.
- Box's code exchange, if C0 finds that it needs a secret.
- **Sign-in**: passkeys (WebAuthn) and an emailed one-time code or link as the fallback. In the web
  they work, better on an own domain than on `github.io` (passkeys are bound to the domain for
  good). In the app's webviews support is uneven (WKWebView needs a signed app with associated
  domains; WebKitGTK has none), so the app signs in through the system browser and returns by a
  deep link, as desktop apps commonly do.
- Its cost: hosting, a database, an email provider, and a backend that holds tokens with access to
  a person's whole Drive: a security and privacy responsibility, hence opt-in.

### GDPR and transactional email (answering the owner)

Not legal advice. Transactional emails (sign-in codes, security notices) are not exempt from the
GDPR. What they are exempt from is the consent the ePrivacy rules (in Spain, LSSI art. 21) require
for commercial messages. The GDPR applies to any processing of personal data, and an email address
is personal data. For the account that means:

- A lawful basis: performing the contract (art. 6(1)(b)); no consent needed for the account's own
  emails. Newsletters or marketing would need consent apart.
- A privacy notice (art. 13): who, what, why, how long, which processors, the person's rights.
- A data processing agreement (art. 28) with the email provider and the host, and a legal basis
  for transfers outside the EU (the EU–US Data Privacy Framework or standard contractual clauses)
  if they are in the US.
- Minimal data (email, passkey public keys, encrypted tokens), retention limits, deletion of the
  account with its data on request, security proportionate to the risk (the tokens), and breach
  notification (72 h to the AEPD where it applies).

### Proposed steps (after E4)

| Step | Scope | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C0 Spike | Box's OAuth for a public client (PKCE without a secret?); Drive's `drive.file` with a folder from the Picker; a Service Worker range proxy playing a Drive file in Chromium; passkeys in the three webviews and the system-browser sign-in | the answers, with what each changes below, are in this record |
| C1 Drive, no backend | the `GDriveAdapter` as a source (web and app); the Picker or the restricted scope as C0 decides; playback through the proxies; read-only until D9 / `.meta` v2 allow writes | a Drive library opens, plays and shows its tags in the web and the app |
| C2 Box | a `BoxAdapter` contributed to vfs-sync; without a backend if C0 allows it | the same as C1, with Box |
| C3 Account | the backend: email and passkeys, encrypted refresh tokens, settings across devices; the app signing in through the system browser; the privacy notice and the processors' agreements | sign-in on web and app, a Drive connection surviving a new device |
| C4 Relay | the account's relay peer for the vfs-sync mesh (M3) | two devices, never on together, converge |

Open question for the owner: whether the account would be a service of their own (vfs-sync is
published by cloudauthn), which would change C3.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 21:12 — Three deployables; a jackbird folder per user on the backend: proposal; where things stand

**Summary**: The owner sees three developments (web, app, backend) over three kinds of source (the
local file system, Box, Google Drive), and a backend that adds a folder of its own per user. Three
deployables, but two shared code bases; the folder per user fits well and replaces C4. Its
decisions are open and listed. The extension ends with where everything stands, for the next
session (the owner continues in a new session and branch once this one is merged).

User messages (translated): *"So it would be three developments: web, app, backend, given that we
have several kinds of source: Web FS, Box.com, GDrive. I'm thinking the backend could add a local
folder per user."*, then *"Can I merge the current branch? Is the RECORD up to date? I'll continue in
another session and another branch."*

### Three deployables, two shared code bases

- **Shared**: the Rust core (`crates/tags`, and what follows it), built to wasm for the web and
  natively for the app and the backend; the jq79 interface and `src/lib/`, the same in the web and
  the app.
- **Web**: today's static build. **App**: a thin Tauri layer (the native adapter, playback from
  disk, E4's local server on Linux). **Backend**: the one new development; a server with no
  interface of its own (the account's screens live in the web and app).

| Source | Web | App | Backend |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Local folder | File System Access API (Chromium) | native paths | — |
| Google Drive | browser token, Service Worker proxy | PKCE, Rust proxy | server-side, refresh token |
| Box | if PKCE allows it, else through the backend | PKCE | server-side |
| **jackbird folder** (new) | HTTP to the backend | HTTP to the backend | its own disk or object storage |

### A jackbird folder per user (proposal, awaiting approval)

What it gives:

- **Cheap tag edits**: with `crates/tags` native on the server, a retitle sends a few bytes and the
  server writes the tag in place, reusing the padding (the merged writer). Drive and Box would
  upload the whole file again. This is the "operations interface" this record proposed for M2.
- **The relay for the vfs-sync mesh**: always on, holding the history. It replaces C4.
- **Server-to-server copies** from Drive or Box, without the person's device in between (§5,
  contribution 4).

Open decisions:

- **Storage and money**: libraries take 10–500 GB. Server disk or object storage (S3, R2, B2; some
  5–25 USD per TB a month, plus traffic); quotas; who pays.
- **Encryption**: at rest on the server (it can still edit tags and serve ranges) or end to end
  (the server only stores). Proposed: at rest, data hosted in the EU.
- **Legal** (not advice): a personal locker of the person's own files; as a host, the DSA's
  notice-and-action protects the service; sharing between users would change the risk. The GDPR
  points of 20:59 apply.
- **The promise**: "your library, right where it is" stays true without an account; the jackbird
  folder is one more option.
- **Protocol**: an HTTP API mirroring `VFSAdapter` plus server-side operations (read and write
  tags, hash) and ranged reads for playback, so the web and app use one `HttpAdapter`. WebDAV is the
  standard alternative (mountable by operating systems) but has no such operations.
- **In the roadmap**: C3b after the account (C3), absorbing C4; C1 and C2 unchanged. Not added to
  `ROADMAP/2026-11-04/cloud-sources-and-accounts.md` until approved.

### Where things stand (for the next session)

Done on branch `claude/adoring-gauss-2e9fqs`
([jgermade/jackbird#9](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/pull/9)):

- E0, points 1–3: the spike in `spikes/e0-lofty/` (`run-all.sh` repeats it), the Tauri shell in
  `src-tauri/`, `tauri-shell.yml` (two CI runs recorded at 19:20 and 19:41).
- The UI changes of `RECORD/2026-11-04.github-actions-workflows.WIP.md` (18:28, 18:50): the display's
  1px rim and even padding, the spectrum from 641px, the README's screenshots.

Approved, to do: E1 and E2 with the 18:30 changes (lofty's concrete tags only, no `Probe`, the merge
layer, lofty pinned, the wasm's memory freed after batches).

Awaiting the owner:

- The run by hand of the CI bundles on real macOS and Windows machines (19:41).
- Optionally, a re-run of the cancelled macOS job of run 41885825373 (the HTTP server on macOS).
- Approval of: E4's media URLs (19:41); the cloud sources and account plan (20:59); the jackbird
  folder per user (this extension); whether the account would be a service of the owner's own.

Next on the roadmap: E1, `crates/tags` (`ROADMAP/2026-11-04/rust-tag-engine-and-tauri.md`).

Note for the merge: a push to `main` that touches `src-tauri/` runs `tauri-shell.yml` (macOS
minutes included); the web build, the release and the Pages deploy do not change.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 21:20 — Correction: the jackbird folder does not replace C4

**Summary**: The owner corrects the 21:12 proposal: the jackbird folder per user is optional, on or
off for each user, so it cannot be the mesh's relay. C4 stays its own step and works for an account
without the folder. What the 21:12 extension says about the folder replacing or absorbing C4 is
superseded by this one; the rest of it stands.

User message (translated): *"2. It does not replace C4: the local folder could be turned on, or
not."*

- **The folder (C3b)**: an option of the account, off by default or chosen per user; when on, it is
  one more peer of the mesh, which the relay can use, never a condition for it.
- **The relay (C4)**, without the folder, still has to bridge devices that are never on together.
  Two ways, for C4's own plan to decide:
  - *Store and forward*: it keeps the commit graph, the trees, and the blobs in transit, each blob
    deleted once every peer has it, under a quota and an expiry.
  - *Metadata only*: it keeps `.meta/` and the commit graph; audio moves directly when two peers are
    on at once, or through a source they share (Drive, Box, the folder when it is on).
- **Roadmap, once approved**: C3b (the folder, optional) after C3; C4 after C3, independent of C3b.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 21:22 — A family instance: self-hosted, a few users, one shared catalogue (proposal)

**Summary**: The owner's aim for the backend: an instance of their own, for three or four people
(the owner and their family), sharing one catalogue. That makes the backend self-hosted and
single-tenant rather than a public service, which removes most of what 20:59 and 21:12 worried
about (billing, quotas, much of the legal side) and brings back this record's M2 (an axum server
with SQLite) in a simpler form. Awaiting approval; its decisions are listed.

User message (translated): *"My idea is to be able to have my own instance with 3 or 4 users, for me
and my family, sharing the catalogue."*

### The shape

- **One server binary** (Rust: axum, SQLite, `crates/tags`), shipped as a Docker image and as plain
  binaries, run on a NAS, a mini PC or a small VPS. It holds the **shared library**: a folder on its
  own disk, with `.meta/` as today.
- **It serves the web build itself**, so the web talks to it from the same origin (no CORS), and the
  Tauri app adds it as a source (an `HttpAdapter` over the API of 21:12).
- **Shared and personal**: the catalogue (files, tags, covers) is shared; each person has their own
  favourites, playlists, play history and settings, in SQLite. Each can still add their own local
  folders, Drive or Box in their own client, outside the shared catalogue.
- **Tag edits run on the server**, in place (the merged writer, the padding reused): a retitle sends
  a few bytes, and everyone sees it.
- **Playback** streams from the server with byte ranges, to the web, the app and, later, mobile.

### What becomes simpler

- **No billing, no quotas, no multi-tenancy**: the owner's disk is the limit.
- **Accounts**: an admin and invitations (a one-time link), passkeys to sign in; email becomes
  optional (no mail provider needed for three or four people).
- **GDPR** (not legal advice): an instance run by a person for their household falls under the
  household exemption (art. 2(2)(c)), so the obligations of 20:59 apply to a public service, not to
  this. **Copyright**: the family's own copies, for private use, not published.
- **The relay (C4)**: an always-on instance is a natural peer of the mesh; C4 stays independent of it
  (21:20).

### Decisions to take

- **Reaching it from outside home**: a private network (Tailscale or WireGuard; Tailscale also gives
  HTTPS certificates) or a domain with a tunnel (Cloudflare Tunnel) or a port with Let's Encrypt.
  Passkeys need HTTPS and a stable domain (their RP ID): choosing it is choosing the passkeys' home
  for good. Proposed: Tailscale first, the server off the public internet.
- **Roles**: who may edit tags, rename, delete (proposed: admin and editors edit; listeners only
  play), and a log of who changed what.
- **Concurrent edits**: two people editing the same album: the server is authoritative, each write
  carries the version it read (an etag, as vfs-sync's `writeIf`), and a stale one is refused and
  shown, not merged blindly.
- **Mobile before E6**: expose the OpenSubsonic API as well, so the family can use existing apps
  (Symfonium, DSub, Substreamer…) on their phones from the start; jackbird's own interface stays the
  place to organise and edit. Comparable self-hosted servers (Navidrome, Jellyfin) do this.
- **Backups**: the library folder and the SQLite database, which the server can snapshot on a
  schedule; the files themselves are the person's own backups' business.

### Where it goes

It reshapes C3 and C3b of `ROADMAP/2026-11-04/cloud-sources-and-accounts.md` into a "family
instance" step (the server, the shared catalogue, invitations and passkeys, roles, per-person data)
after E4 and before C1 (Drive), since it needs no cloud provider. The roadmap is not changed until
the owner approves.
