## Extension — 2026-11-04 21:32 — The family instance: the owner's decisions, backups (proposal), the roadmap

**Summary**: The owner answers 21:22's decisions: remote access through intergate (their own
self-hosted tunnel), four roles, songs stored on the server by their hashes with an optional
version of the metadata per person, and OpenSubsonic under the owner's own study. Backups are
proposed below. The roadmap gains `ROADMAP/2026-11-04/family-instance.md` (steps F0–F5, after E4),
`cloud-sources-and-accounts.md` is reshaped around it, and E0 is marked done in
`rust-tag-engine-and-tauri.md`.

User message (translated): *"For access from outside home, I might use
[intergate](https://jgermade.github.io/intergate/). Roles: listeners, editors, admins and owners.
The songs on the backend's disk could be stored by hashes, and each user could have, if they want,
their own version of the metadata. About OpenSubsonic, I'll look into it. 5. What do you propose? If
you update the ROADMAP, I'll merge."*

### Remote access: intergate

intergate (v0.0.24, read from its page) is a self-hosted tunnel: a gate on a public host the owner
controls (DNS for `gate.example.com` and `*.gate.example.com`) receives an SSH reverse tunnel from
the machine at home (`ssh -p 2222 -R jackbird:80:localhost:<port> gate.example.com`) and serves
`https://jackbird.gate.example.com` with a Let's Encrypt certificate made on demand. Subdomains are
opened only by the SSH keys added to them, and each can be public, team or private.

- That subdomain becomes the passkeys' RP ID, so it must not change. To check in F0: that a name
  stays the same across reconnects; that byte-range requests (streaming) and long responses pass
  through the tunnel; how the gate's own visibility (team, private) sits with jackbird's sign-in
  (proposed: the subdomain public at the gate, jackbird's passkeys in front of everything); that the
  home side reconnects by itself (the intergate client, or `autossh`).

### Roles

| Role | May |
| --- | --- |
| Listener | play; their own favourites, playlists, history and settings; their own version of the metadata |
| Editor | the above; edit the shared metadata and covers; add music; organise |
| Admin | the above; invite people and change their roles up to editor; move music to the trash; the instance's settings |
| Owner | the above; storage, backups and restores, remote access; make and unmake admins and owners; there is always at least one owner, and admins cannot remove one |

Every change to the shared catalogue is logged with who made it.

### Songs by their hashes; metadata in layers

- **Storage**: each song is kept once, immutable, named by the SHA-256 of its audio region (the bytes
  between the tags: `getAudioFrameSlice`'s region, today's `audioChecksum`, with D7's SHA-256). Its
  name does not change when a tag is edited or the song is renamed, and the same audio imported twice
  is stored once. Covers are kept the same way, by the SHA-256 of their bytes.
- **Metadata in three layers**, per field: the file's (the tags as imported), the family's (what
  editors set), and, if a person wants it, their own. Each person sees the result; a switch shows
  the family's version or theirs.
- **Files out**: a download, a stream to a Subsonic client, or an export gets its tags written on the
  fly by `crates/tags` (ID3v2 and FLAC's blocks sit at the head of the file, the audio region comes
  from storage), with the family's or the person's metadata.
- **Consequence**: the server's disk is a store, not a folder to browse. An export rebuilds a plain
  `Artist/Album/NN - Title` tree with tags written, at any time.
- This is D9 (canonical tags outside the files), `.meta` v2's content-addressed images, and
  vfs-sync's own model, on the server.
- Open: the same audio imported again with other tags: proposed, the second import's differences
  become that person's own layer, and editors see them as a suggestion for the family's.

### Backups (proposed, answering the owner's question 5)

The 3-2-1 rule: three copies, on two media, one away from home.

1. **Built in.** The SQLite database (users, roles, metadata layers, playlists) is snapshotted
   nightly with `VACUUM INTO`, kept 7 daily, 4 weekly, 12 monthly. The songs and covers need no
   snapshot: they never change. A deletion goes to a trash for 30 days before its blob is removed. A
   weekly scrub hashes every blob again and reports any whose bytes no longer match its name (a
   failing disk), so it can be restored.
2. **Away from home.** The data folder (blobs and snapshots) copied with restic, encrypted before it
   leaves, to a second place: a relative's machine, a USB disk kept elsewhere, or object storage (B2,
   a Hetzner Storage Box). Since blobs never change, after the first copy only new music travels.
   Shipped as a documented service beside the server; restores are an owner's action.
3. **No lock-in.** The plain-folder export above, with tags written, readable by any player,
   whenever wanted.

### OpenSubsonic

The owner looks into it; F1's API is kept so that it can be added beside it later.

### Roadmap

- `ROADMAP/2026-11-04/family-instance.md` (new): F0 spike, F1 the server and its store, F2 people and
  roles, F3 each person's own data and metadata, F4 concurrency and backups, F5 packaging; after E4.
- `ROADMAP/2026-11-04/cloud-sources-and-accounts.md`: after the family instance; the public account
  (C3, C3b) goes, since the instance's accounts are F2's; C0 keeps Box, Drive and the playback
  proxy; C4, the relay, stays its own step.
- `ROADMAP/2026-11-04/rust-tag-engine-and-tauri.md`: E0 done on 2026-11-04; the later dates move a
  day earlier.

Suggested commit message: `docs: the family instance's decisions, backups and roadmap; E0 done`.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 21:40 — Correction: passkeys independent of intergate; the projects are not tied

**Summary**: The owner will not tie jackbird to intergate. jackbird's passkeys are its own, bound to
the instance's own address, which the owner sets; intergate is one way of reaching the instance among
others, and nothing in jackbird knows about it. What 21:32 says about the intergate subdomain
becoming the passkeys' RP ID is superseded by this; the rest of 21:32 stands.

User message (translated): *"I would do the passkeys independently of intergate; I'm not going to tie
the projects together."*

- **The instance's address is a setting of its own** (its public origin), and the passkeys' RP ID is
  that domain. Any route that serves it works: the home network, a private network (WireGuard,
  Tailscale), a reverse proxy with a domain, a tunnel (intergate or another). The route can change;
  the address should not.
- **What the web imposes, whatever the route**: a passkey is bound to the domain the browser sees, and
  WebAuthn needs a secure context (HTTPS, or `localhost`). So the owner picks the address once;
  changing it later means registering the passkeys again. WebAuthn's related origins
  (`/.well-known/webauthn`, in current Chromium and Safari) can add more addresses for the same
  passkeys later, such as a name on the home network.
- **Sign-in without a passkey** for the first owner and for recovery: a one-time code the server
  prints on its console (or in its logs), so no email is required.
- **Roadmap**: F0 checks passkeys on the instance's own address, reached by more than one route;
  intergate is tried as one of them, not built in. F5 documents routes and bundles none.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 21:35 — Instance modes: open or with sign-in, one person or several (proposal)

**Summary**: The owner wants the way in to be a setting of each instance: open or with sign-in, for
one person or several. Sign-in is what makes several people safe; an open instance is for one
person, or for a few who trust each other on a trusted network. Awaiting approval; the roadmap's F2
takes it.

User message (translated): *"For example, jackbird could run with a login or open. The login lets us
enable several users. An instance could have a single user or several, with a login or open, etc."*

| | One person | Several people |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Open** | no sign-in; the person is the owner (a personal instance on the machine itself or a private network) | profiles to pick, as a family television does, with no sign-in; roles are defaults, not protection, since anyone can pick any profile (an optional PIN per profile only keeps honest people honest) |
| **Sign-in** | one owner with a passkey | the family instance of 21:32: passkeys, roles enforced |

- **One server, one setting**: the mode is the instance's configuration; the store, the metadata
  layers and the per-person data are the same in every mode, so a mode can change without
  migrating anything.
- **From open to sign-in**: the first owner claims the instance with the one-time console code
  (21:40); the existing profiles become people who register their passkeys through invitations,
  keeping their data and their own metadata.
- **Guard for open instances**: open means anyone who reaches it can edit and delete the catalogue,
  so an open instance listens only on the machine itself and private network ranges by default,
  says so on screen, and refuses a public address unless the owner forces it explicitly.
- **A thought, not a commitment**: the desktop app is, in effect, an instance with one person, open,
  on the machine itself; whether the app and the server share that code path is for E4 and F1 to
  weigh.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 21:45 — E1: what lofty's tag model loses beyond E0's cases; E1 with lofty's frame codecs only (proposal)

**Summary**: Before building E1 as approved at 18:35 (lofty's concrete tags, plus a merge layer),
lofty 0.25.5 was tried on cases E0 did not build: duplicate frames, years that are not dates, empty
frames, a v2.4 frame with a data length indicator, and odd Vorbis comments. Its tag-wide models
(`Id3v2Tag`, `VorbisComments`) lose data in its default mode in five more ways, refuse real files in
strict mode, cannot read at all an MP3 whose year is "c. 1959", and write one kind of frame broken.
Each loss comes from the same design: lofty parses every frame to edit one, and writes every frame
again. Proposal (awaiting approval): E1 keeps the tag's structure in its own code, so a frame,
block or comment jackbird does not edit is never parsed and goes back byte for byte; lofty is used
only to decode and encode the frames jackbird reads and writes. The plan for E1 follows.

User message (translated): *"Go ahead with E1."*

### What was tried

A scratch crate in `.tmp/e1/probe/` (not committed) over `lofty` `=0.25.5` (with
`id3v2_compression_support`, without which it does not build), Rust 1.97. Tags built byte by byte
around 0.5 s of `ffmpeg` audio; each read in lofty's three parsing modes (`Strict`, `BestAttempt`,
its default, and `Relaxed`) through `MpegFile` / `FlacFile`, and written back through
`Id3v2Tag::save_to`.

### Findings

| Case | Default mode (`BestAttempt`) | `Strict` | What a save would do |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Two `TIT2` frames, or two `TXXX` with one description (invalid, but found in files) | the later one replaces the earlier on reading | the same | drops a frame |
| `TYER` / `TDRC` "c. 1959" | **the whole file fails to read** | fails | nothing: the file shows no tags |
| `TDRC` "1959-1960" | read as "1959-19" | refused | writes "1959-19" |
| `TYER` / `TDRC` "59", "" (empty) | the frame is dropped | refused | drops it |
| `TYER` / `TDRC` " 1959", "1959?" | read as "1959" | refused | rewrites it |
| A frame of size 0 | dropped | refused | drops it |
| A v2.4 frame with a data length indicator | read | read | written with the flag and without the indicator: **the result does not parse** (E-D3's check would refuse every save of such a file) |
| v2.3 `TYER` + `TDAT` | merged into a `TDRC` moved to the end; `FrameList::insert` also moves any replaced frame to the end | the same | the frames' order changes |
| FLAC: a comment without `=` | dropped | dropped | drops it |
| FLAC: a comment with invalid UTF-8, or a key with a non-ASCII letter | dropped | the whole file fails | drops it |

Beside E0's five (18:30: v2.4 plain sizes cut, a text `TDRC` dropped on writing, `set_date`'s
second `TDRC`, padding never reused, a FLAC with a leading ID3v2 tag not writable).

### What it means

- The spike's merge layer works around losses one at a time, after lofty has parsed the whole tag.
  New cases keep turning up, and some cannot be worked around after the fact: once lofty has read
  a tag, a dropped duplicate or comment is gone, and a file it cannot read cannot be saved.
- Strict mode turns silent losses into refusals, but of files real libraries hold (empty frames,
  "1959?"), and still drops comments without `=`.
- "Keep what is not edited" should hold by construction, not by a list of cases. Today's
  `src/helpers/id3v2.ts` already works that way (frames as raw bytes, the edited ones replaced), and
  its tests are the cases E1 has to cover.

### Proposal: the structure in jackbird's code, lofty for the frames' contents

- **ID3v2**: the crate reads and writes the tag's structure itself, as `id3v2.ts` does today: the
  header, an extended header (skipped), a v2.4 footer, v2.3's whole-tag unsynchronisation, v2.4
  tags written with plain frame sizes, padding. Frames are kept as raw `(id, flags, body)`. Only the
  frames jackbird reads (`TIT2 TPE1 TALB TYER|TDRC TCON TRCK`, `APIC`) are decoded, with lofty's
  frame codecs (`TextInformationFrame::parse`, `AttachedPictureFrame::parse`, after undoing a v2.4
  frame's unsynchronisation and data length indicator), and the ones a save changes are encoded
  with them (`as_bytes`). Every other frame is written back byte for byte, in its place.
- **FLAC**: the crate reads and writes the metadata blocks itself; every block but
  `VORBIS_COMMENT`, the replaced `PICTURE` and `PADDING` goes back byte for byte. The comments are
  kept as their raw entries (whatever bytes they hold); only the first entry of an edited key is
  replaced in place, or removed. The new front cover's block is encoded with lofty's
  `Picture::as_flac_bytes`, and pictures are read with `Picture::from_flac_bytes`.
- **What changes from 18:30**: "lofty's concrete tags only" becomes "lofty's frame and picture
  codecs only" (no `Id3v2Tag`, `MpegFile`, `FlacFile`, `VorbisComments` or `Probe`). The merge
  layer's steps become properties of the crate's own layer (changed fields only and in place,
  plain sizes read, padding reused, a FLAC's leading ID3v2 tag left as it is), and E-D3's checks
  stay and grow (below). lofty stays pinned (`=0.25.5`). The wasm should be smaller than E0's
  145 KB, since lofty's file parsers go; E2 measures it.

### The year

- **Read** as the text the frame holds ("c. 1959" included), as today.
- **Written** as an ID3 timestamp, or the save is refused with a message, as 18:30 approved: `yyyy`,
  `yyyy-MM` or `yyyy-MM-dd`. v2.4 writes it to `TDRC`; v2.3 writes `TYER` (`yyyy`) and, with a day,
  `TDAT` (`ddMM`), removing an old `TDAT` and `TIME`. An empty year removes the year's frames. A year
  left as it was is not written, so a file holding "c. 1959" keeps it.
- FLAC's `DATE` is free text in Vorbis comments, so any text is written there.

### E1 in detail

**Layout.** A Cargo workspace at the repository's root, `members = ["crates/*"]`, excluding
`src-tauri/` (it joins in E4) and `spikes/`; its `target/` ignored by git. `crates/tags/`, package
`jackbird-tags`, no I/O, no `unsafe`:

| Module | What |
| --- | --- |
| `lib.rs` | the public API below |
| `region.rs` | `format_of`, `audio_region` |
| `id3v2.rs` | the tag's structure: read (every case `id3v2.ts` reads), write, frame flags |
| `mp3.rs` | the six fields and the cover over the frames; the MP3 save |
| `flac.rs` | blocks, Vorbis comment entries, pictures; the FLAC save |
| `checks.rs` | the safeguards |

**API.**

```rust
pub enum Format { Mp3, Flac }
pub fn format_of(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<Format>        // fLaC (after an optional ID3v2 tag), or an MPEG frame sync
pub fn audio_region(bytes: &[u8]) -> Range<usize>       // what the audio checksum hashes
pub struct Fields { title, artist, album, year, genre, track: String }
pub struct Cover { data: Vec<u8>, mime: String }
pub struct Tags { format: Format, fields: Fields, cover: Option<Cover> }
pub fn read(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Tags, Error>
pub struct Edit { title, artist, album, year, genre, track: Option<String>, cover: Option<Cover> }
pub fn write(bytes: &[u8], edit: &Edit) -> Result<Vec<u8>, Error>
pub enum Error { … }                                     // with a stable code() for E2's translations
```

- `audio_region` for MP3 is `getAudioFrameSlice`'s definition exactly (after the ID3v2 tag and its
  footer; before an ID3v1 tag and a 32-byte APEv2 footer; the whole file when that leaves
  nothing), so the checksums in every `.meta/` stay valid (E-D5). FLAC: after the last metadata
  block. Anything else: the MP3 definition, as today for every file.
- `read`: the six fields as strings, as `TrackMetadata` holds them, and the cover shown (the front
  cover, else the first picture). A v2.4 frame with several values gives them joined by `/`; FLAC
  gives a key's first value, and `TRACKNUMBER` with `TRACKTOTAL` (or `TOTALTRACKS`) as `n/total`.
  ID3v2.2 tags are read too.
- `write`: a field set to its current value, or `None`, is left alone; an empty one removes the
  field; the cover replaces the shown picture in place, or is added. Nothing changed gives back the
  same bytes. A changed tag keeps its version (v2.3 or v2.4; a new tag is v2.3, as today); its text
  is ISO-8859-1 when it fits, else UTF-16 (v2.3) or UTF-8 (v2.4); its extended header goes (its
  CRC would be stale); frames flagged "discard when the tag is altered" go, as `keepUneditedFrames`
  does; it is padded back to its old length when it fits, so the audio does not move, else given
  1 KB of padding. FLAC: the padding is put last and sized so the audio does not move when it
  fits; `n/total` writes `TRACKNUMBER` and `TRACKTOTAL`. ID3v1 and APE tags at the end, and a FLAC's
  leading ID3v2 tag, are left as they are.
- **Refused**, with a code and a message: not MP3 or FLAC; a tag whose structure cannot be read; a
  year that is not a timestamp (ID3); an ID3v2.2 tag (read, not written: today's save replaces such
  a tag and loses its other frames; converting it is a later step if wanted); a compressed or
  encrypted frame among those a save must change.

**Safeguards** (E-D3), on every save, before the bytes are given back:

1. The result is read again by the crate: its fields and cover must be the ones asked for.
2. Its audio region must be byte-identical to the source's.
3. Every frame (ID3v2), block and comment entry (FLAC) of the source that the edit does not touch
   must be in the result, byte for byte (after undoing v2.3's unsynchronisation), in the same order.

**Fixtures** (`fixtures/tags/`, shared with E2's `npm test`): the source files, small (0.5 s of
audio, 1 KB covers), and `cases.json` with what each one must give: format, fields, cover (MIME
type, size, SHA-1), audio region (start, end, SHA-1). Made by `fixtures/tags/make.mjs` (Node,
`ffmpeg`, `browser-id3-writer` and `src/helpers/id3v2.ts`), and committed, so tests need neither
`ffmpeg` nor Node. The expectations come from how each file is built, not from the crate.

- MP3: v2.3 and v2.4 with padding and the 18 frames of E0; v2.3 unsynchronised; v2.4 with plain
  frame sizes; an extended header (v2.3, v2.4); a v2.4 footer; an ID3v1 tag; an APEv2 footer; no
  tag; a v2.4 frame with unsynchronisation and a data length indicator; duplicate frames; years
  that are not dates; empty frames; UTF-16 and several values; ID3v2.2; a file saved by today's
  app (`browser-id3-writer` and `keepUneditedFrames`).
- FLAC: E0's four (full, padding first, no padding, an ID3v2 tag before `fLaC`); no comments; odd
  comments (no `=`, invalid UTF-8, a non-ASCII key); a track number with a total.

**Tests** (`cargo test`): every case of `src/helpers/id3v2.test.ts`, as unit tests of `id3v2.rs`;
for each fixture, `read` and `audio_region` against `cases.json`; edits on each (the title; every
field; a field emptied; a new cover; nothing), each passing the three safeguards, keeping the audio
where it was when the tag fits, and giving the same bytes when made twice; the refusals. As an
outside check in the tests, lofty's own file reader reads each saved fixture whose years are dates
and finds the same fields. Once by hand, recorded in the walkthrough: `mutagen` parses and `ffmpeg`
decodes every saved file, as in E0.

**CI**: `.github/workflows/tags.yml`, on a push or pull request touching `crates/`, `fixtures/`, the
`Cargo` files or itself: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy -D warnings` and `cargo test --locked`.
The web build and its release need no Rust until E2.

**Docs**: `docs/development.md` (the crate, `cargo test`, the fixtures), `docs/architecture.md` (the
engine being built, not yet used by the app), `AGENTS.md` (the `crates/` rule: no I/O, tests on the
shared fixtures).

**The spike goes**: `spikes/e0-lofty/` is removed once E1 has taken what it needs (as 18:35 said);
its code stays in the history.

**Roadmap**: unchanged until this is approved; then E1's row says "lofty's frame codecs".

**Done when** `cargo test` passes on every fixture, including every case of the TypeScript
`id3v2` tests, with the three safeguards on every save.

Suggested commit message: `docs(record): E1, lofty's tag model loses more than E0 found; its frame codecs only (proposal)`.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 22:00 — E1: alternatives to lofty (answering the owner)

**Summary**: The owner asks for alternatives to lofty. Every tag library looked at keeps a tag as a
whole model (read every frame, write every frame again), as lofty does, so none gives "keep what
is not edited" by itself: the tag's structure has to be jackbird's own whichever library decodes
the frames. With the structure its own, what a library adds is small: decoding and encoding text
frames, `APIC` and FLAC `PICTURE` blocks. Recommended: no tag library at all (option C below);
lofty's codecs (21:45's proposal) remain the alternative. Awaiting the owner's choice.

User message (translated): *"Alternatives to lofty?"*

| Library | What it is | For jackbird |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [`id3`](https://docs.rs/id3) (rust-id3, 1.17) | ID3v1 read, ID3v2.2–2.4 read and write; MP3, WAV, AIFF | mature; keeps unknown frames (`Content::Unknown`); writes into the padding when it fits. A whole-tag model too (`add_frame` replaces "any conflicting frame"), so the duplicates and odd years of 21:45 need the same checks; no FLAC |
| [`metaflac`](https://docs.rs/metaflac) (0.2.8) | FLAC metadata blocks | Vorbis comments held as `HashMap<String, Vec<String>>`: the comments' order is lost on writing |
| `audiotags` | one API over `id3`, `metaflac` and `mp4ameta` | inherits their losses, with a coarser model |
| `symphonia` | decoding, with metadata reading | read only; of use for E5 (native output) |
| TagLib (C++): [`taglib-wasm`](https://charleswiltgen.github.io/taglib-wasm/) on the web, bindings natively | the library most players use | the most tested, many formats; but C++ (Emscripten for the web, a C++ toolchain on the three desktop systems, outside wasm-bindgen), a much larger module, and a whole-tag model as well |

Options for E1, the structure being jackbird's own in all of them:

- **B (21:45)**: lofty's frame codecs (`TextInformationFrame`, `AttachedPictureFrame`,
  `Picture::from_flac_bytes` / `as_flac_bytes`).
- **C (recommended)**: no tag library. The codecs are small and fully specified: text encodings
  0–3 (ISO-8859-1, UTF-16 with a BOM, UTF-16BE, UTF-8; decoded leniently, so a broken string reads
  instead of failing), `APIC` (and v2.2's `PIC`), FLAC's `PICTURE` block, Vorbis comments as UTF-8.
  Some 200 lines more than B, and in return: no dependency to pin and watch (lofty 0.25.5 does not
  even build without its compression feature), the smallest wasm, and every byte the crate writes
  is code the project tests. lofty stays as a dev-dependency only, as the outside check of 21:45's
  tests (it reads every saved fixture whose years are dates and must find the same fields).

The rest of 21:45's plan is the same under B or C.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 22:12 — E1: jackbird's own tag libraries, no tag dependency (the owner's decision); the crates (proposal)

**Summary**: The owner chooses option C of 22:00, as jackbird's own library: the tag code is
written in the project, with the libraries named at 22:00 as references, not dependencies, so none
has to be pinned, watched or replaced later. Proposed: three crates, `jackbird-id3`,
`jackbird-flac` and `jackbird-tags`, each usable on its own and publishable later. What 21:45 says
about lofty's codecs is superseded by this; the rest of 21:45 (the structure kept byte for byte,
the year rule, the refusals, the safeguards, the fixtures, CI) stands. Awaiting approval of the
crates and a go.

User message (translated): *"On the alternatives to lofty, let's make jackbird/id3, relying on the
ones we mentioned, and so we avoid dependencies in the future. Does that fit?"*

### The crates

| Crate (`crates/…`) | What | Like |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `id3` → `jackbird-id3` | ID3v2.2–2.4 tags as frames: read (the header, an extended header, a v2.4 footer, v2.3's unsynchronisation, v2.4's per-frame unsynchronisation and data length indicator, plain-size v2.4 tags, padding), write (frames kept as raw bytes, padding sized), and the codecs of the frames jackbird uses: text frames (encodings 0–3), `APIC` / `PIC`. ID3v1 read; where an APEv2 footer ends | `rust-id3`, without its whole-tag model |
| `flac` → `jackbird-flac` | FLAC metadata: blocks (read, write, padding last and sized), Vorbis comments as raw entries with their lenient UTF-8 view, `PICTURE` blocks; an ID3v2 tag before `fLaC` is skipped and kept | `metaflac`, keeping the comments' order and bytes |
| `tags` → `jackbird-tags` | jackbird's layer: the format told from the first bytes, the six fields and the cover over both, `audio_region`, the year rule, the refusals and the three safeguards (21:45) | — |

- **No dependencies** in any of the three (only `std`). lofty and `id3` may stay as
  **dev-dependencies** of `jackbird-tags`, for the outside check of 21:45's tests (another
  library reads each saved fixture and finds the same fields); they never reach a build. Whether
  to keep that check is the owner's call; `mutagen` and `ffmpeg` are checked once by hand either way.
- E2's wasm bindings are the one place a dependency was planned (`wasm-bindgen`). They can do
  without it too: a few exported functions over the module's memory (allocate, read, write, free)
  and some 50 lines of hand-written JavaScript. Decided in E2.
- `id3` and `flac` know nothing of jackbird (no fields, no checksums); `tags` is what the app,
  the wasm and the Tauri side use.

### References, not code

- **The specifications** first: ID3v2.3 and v2.4 (id3.org's informal standards), ID3v2.2,
  ID3v1; FLAC (RFC 9639); Vorbis comments (Xiph's specification).
- **The libraries** for behaviour and edge cases: lofty (MIT or Apache-2.0), rust-id3 (MIT),
  metaflac, mutagen (GPL-2.0), TagLib (LGPL-2.1 / MPL-1.1). The code is written from the
  specifications; no code is copied from the GPL and LGPL ones, and anything taken from a
  permissive one keeps its licence notice. The cases each library's tests and issue trackers
  document (iTunes' plain sizes, broken UTF-16, duplicate frames…) become fixtures.
- jackbird has no licence file yet; publishing the crates (crates.io, or their own repositories)
  needs one: the owner's call, not E1's.

### What changes in 21:45's plan

- The workspace's members are `crates/id3`, `crates/flac` and `crates/tags`; each has its own
  unit tests, and the shared fixtures (`fixtures/tags/`) are tested through `jackbird-tags`.
- `lofty` and its pin go; the 18:30 decision "lofty pinned, the fixtures run on every bump" no
  longer applies.
- The ID3v1 tag is read only as far as `audio_region` needs (where it starts); its fields are not
  read, as today.
- Done when `cargo test` passes for the three crates, on every fixture and every case of the
  TypeScript `id3v2` tests, with the three safeguards on every save.

Suggested commit message: `docs(record): E1, jackbird's own tag crates (id3, flac, tags), no tag dependency`.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 22:45 — E1 done: jackbird's own tag crates; walkthrough

**Summary**: E1 is built as approved at 22:20: three crates with no dependency (`jackbird-id3`,
`jackbird-flac`, `jackbird-tags`), 26 shared fixtures with their expectations, 70 tests, a CI
workflow, the docs, and the spike removed. Every write passes the three safeguards; lofty and
rust-id3 read what jackbird writes; `mutagen` parses and `ffmpeg` decodes all 72 files saved from
the fixtures with their audio unchanged; the 19 MP3 fixtures' audio regions are exactly
`getAudioFrameSlice`'s. Nothing in the app uses the crates yet (that is E2).

### What was built

- **Workspace**: `Cargo.toml` at the root (`members = crates/*`, `src-tauri/` excluded, so its own
  lock, target and CI stay as they were: checked with `cargo locate-project`), `rustfmt.toml`
  (120 columns), workspace lints (`unsafe_code = "forbid"`, `missing_docs`, clippy's `all`),
  `rust-version = "1.88"` (let chains). `/target/` ignored.
- **`crates/id3`** (`jackbird-id3`, 520 lines and 351 of tests): `read` gives a `Tag` (version,
  header flags, frames as raw `(id, flags, body)`, its length with a v2.4 footer, its padding,
  `clean`, `plain_sizes`): v2.2's three-letter frames, v2.2 and v2.3 whole-tag unsynchronisation
  undone, extended headers skipped, a v2.4 tag's syncsafe sizes tried first and plain ones after,
  junk or an overrunning frame flagged as not clean (the frames before it still given). `write`
  writes v2.3 or v2.4 from frames and padding (no extended header, no footer; only a v2.4 tag's
  unsynchronisation flag kept). `Frame::content` undoes a v2.4 frame's own unsynchronisation (or
  its tag's) and skips its group, encryption and length bytes, and says when a frame is compressed
  or encrypted. `decode_text` / `encode_text` (the four encodings, leniently: a missing byte order
  mark guessed, invalid sequences as U+FFFD; ISO-8859-1 when it fits, else UTF-16 in v2.3 and UTF-8
  in v2.4), `decode_picture` / `encode_picture` (`APIC`, and v2.2's `PIC`).
- **`crates/flac`** (`jackbird-flac`, 300 and 160): `read` gives the blocks and where `fLaC` and
  the audio are (after an ID3v2 tag some writers put first); `write` sets the last-block flag;
  `with_padding` puts one `PADDING` block last, sized so the audio stays put, or 1 KB when it does
  not fit; `Comments` keeps the vendor string, each entry's bytes and any trailing bytes;
  `Picture` codec; `image_size` reads a PNG's or JPEG's size and depth for a new picture block.
- **`crates/tags`** (`jackbird-tags`, 856 and 642, plus 377 of fixture tests): `format_of`,
  `audio_region`, `read`, `write`, `Fields`, `Edit`, `Cover`, `Error` with `code()`
  (`not-supported`, `unreadable`, `invalid-year`, `id3v22`, `too-large`, `refused-fields`,
  `refused-audio`, `refused-kept`). Every write goes through `verify`: the result read back must
  give the fields and cover asked for; its audio, and what follows it, must be byte-identical; and
  every frame, block and comment entry the edit does not touch must be in it, byte for byte and in
  order (`check_kept`, per format, written from rules apart from the writer's own code).
- **Fixtures** (`fixtures/tags/`, 188 KB): `make.mjs` builds 19 MP3 and 7 FLAC cases around half a
  second of `ffmpeg` audio (deterministic: two runs, the same bytes), and `cases.json`; a README.
- **CI**: `.github/workflows/tags.yml`, on changes to the crates, the fixtures or the Cargo files:
  `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy -D warnings`, `cargo test --locked`. `make test-crates` runs the
  same three.
- **Docs**: `docs/development.md` (Rust as a requirement for `crates/`, the commands, the
  fixtures), `docs/architecture.md` (the tag engine), `STACK.md` (tooling and the tree), `AGENTS.md`
  (the crates' rules: no I/O, no dependencies, specifications first, the checks before a commit).
- **The spike is gone** (`spikes/e0-lofty/`, and its `.gitignore` line): E1 took what it needed;
  its code stays in the history (#9).

### Decisions taken while building (within the approved plan)

- **Where edits go**: a changed field replaces the frame (or entry) it was read from, in place;
  a field the file did not have is added after the other frames. Emptying a field removes every
  frame or entry of it (duplicates included: the reader would otherwise show the next one);
  setting one replaces only the one shown and keeps any duplicate, untouched.
- **The year's frames**: setting or emptying the year removes every frame of a date (`TDRC`,
  `TYER`, `TDAT`, `TIME`) and puts the new one where the old was read from. v2.3 holds `yyyy` or a
  full date (`TYER` plus `TDAT`); `yyyy-MM` and times are refused there with `invalid-year`. v2.4
  takes every ID3v2.4 timestamp. To read a v2.3 date back, a `TYER` with a valid `TDAT` now reads
  as `yyyy-MM-dd` (today's app shows the year alone); a `TYER` alone reads as before.
- **No refusal for compressed or encrypted frames** (21:45 listed one): the writer never needs to
  read a frame it replaces, and a field it cannot read shows empty, so it is only replaced when the
  person types a value into it.
- **Reading is lenient, writing is not**: an MP3 whose tag cannot be read reads as untagged (as
  today); writing to it is refused (`unreadable`), so nothing it could not read is lost.
- **FLAC**: a missing comment block is created right after `STREAMINFO`, with the vendor string
  `jackbird`; the year goes to `DATE` unless the file keeps it in `YEAR` alone; `n/total` writes
  `TRACKNUMBER` and `TRACKTOTAL` (or `TOTALTRACKS` when that is the file's key), and a number with
  no total removes the old total, so the field reads back as written.
- **The format**: FLAC when `fLaC` starts the file (after an optional ID3v2 tag); MP3 when an
  ID3v2 tag does, or an MPEG frame sync is found in the first 64 KB.
- **Outside checks** skip what another library reads differently by design, each with its reason
  in the test: lofty cannot read a file whose year is "c. 1959" and keeps the later of two `TIT2`;
  rust-id3 refuses a grouped frame (`mp3-v24-frame-flags`). rust-id3 is a dev-dependency with its
  default features, which decode pictures.

### Checks

- `cargo test --workspace --locked`: 70 tests pass. `jackbird-id3` 28 (every case of
  `src/helpers/id3v2.test.ts`, v2.2, frame flags, codecs); `jackbird-flac` 8; `jackbird-tags` 21
  unit tests (every case of `keepUneditedFrames`' tests, each safeguard refusing a tampered result,
  years, regions, formats, errors) and 13 on the fixtures: each reads as `cases.json` says, has its
  region, saves a new title or is refused as it says (`id3v22`, `unreadable`); a title that fits
  leaves the audio where it was and the file the same size (every tagged case but
  `flac-no-padding`, which shrinks by a byte, too little for a padding block); every field at once,
  emptied fields, a new cover; the same edit twice gives the same bytes; nothing changed gives the
  input back; lofty reads every result's six fields and front cover, rust-id3 every MP3's.
- `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`: clean.
- By hand (`.tmp/scripts/validate-e1.py`, not committed): the 24 writable fixtures saved three
  ways each (a title; every field and a cover; two fields emptied), 72 files: `mutagen` 1.48.1
  parses each and finds the title written, `ffmpeg` decodes each without an error, and the decoded
  audio's MD5 equals its source's. 0 problems.
- By hand (`.tmp/scripts/region-parity.mjs`): `getAudioFrameSlice`, taken from `src/lib/id3.ts`,
  gives every MP3 fixture's region exactly as `cases.json` and `audio_region` do (19 of 19).
- `npm test` passes (nothing in `src/` changed).

### Next

E2: the crates in the web app, as WebAssembly (with or without `wasm-bindgen`, to decide there),
behind `src/lib/tags/`; the fixtures run in `npm test` too. Before that, W1 and W2 of the web plan.

Suggested commit message: `feat(crates): jackbird's own tag engine (id3, flac, tags) with shared fixtures; E1`.

## Extension — 2026-11-04 22:55 — E1: CI's first run, a lint of a newer clippy

**Summary**: `tags.yml`'s first run
([run 41927741918](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/41927741918)) failed at
clippy: CI's stable is Rust 1.99, whose clippy has a lint this container's 1.97 lacks
(`chunks_exact_to_as_chunks`, on the UTF-16 decoder in `crates/id3/src/text.rs`). Fixed with
`as_chunks::<2>()` (stable since 1.88, the crates' `rust-version`). `cargo fmt`, `clippy -D warnings`
and the 70 tests pass on both 1.99 and 1.97. CI keeps following stable, so a new lint can fail it
again; pinning the toolchain (`rust-toolchain.toml`) would stop that at the cost of the new lints,
the owner's call if it recurs.

## Extension — 2026-11-07 20:16 — E2, the tag engine in the web app as wasm: implementation plan

**Summary**: The plan for E2 of `ROADMAP/2026-11-04/rust-tag-engine-and-tauri.md`, at the owner's
request (*"redacta el plan de E2"*). `jackbird-tags` is built for `wasm32-unknown-unknown` through
a new `crates/tags-wasm`, loaded lazily behind one interface in `src/lib/tags/`, and replaces
`jsmediatags`, `browser-id3-writer` and `src/helpers/id3v2.ts`. Eight decisions are proposed, each
with its alternative; three questions are left to the owner. Nothing is implemented until it is
approved.

### Where it starts

- `src/lib/id3.ts` is today's engine: `readTrackMetadataFromBytes` (jsmediatags, the cover as a
  data URL), `writeTrackMetadataToBytes` (browser-id3-writer, then `keepUneditedFrames` from
  `src/helpers/id3v2.ts` puts back the frames it dropped), `getAudioFrameSlice` and
  `calculateDualChecksums` (SHA-1 through WebCrypto, of the whole file and of the audio region).
- Three callers: `src/lib/roots.ts` (the scan: checksums of every new or changed file, its tags
  when the checksum differs from `.meta/`), `src/lib/verifier.ts` (the background check, from 3 s
  after a root is ready: checksums of every track, its tags when they changed) and
  `src/lib/editor.ts` (a save: each selected track written, then its checksums).
- `TrackMetadata` holds the cover as a data URL, and `.meta/mp3-manager.json` stores it so.
- `jackbird-tags` (E1) already gives `read`, `write` with the three safeguards, `audio_region`
  (MP3: `getAudioFrameSlice`'s definition exactly, checked 19 of 19 in E1) and `format_of`.

### Decisions (proposed)

| # | Proposal | Alternative | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| E2-D1 | **No `wasm-bindgen`.** `crates/tags-wasm` exports a few plain functions over two buffers it owns (`input(len) -> ptr`, `read() -> status`, `write() -> status`, `region() -> status`, `output_ptr()`, `output_len()`), all in safe Rust: a pointer is handed out as a number (`as_mut_ptr()` is safe), never taken back as a slice, so the workspace's `unsafe_code = "forbid"` holds. The JavaScript side is ~80 lines of TypeScript in `src/lib/tags/wasm.ts`, typed by hand. | `wasm-bindgen` (=0.2.130, as in E0) and its CLI, pinned in CI | no dependency at all, as E1 decided for the crates (22:12); no CLI to pin and keep in step with the crate; the interface is four calls, so the generated glue (16 KB raw in E0) buys little |
| E2-D2 | **A small binary wire format** between the two: little-endian, length-prefixed (a status byte; then the format, the six fields as UTF-8, the cover's MIME type and bytes; or an error code and its text). Encoded in `crates/tags-wasm`, decoded and encoded in `src/helpers/tagwire.ts` (pure, tested, 90% floor), with a round trip on both sides. | JSON for the fields and a second buffer for the cover | no JSON in Rust without a dependency; one copy of the cover, not a base64 one |
| E2-D3 | **Built, not committed.** `scripts/build-wasm.mjs` runs `cargo build -p jackbird-tags-wasm --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --locked` (a `[profile.release]` for size: `opt-level = "z"`, LTO, one codegen unit, `panic = "abort"`, symbols stripped) and copies the module to `src/lib/tags/engine.wasm`, which `.gitignore` lists. `npm run dev`, `npm test` and `npm run build` run it first (`predev`, `pretest`, `prebuild`); it skips the cargo call when the module is newer than every file under `crates/`. Rust stable with the `wasm32-unknown-unknown` target becomes a development requirement (README, `STACK.md`, the Makefile's help); `build.yml` (so `deploy.yml` and `release.yml` too) installs it. `wasm-opt` is not used unless the size asks for it. | Commit the built `.wasm` (a web-only checkout needs no Rust), with a CI job that fails when it differs from a fresh build | one source of truth and no binary in the history; the cost is Rust for anyone who runs the web app from source |
| E2-D4 | **Lazy.** `src/lib/tags/index.ts` is the interface (`readTags`, `writeTags`, `checksums`); its implementation loads the module on the first call (`import("./wasm")`, the module itself through `new URL("./engine.wasm", import.meta.url)` and `WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming`), once, and every later call reuses it. A library that reopens from `.meta/` with nothing changed fetches neither. | Load it with the app | E-D2 of this record: the first load stays as fast as today |
| E2-D5 | **One definition of the audio region**, the engine's: `checksums(bytes)` asks the module for the region and hashes it, and the whole file, with WebCrypto's SHA-1 as today (E-D5). `getAudioFrameSlice` retires. Consequence: the verifier loads the module, from 3 s after a root is ready. | Keep `getAudioFrameSlice` in TypeScript for MP3 until E3 | E3 needs the FLAC region, which only the engine has; two definitions are what E-D5 wants to avoid; npm test checks the region of every fixture against `cases.json`, so the checksums `.meta/` holds stay valid |
| E2-D6 | **The cover can be removed.** The editor can remove a cover today (CoverDrop's Remove, `coverDataUrl: null`: `keepUneditedFrames` drops the first APIC), but `jackbird_tags::Edit` can only set one. `Edit.cover` becomes `CoverEdit::{Keep, Set(Cover), Remove}`; Remove takes out the picture shown (the front cover, else the first), and every other picture stays; safeguard 1 expects no cover shown or the next picture, safeguard 3 treats the removed one as edited. New tests, and a fixture with two pictures if none covers it. | Leave covers unremovable until later | otherwise E2 takes away something the web app does today |
| E2-D7 | **Data URLs stay** in `TrackMetadata` and `.meta/`; `src/helpers/cover.ts` turns the engine's bytes and MIME type into a data URL and back (base64 by chunks, not one `String.fromCharCode` per byte). A save passes `Keep` when the form's data URL is the track's own, `Remove` when it is `null` and the track had one, else `Set`. | Object URLs, or covers out of the snapshot | covers out of `.meta/` are `.meta` v2, deferred; no migration in this plan |
| E2-D8 | **Errors by code.** The engine's `Error::code()` (`not-supported`, `unreadable`, `invalid-year`, `id3v22`, `too-large`, `refused-fields`, `refused-audio`, `refused-kept`) becomes a `TagError` with that code, shown with new keys `tags.error.<code>` (English and Spanish), naming the file. A read that fails reads as empty fields, as today. A save that fails on one track, though, today throws out of the loop after earlier tracks were written but before any was recorded in the library and `.meta/`: E2 records the tracks saved before the failure, then reports it (the files on disk and `.meta/` no longer disagree; the verifier would mend it only later). | Keep today's handling | the engine refuses more than browser-id3-writer did (an invalid year, a v2.2 tag, a safeguard), so a partial save becomes likelier |

### What changes for the person (on files read again or saved)

A library that reopens from `.meta/` shows what it showed. A file read again (new, changed, or
found changed by the verifier) is read by the engine, which differs from jsmediatags in a few
places, each one as E1 decided: an artist with several values reads joined by `/`; the year of a
v2.3 tag keeps its date (TDAT), v2.4's TDRC is read first; the cover shown is the front cover, else
the first picture (jsmediatags: the first); ID3v2.2 tags are read. A save keeps the tag's version
(v2.3 stays v2.3, v2.4 stays v2.4; an untagged file gets v2.3, as today), keeps every frame it
does not edit byte for byte, rewrites the cover only when it changed, removes a field left empty
(today title, artist and album were written empty), and refuses a year that is not a date the tag
can hold. FLAC is not offered yet: the walk still takes `.mp3` only (E3).

### Layout

```
crates/tags/            CoverEdit (E2-D6)
crates/tags-wasm/       the exports and the wire encoder (E2-D1, D2); cargo tests of the encoding
scripts/build-wasm.mjs  the build of src/lib/tags/engine.wasm (E2-D3)
src/helpers/tagwire.ts  the wire format (+ .test.ts)
src/helpers/cover.ts    data URL <-> bytes and MIME type (+ .test.ts)
src/lib/tags/index.ts   the interface: readTags, writeTags, checksums, TagError; loads ./wasm lazily
src/lib/tags/wasm.ts    the module's glue; instantiate(bytes) for tests, load() for the app
src/lib/tags/wasm.test.ts  the fixtures against the built module
```

Retired: `src/lib/id3.ts`, `src/helpers/id3v2.ts` and its test, `src/types/jsmediatags.d.ts`, the
`jsmediatags` and `browser-id3-writer` dependencies. `roots.ts`, `verifier.ts` and `editor.ts`
import from `./tags`. `tsconfig.test.json` takes `src/lib/tags/` in. The workspace gains
`crates/tags-wasm`; `tags.yml` builds it for wasm too; `npm test`'s layer check already treats the
`.wasm` as an asset.

### Tests and measures

- `cargo test --workspace`: CoverEdit's cases (remove the only cover, the front of two, none to
  remove), and the wire encoding of every result and error.
- `npm test`, on the built module: every case of `fixtures/tags/cases.json` reads its fields and
  cover (SHA-1) as the file says, has its region (start, end, SHA-1), and saves a new title as it
  says (ok, or the error's code); the saved title reads back. `tagwire.ts` and `cover.ts` at the
  90% floor.
- By hand, in Chromium (Playwright), against today's engine on E0's 13.38 MB benchmark: the
  module's size raw and gzipped; read and save times; that a reload of a library whose `.meta/` is
  current requests no `.wasm` (the network log) and takes as long as today (medians of 9); a save,
  a cover removed, a refused year; `ffmpeg` decodes every saved file and its audio MD5 is its
  source's. The results go in the walkthrough.

### Steps (estimates)

1. `CoverEdit` in `jackbird-tags`, with its tests (0.5 d).
2. `crates/tags-wasm`, the release profile, `tags.yml` (0.5 d).
3. `tagwire.ts`, `cover.ts`, `src/lib/tags/` and `scripts/build-wasm.mjs`; the npm scripts and CI (1 d).
4. The callers moved to `src/lib/tags/`; the save's partial failure (E2-D8); the error texts (1 d).
5. The fixtures in `npm test`; the old engine and its dependencies removed (0.5 d).
6. The measures, the checks by hand, the walkthrough, the roadmap's row (1 d).

### Done when

E2's row: `npm test` runs the fixtures against the wasm build; an existing `.meta/` reopens without
re-reading any file (nor fetching the module); the first load is no slower. Plus: `jsmediatags`,
`browser-id3-writer` and `id3v2.ts` gone; covers can still be removed.

### Questions for the owner

1. E2-D1: no `wasm-bindgen` (recommended), or with it?
2. E2-D3: build the `.wasm` from source and require Rust to run the web app (recommended), or
   commit it?
3. E2-D8: record the tracks saved before a failure in the same change (recommended), or leave
   today's handling for a later fix?

## Extension — 2026-11-07 21:10 — E2 done: the tag engine in the web app as wasm; walkthrough

**Summary**: The plan of 20:16, as approved at 20:30, in its six steps. The web app reads, writes
and hashes files through `jackbird-tags` built as WebAssembly, loaded only when a file has to be
read, written or hashed; `jsmediatags`, `browser-id3-writer`, `src/lib/id3.ts` and
`src/helpers/id3v2.ts` are gone. Covers can still be removed (`CoverEdit`), and a save that the
engine refuses on one track records the tracks saved before it. E2's "done when" is met.

### What was done

1. **`CoverEdit`** (`crates/tags`): `Edit.cover` is `Keep`, `Set(Cover)` or `Remove`. Remove
   takes out the picture shown (the front cover, else the first) and keeps every other picture;
   nothing to remove gives the same bytes. Inside, the writers take a `CoverChange` (`Set` or
   `Remove`); safeguard 1 does not compare the cover after a removal, since safeguard 3 already
   checks that every other picture stays, byte for byte and in order. New tests: removing from
   three pictures, from one, from none; safeguard 3 refusing a removal that took the wrong picture;
   and on every writable fixture, removing pictures one by one until none is shown, the count
   equal to the pictures the file holds, the fields and the audio unchanged.
2. **`crates/tags-wasm`** (E2-D1, D2): seven exports, `tags_input`, `tags_read`, `tags_write`,
   `tags_region`, `tags_output_ptr`, `tags_output_len`, `tags_release`, over two buffers the
   module owns, in safe Rust. Two things found on the way:
   - The workspace's `unsafe_code = "forbid"` cannot hold for this crate: the `unsafe_code` lint
     counts `#[unsafe(no_mangle)]` itself as unsafe code (as it would `wasm-bindgen`'s generated
     exports). The crate sets the lint to `deny` and each export allows it alone; there is no
     unsafe block. `AGENTS.md` says so.
   - Exports named `read` and `write` replaced the C library's own when the crate was built
     natively for its tests, and the test binary hung. Hence the `tags_` prefix.
   The wire format (`src/wire.rs`): little-endian, lengths as `u32`, described at its top;
   `src/helpers/tagwire.ts` mirrors it, and both sides test the same bytes. A `[profile.wasm]` in
   the root `Cargo.toml` (opt-level `z`, LTO, one codegen unit, `panic = "abort"`, stripped)
   builds it for size without changing native release builds. `tags.yml` also lints it for
   `wasm32-unknown-unknown` and builds it.
3. **The web side**: `src/helpers/tagwire.ts`, `src/helpers/cover.ts` (data URL ⇄ bytes and MIME
   type by chunks; `editFor`, which keeps the cover when the form's is the track's own, removes it
   when the form has none, else sets it); `src/lib/tags/wasm.ts` (the glue: copy in, run, copy
   out; a trap marks the instance broken, and the next call loads a new one), `error.ts`
   (`TagError` with the engine's code) and `index.ts` (`readTags`, `writeTags`, `checksums`; it
   imports `wasm.ts` dynamically, so the glue is a chunk of its own, 2.7 KB, fetched with the
   module). `scripts/build-wasm.mjs` builds the module into `src/lib/tags/engine.wasm`
   (gitignored), skipping cargo when the module is newer than `crates/`, `Cargo.toml` and
   `Cargo.lock`; `predev`, `pretest` and `prebuild` run it, `npm run build:wasm` forces it.
   `build.yml` and `tauri-shell.yml` install Rust with the wasm target.
4. **The callers**: `roots.ts` and `verifier.ts` use `readTags` and `checksums`; `editor.ts` uses
   `writeTags` with the track's tags as they were, and `checksums`. A refused track stops the
   save: the tracks before it are written to the library and `.meta/` (`flushMeta`), and the
   message names the file and the reason, translated from the engine's code (new keys
   `message.saveFailedFor`, `message.savedBefore.*`, `tags.error.*`, English and Spanish). An
   unknown code falls back to the engine's English message.
5. **Retired**: `src/lib/id3.ts`, `src/helpers/id3v2.ts` and its test, `src/types/jsmediatags.d.ts`,
   the two npm dependencies. `fixtures/tags/make.mjs` imported both to make
   `mp3-saved-by-jackbird.mp3`; it now keeps that file as it was made (nothing writes it that way
   any more) and computes its case from its bytes. Run again, it gives the same 26 files, byte for
   byte; only that case's `about` changes.
6. **Docs**: `README.md` (Rust and its wasm target in the quick start), `STACK.md`,
   `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/development.md`, `fixtures/tags/README.md`, the Makefile's help,
   `AGENTS.md`; the roadmap marks E2 done.

### Checks

- `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings`, and
  the same clippy on `jackbird-tags-wasm` for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`: clean. `cargo test
  --workspace --locked`: 78 pass (`jackbird-id3` 28, `jackbird-flac` 8, `jackbird-tags` 23 unit and 14 on the fixtures, `jackbird-tags-wasm` 5).
- `npm test`: 300 pass, including `src/lib/tags/wasm.test.ts`, which runs every case of
  `fixtures/tags/cases.json` against the built module (fields, cover SHA-1, region and its SHA-1,
  a new title saved or refused with the expected code, the audio unchanged after), plus a cover
  set and removed, an invalid year refused by code, and a 32 MB file that grows the module's
  memory. The coverage check passes with 18 helpers at 90% or more.
- By hand in Chromium (Playwright, `.tmp/scripts/`, not committed), the built app on `vite
  preview`, a library of four fixtures in OPFS:
  - First open: the module is fetched once (~140–180 ms after the click), the tags and covers
    show.
  - Reopen from a current `.meta/` (IndexedDB cleared first, since headless Chromium crashes
    restoring OPFS handles): **no `.wasm` requested**, nor within the next 5 s (the verifier only
    re-reads tracks whose checksums are missing or older than a day). Shown in a median of
    129 ms, against 131 ms on `main` (9 reopens each).
  - Saves through the edit dialog: a title on the untagged file; the cover removed and a date
    (`1960-03-02`) written into a v2.3 tag (TYER + TDAT); `1960-03` refused on that v2.3 tag with
    "Could not save mp3-v23-id3v1.mp3: the year “1960-03” is not a date its tag can hold." The
    saved files read back as written, the cover removed (two pictures became one), the bytes
    after the tag identical, and `ffmpeg -map 0:a -f md5` gives each its source's MD5. (Without
    `-map 0:a`, ffmpeg also decodes the pictures as a video stream, so removing one changes that
    MD5: not the audio.)
- **Size**: the module is 103.3 KB raw, 40.1 KB gzipped (E0's lofty build: 430.5 / 144.8 KB for
  MP3 and FLAC), and imports nothing. The page's main chunk went from 244.7 KB (67.5 KB gzipped)
  on `main` to 181.2 KB (49.5 KB): the two tag libraries no longer load with the app.
- **Speed**, Chromium, dev server, a 13.35 MB file (5 min of pink noise at 320 kbps and a 1.34 MB
  JPEG cover), medians of 9 after one discarded, today's engine (`main`) against the wasm one:

  | Step | Today | wasm |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Load the module (once, fetch and compile) | — | 20 ms |
  | Read the six fields and the cover, as a data URL | 101 ms | 45 ms |
  | Save a title (today the cover written again; now left alone) | 96 ms | 25 ms |
  | Both checksums (the region now asked of the module) | 41 ms | 31 ms |

  Most of the 45 ms read is the cover's data URL (E0 measured the engine's read alone at ~10 ms).

### Left to the owner, by hand

- A save on a real folder with several tracks of which one is refused (the order of the message
  and the library afterwards), and a reload with the queue across two folders (W3), which this
  container cannot do (headless Chromium and OPFS handles).
- CI: `build.yml` now installs Rust; its first run on GitHub confirms the toolchain step and the
  cache.

### Next

By the roadmap: E3 (FLAC: the walk takes `.flac`, MIME types by extension, `Mp3Track` becomes
`Track`) and E4 (the desktop app), awaiting the owner's go.

Suggested commit message: `feat: the tag engine in the web app as WebAssembly; covers removable; E2`.

## Extension — 2026-11-07 22:28 — E3, FLAC in the web app: implementation plan

**Summary**: The plan for E3 of `ROADMAP/2026-11-04/rust-tag-engine-and-tauri.md`, at the owner's
request (*"Adelante con E3"*). The engine already reads and writes FLAC (E1) and runs in the web
app (E2); what E3 changes is the app around it: the walk, the playlists and the player take
`.flac` files, `Mp3Track` becomes `Track`, and the texts stop saying MP3 only. Six decisions are
proposed, one question is left to the owner. Nothing is implemented until it is approved.

### Where it starts

- `.mp3` is hard-coded in three places: the walk in `src/lib/roots.ts` (twice: the first scan and
  the resync) and the playlists' list of audio files in `src/lib/playlists.ts` (what VLC's
  absolute paths are matched against).
- The player plays a track from its file's object URL (`src/lib/media.ts`), with whatever MIME
  type the browser gave the file.
- `Mp3Track` is the track's type: 59 uses in 15 files (helpers, lib and `src/types.ts`).
- Texts: `landing.note` ("…your MP3 files"), `info.audioChecksum` ("…without ID3"), `README.md`,
  `docs/usage.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `STACK.md`.
- `npm test` already runs the seven FLAC fixtures through the module (E2).

### Decisions (proposed)

| # | Proposal | Alternative | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| E3-D1 | **One list of formats**, `src/helpers/formats.ts`: `AUDIO_FORMATS` (`.mp3` → `audio/mpeg`, `.flac` → `audio/flac`), `isAudioFile(path)` (by extension, any case) and `audioMimeType(path)`, tested. The walk (both places) and the playlists use it. | Two regexes, `/\.(mp3\|flac)$/i`, where `.mp3` is today | one place to add a format later (Ogg, M4A); the player needs the MIME type too |
| E3-D2 | **The player says the type.** `openMedia` gives the element `file.slice(0, file.size, audioMimeType(path))`: still the file on disk, not read into memory, but typed by extension, so playback does not depend on the MIME type the system gives `.flac` (empty on some). | Leave the file's own type | the roadmap's "MIME types by extension"; the cost is one line |
| E3-D3 | **`Mp3Track` becomes `Track`**, a mechanical rename, and `mp3Files` and its kin in `roots.ts` become `audioFiles`. The names that reach the person's disk stay: `.meta/mp3-manager.json`, the `mp3-manager` IndexedDB database, the package name. | Rename those too | renaming them would make every existing library read its files again and forget its session; that rename belongs to the Musikmatch rename, deferred |
| E3-D4 | **Texts**: `landing.note` says "your music files"; `info.audioChecksum` "without tags"; the docs say MP3 and FLAC where they say MP3. Both languages. | — | the roadmap's "texts no longer say MP3 only" |
| E3-D5 | **Info shows the format.** The selection's Info tab gets a "Format" row (MP3, FLAC), from the extension (`formats.ts`), so a library that mixes both says which is which. | Leave it out | one row, and the only place the person can tell a FLAC from an MP3 of the same album |
| E3-D6 | **Measure memory, change nothing yet.** A read hands the whole file to the module, which keeps some 3× the largest file it has seen (wasm memory never shrinks): ~40 MB for a 13 MB MP3, but a 24/96 FLAC track can be 150 MB. E3 measures it on such a file in Chromium; if it is a problem, a follow-up gives the module only the head of the file to read (the tags and metadata blocks are at the start), which needs a change to the engine's interface. | Do that change now | it needs the engine to read a prefix and to compute the region from a prefix, a tail and a length: a step of its own, worth taking only if the measure asks for it |

### What does not change

The engine (E1, E2), the checksums' definitions, `.meta/`'s format, renaming (it keeps each file's
own extension), the editor (the year is any text in FLAC, as the engine allows), the safeguards.

### Tests and checks

- `npm test`: `formats.ts` at the 90% floor; the helpers' tests that build tracks use `Track`.
- By hand in Chromium (Playwright, `.tmp/scripts/`, not committed), the built app, a library in
  OPFS of FLAC files made with `ffmpeg` (16/44.1 and 24/96, with comments jackbird does not edit:
  ALBUMARTIST, DISCNUMBER, a COMMENT, REPLAYGAIN_*; a picture and padding) and the FLAC fixtures,
  beside MP3s:
  - it opens and shows the tags and covers; a FLAC track plays (the element's time advances); the
    Info tab says FLAC;
  - edits save: a title, every field, a new cover, the cover removed; each saved file decodes
    with `ffmpeg` to the same audio MD5 as its source, and keeps every comment and block it did
    not edit (`ffprobe`'s tags, and the blocks compared with the engine's own reader);
  - a reload from `.meta/` reads no file;
  - the module's memory after reading the 24/96 file (E3-D6).

### Steps (estimates)

1. `formats.ts`; the walk, the playlists and the player use it (0.5 d).
2. `Mp3Track` → `Track` (0.25 d).
3. The texts, the Info row, the docs (0.25 d).
4. The checks by hand, the memory measure, the walkthrough, the roadmap's row (0.5 d).

### Done when

E3's row: a FLAC library opens, plays, edits and saves, and every saved file decodes with `ffmpeg`
and keeps its unedited comments and blocks.

### Question for the owner

1. E3-D5: the "Format" row in Info (recommended), or leave it out?

## Extension — 2026-11-07 22:35 — E3 done: FLAC in the web app; walkthrough

**Summary**: The plan of 22:28, as approved at 22:29, in its four steps. A folder's `.flac` files
are tracks: they open, play, edit and save, and every saved file decodes with `ffmpeg` to its
source's audio and keeps every comment and block it did not edit. E3's "done when" is met. The
memory measure (E3-D6) and one behaviour of E1's FLAC writer are left to the owner.

### What was done

1. **`src/helpers/formats.ts`** (E3-D1): `AUDIO_FORMATS` (`.mp3` `audio/mpeg` MP3, `.flac`
   `audio/flac` FLAC), `audioFormatOf`, `isAudioFile`, `audioMimeType`, with tests. The walk
   (`roots.ts`, first scan and resync; `mp3Files` is now `audioFiles`) and the playlists'
   audio files (`playlists.ts`) use it.
2. **The player** (E3-D2): `openMedia` hands the element
   `file.slice(0, file.size, audioMimeType(path) || file.type)`, still the file on disk.
3. **`Mp3Track` → `Track`** (E3-D3) in the 15 files that used it. `.meta/mp3-manager.json`, the
   `mp3-manager` IndexedDB database and the package name stay.
4. **Texts** (E3-D4): `landing.note` ("your music files" / "tus archivos de música"),
   `info.audioChecksum` ("without tags" / "sin etiquetas"); `README.md`, `docs/usage.md` (what a
   save writes in each format, and that a FLAC year is any text), `docs/architecture.md`,
   `THESAURUS.md` (Track, Metadata).
5. **Info's "Format" row** (E3-D5): `info.format` ("Format" / "Formato"), from `audioFormatOf`.
6. The roadmap marks E3 done.

### Checks

- `npm test`: 304 pass; the coverage check passes with 19 helpers at 90% or more. `tsc --noEmit`
  clean. The crates are unchanged.
- By hand in Chromium (Playwright, `.tmp/scripts/e3-browser.cjs` and `e3-files.py`, not
  committed), the built app, a library in OPFS of `cd.flac` (20 s, 16/44.1, a 1.34 MB JPEG
  picture, ALBUMARTIST, DISCNUMBER, COMMENT and REPLAYGAIN_TRACK_GAIN beside the six fields),
  `hires.flac` (4.5 min of pink noise, 24/96, 70 MB, no picture, ALBUMARTIST and COMMENT),
  the `flac-full` fixture and an MP3:
  - It opens with every title and cover; the module is fetched once. `hires.flac` plays: its
    title on the display and the time at 00:02 after 2.5 s. Info says "Format FLAC".
  - Four saves through the edit dialog, all "Metadata saved": a title (`cd.flac`); every field
    and a PNG cover, with the year "c. 1959" (`flac-full`); a date (`hires.flac`, 70 MB); the
    cover removed (`cd.flac`, one picture to none).
  - Each saved file against its source: `ffmpeg -map 0:a -f md5` the same, the bytes after the
    metadata identical, every block other than the comments, pictures and padding identical,
    every comment of a key the editor does not manage identical and in order, the vendor string
    kept; `flac-full` keeps its two pictures (the front one replaced), `cd.flac` goes from one
    to none. `ffprobe` still reads album_artist, disc, comment and REPLAYGAIN_TRACK_GAIN.
  - A reopen from `.meta/` requests no `.wasm` (nor within 5 s), and shows the saved titles.
- **Memory** (E3-D6), the same module in Node (`.tmp/scripts/e3-memory.mts`): reading grows the
  module's memory to about the file's size (67.8 MiB after a 70 MB file, 284.7 MiB after a
  150 MB one), and saving to about three times it (134.6 MiB after saving the 70 MB file,
  434.8 MiB after the 150 MB one), which the page keeps until it is reloaded, since wasm memory
  never shrinks. Times: reads 43–45 ms, saves 255 ms (70 MB) and 389 ms (150 MB). It works; on
  a phone, or in a library of large hi-res files, that memory is the cost.

### Left to the owner

1. **Memory** (E3-D6): keep it as it is, or a follow-up that hands the module only the head of
   a file to read (the tags and metadata blocks are at its start), so reading and hashing a
   library no longer grows the module to the largest file; saves still need the whole file.
   That needs the engine to read a prefix and to compute the region from a prefix, a tail and a
   length. Recommended before the phones' Player (E6), not urgent on a computer.
2. **Several values of one key in FLAC.** `flac-full` has two ARTIST comments (One, Two). The
   engine shows the first ("One"), and an edit to "Someone" replaces that one only: the file
   ends with `ARTIST=Someone` and `ARTIST=Two`, which other players show as "Someone; Two".
   That is E1's rule ("only the fields that changed are written, and in place: the first value of
   a key changes, the others stay", 2026-11-04 18:30), which keeps what jackbird does not show.
   An MP3's several values, though, read joined ("One/Two"). Whether FLAC should read joined too,
   and an edit replace every value of the key, is the owner's call; it would change E1's rule
   for that case.
3. By hand, on a real computer: a FLAC library from a real disk (the system's MIME type for
   `.flac`, which this container cannot show), and playback through speakers.

### Next

By the roadmap: E4, the Tauri v2 desktop app, awaiting the owner's go.

Suggested commit message: `feat: FLAC libraries in the web app; Mp3Track becomes Track; E3`.

## Extension — 2026-11-08 03:09 — Correction: "No I/O" in E-D1 was Claude's proposal, never put to the owner

**Summary**: A correction of provenance, at the owner's request (*"No entiendo lo de 'bytes dentro,
bytes fuera, sin I/O', no es algo que haya decidido yo"*, then *"añade la corrección"*). The rule
that the tag crates take a whole file as bytes and give a whole file back was never the owner's
decision. Nothing earlier in this record is changed; this extension sets the record straight.

### What happened

- 2026-08-11 20:34 (this record's proposal, by Claude): the target architecture described
  `tags/` as "read/write tags and artwork over bytes" and `core/` as "No I/O".
- 2026-11-04 12:44: the owner approved lofty and Tauri v2 (*"OK, let's go with your
  recommendations of lofty and Tauri v2. Write the ROADMAP and RECORD"*). Writing that re-plan,
  Claude put "over bytes … No I/O" into E-D1, with D5 as its reason. D5 asks for one engine giving
  the same bytes on every platform; it does not ask for a whole file in and a whole file out.
  That part of E-D1 was not put to the owner as a decision of its own.
- E1 (2026-11-04 22:12 onwards) wrote it into `AGENTS.md` as a rule of the crates
  ("They do no I/O (bytes in, bytes out)"), and E2 and E3 built on it.
- 2026-11-08, in the conversation: Claude called it "decisión E-D1" as if the owner had made it.
  It was a design choice of Claude's, presented inside a table the owner approved for other
  reasons.

### Its consequence

A whole file goes into the WebAssembly module to be read, and two whole files are in it to save
(E3's measure: 151 MiB after reading a 150 MB file, 301 MiB after saving it, kept until the page
reloads, since wasm memory never shrinks). The reference libraries (lofty 0.25.5's `splice`,
rust-id3 1.17.2's `PlainStorage`, read in this container) read only the tag, and write it in
place, shifting the rest of the file in fixed-size chunks when its size changes.

### The owner's decision (2026-11-08)

Of three options (a range- and splice-based engine that still does no I/O; crates with real file
I/O, as lofty; leaving it), the owner chose the first: *"Opción 1"*. Its plan follows.

## Extension — 2026-11-08 03:10 — E2b, the engine by ranges and splices: implementation plan

**Summary**: The plan for the owner's option 1 (03:09): the tag crates still open no file, but they
ask for the parts of a file they need (its head and its last bytes) and give back a splice
(replace the file's first N bytes with these), not a whole file. The audio never enters the
WebAssembly module, so its memory follows the size of the tags (a few KB, a few MB with a large
cover), not the file. The same interface serves E4, where the desktop app reads those ranges from
disk and writes the splice in place. Called E2b in the roadmap, before E4. Two questions are left
to the owner. Nothing is implemented until it is approved.

### The interface (`jackbird-tags`)

```rust
/// Where a file's tags end and its audio is, from its first bytes, its last ones and its length.
pub fn layout(head: &[u8], tail: &[u8], len: u64) -> Result<Layout, Error>;
pub enum Layout { Known(FileLayout), NeedHead(usize) }   // NeedHead: give me at least this many first bytes
pub struct FileLayout {
    pub format: Format,
    pub tags_end: u64,        // the first byte after the tags: what a save replaces is 0..tags_end
    pub audio: Range<u64>,    // the audio region the checksum hashes (today's definition, unchanged)
}
/// The six fields and the cover, from the file's first bytes (at least `head_len`, below).
pub fn read(head: &[u8], layout: &FileLayout) -> Result<Tags, Error>;
/// What a save writes: the file's first `tags_end` bytes replaced with `with`; the rest unchanged.
pub fn write(head: &[u8], layout: &FileLayout, edit: &Edit) -> Result<Splice, Error>;
pub struct Splice { pub replace: Range<u64>, pub with: Vec<u8> }
```

- **How much head**: MP3 with ID3v2, the tag's size from its first 10 bytes (and its footer);
  MP3 without one, the first 64 KiB (where the frame sync is looked for today), and `tags_end`
  is 0, so a save inserts a tag; FLAC, block header by block header up to the last one, after an
  optional ID3v2 prefix (kept, byte for byte, inside what is replaced). Each step that runs past
  the bytes given answers `NeedHead(n)`; the caller gives more and asks again. Starting with
  64 KiB, a file is known in one step, or two when its cover is large.
- **The tail**: the last 160 bytes, for ID3v1 (128) and an APEv2 footer (32), which the audio
  region leaves out, as today. They are never rewritten: a save replaces only the head.
- **The safeguards**, on the head: (1) the new head reads back as asked; (3) every frame, block
  and comment the edit does not touch is in it, byte for byte, in order; (2), the audio, becomes
  structural: the new head must end exactly where its own layout says the audio starts (its tag
  size, or its last block), so the source's bytes from `tags_end` on follow it unchanged. The
  caller adds the end-to-end check (below).
- **Whole-file functions stay**, built on these (`read_file`, `write_file`, `audio_region` over
  a whole `&[u8]`), with the current three safeguards: the tests, the fixtures and E3's tools
  use them, and a new test checks, on every fixture and edit, that the head-and-splice path gives
  the same file, byte for byte, as the whole-file one.

### The module (`crates/tags-wasm`)

Exports `tags_layout` (input: the length, the tail, the head; output: the layout, or how many
head bytes it needs), `tags_read` (the head) and `tags_write` (the edit and the head; output: the
splice). `tags_region` goes, folded into `tags_layout`. The wire format grows those messages
(`wire.rs`, mirrored by `src/helpers/tagwire.ts`).

### The web app (`src/lib/tags/`)

- **`readTags(bytes)`** and **`checksums(bytes)`**: the scan and the verifier still read the
  whole file, since the full checksum hashes all of it (WebCrypto has no streaming SHA-1); that
  copy lives in JavaScript and is collected after. Only `subarray`s of it go to the module: the
  tail and a head that grows on `NeedHead`. The layout found is kept for both calls on one file.
- **`writeTags(bytes, previous, metadata)`**: the module gives the splice; the app builds the new
  file (`with` followed by `bytes.subarray(tags_end)`), and, **before writing**, checks end to end
  that the new file's audio region hashes as the source's did (the hash is computed anyway); if
  not, nothing is written and the save says `refused-audio`. Then the VFS writes it, as today.
- **Memory**: the module holds the head and the new head; JavaScript holds the source and the
  new file for the length of a save, then collects them.

### Not in this step

- Writing by ranges in the browser (the new head, then the source's own bytes streamed from disk,
  skipping the VFS's whole-file `write`): the VFS has `readRange` and `readStream`, but no
  splice-write, and the File System Access API writes through a swap file anyway. Question 1.
- A streaming SHA-1, which would let the scan stop reading whole files: WebCrypto has none, and
  one in wasm is some 3× slower (E0: 51 ms against 15 ms for 13 MB). Deferred.
- The desktop app's in-place writes: E4, over this same interface.

### Docs and rules

`AGENTS.md`: "They do no I/O (bytes in, bytes out)" becomes "They do no I/O: they are given the
ranges of a file they ask for (its head and its last bytes) and return what to write (a splice);
the audio never passes through them." `docs/architecture.md`, `STACK.md`; the roadmap gains E2b
before E4.

### Tests and checks

- `cargo test`: `layout` on every fixture (its `tags_end` and region against `cases.json`), with
  heads cut at every block or tag boundary and one byte short of it (`NeedHead` asks for the
  right size); the head-and-splice path equal to the whole-file one on every fixture and the
  E1 edits (a title, every field, a cover, a cover removed, emptied fields); the structural
  safeguard refusing a head that ends early or late.
- `npm test`: the fixtures through the module by the new path; `tagwire.ts` at the 90% floor.
- By hand: the module's memory after reading and saving the 150 MB file of E3 (expected: about
  the size of its tags, a couple of MiB), and with a 4 MB cover; E3's Chromium saves again (a
  title, every field and a cover, a cover removed, the 70 MB 24/96 file), each saved file with the
  same audio MD5 under `ffmpeg` and its unedited blocks and comments kept.

### Steps (estimates)

1. `layout`, head-based `read` and `write`, `Splice`, the structural safeguard, the whole-file
   functions on top; tests (1 d).
2. The module's exports and wire format (0.5 d).
3. `src/lib/tags/` on the new path; the end-to-end check before writing (0.5 d).
4. The checks by hand, the memory measure, the docs and rules, the walkthrough, the roadmap (0.5 d).

### Questions for the owner

1. Writing in the browser: keep the VFS's whole-file `write` (recommended now: simple, and the
   JavaScript copies are collected after a save), or write by ranges, which needs a splice-write
   in `@cloudauthn/vfs-sync` first?
2. The first head asked for: 64 KiB (recommended: the MP3 sync search needs it anyway, and most
   tags without a large cover fit), or the size of the tags in `.meta/` when known?
