## Extension — 2026-11-09 20:01 — E5 and E6: the owner's first answers; the output modes explained; OpenSubsonic's state

**Summary**: The owner answered three of the six questions of 19:37 and asked about the other
three. Answered: **macOS first** for the bit-perfect check by hand (the DAC can move later);
**gapless in E5**; **E5, then E6**. Asked: whether the output choice is in the app (it is: both
outputs are in the desktop app, the question is which one it starts with), what the modes are and
whether quality changes, and about OpenSubsonic, which the owner saw as not updated since 2023.
Questions 2, 3 and 5 stay open until the owner answers them.

### The output modes, on macOS

| Mode | What reaches the DAC | Other apps' sound |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Webview** (`<audio>`, today) | every track resampled to the rate the device is set to (Audio MIDI Setup; often 48 kHz); a 96 kHz file is not 96 kHz at the DAC | mixed in |
| **Native, shared** | the device switched to each track's rate, so no resampling; the samples untouched while nothing else sounds and the volume is at 100% or done by the DAC itself (USB DACs with their own volume) | mixed in when they sound (a notification alters those moments) |
| **Native, exclusive** (hog mode) | the same, and guaranteed: nothing else is mixed in, no other app can change the rate | silent while jackbird plays |

Measured quality: the step that changes the signal is resampling, which both native modes remove.
Between shared and exclusive the samples are the same while nothing else plays; exclusive adds the
guarantee. Whether resampling done well can be heard is disputed; bit-perfect is checkable, not
audible by definition. Proposed now: Native **shared** as the default of the native output, with
exclusive as an option (this replaces 19:37's recommendation for question 3).

### OpenSubsonic, checked today

- The **original Subsonic API** (subsonic.org, 1.16.1) is frozen: that is probably what looked
  abandoned.
- **OpenSubsonic** (github.com/opensubsonic/open-subsonic-api) extends it and is active: its last
  change is of 2026-11-09 (`songLyrics` v2), with others in November, October, August and July
  2026 (`topSongsByArtistId`, `ArtistID3.disambiguation`, a revision of authentication).
  Its version number stays at 1 by design: extensions are optional and announced by
  `getOpenSubsonicExtensions`, so the spec grows without new versions.
- Servers listed: Navidrome, gonic, LMS, Ampache, Nextcloud Music, Supysonic and others. Clients:
  Symfonium, Amperfy (iOS), Tempus (Android), Feishin, Supersonic, Airsonic Refix and others.
- **What it means for jackbird**: the classic Subsonic sign-in sends a password (or a hash of it
  with a salt), and jackbird has no passwords (codes and passkeys, S3). OpenSubsonic's
  `apiKeyAuthentication` extension fits: a key per person and app, created and revoked in the
  people dialog. Which clients already take API keys is for the spike to check; where they do
  not, an app password generated by jackbird, revocable, is the fallback.
- A minimal server is about a dozen endpoints (`ping`, `getLicense`, `getMusicFolders`,
  `getArtists`, `getArtist`, `getAlbum`, `getSong`, `search3`, `stream`, `getCoverArt`,
  `star`/`unstar`, `scrobble`, playlists), all over F1's catalogue.

## Extension — 2026-11-09 20:40 — E5, native output: walkthrough (Linux verified here; macOS, Windows and ALSA's loopback in CI)

**Summary**: The desktop app plays its folders' songs through Rust: decoded at their own rate and
depth, sent to the device in shared mode by default, or exclusive, or through the webview as
before (embedded), chosen in the preferences. The next song follows without a gap when it has the
same format. What reaches the device is shown on the display ("24/96 bit-perfect") and in the
preferences. Here, on Linux with no sound card, a 24/96 FLAC played through ALSA's default device
(pointed at a file) arrives sample for sample; in CI the same runs, plus ALSA's loopback card in
exclusive mode, the crate's tests on macOS and Windows, and the desktop check playing natively.
The owner's check by hand on macOS with the DAC is what remains.

### What was built

- **`crates/audio`** (new), beside the tag crates:
  - `Source`: `symphonia` 0.6 (FLAC, MP3), read from disk as it plays; samples as integers at the
    file's depth (MP3 at 24 bits); sample-accurate seeking; FLAC's STREAMINFO MD5 exposed.
  - `Pipe`: the samples decoded ahead (0.5 s), where each song starts in them, the frames the
    device took (the position), pause, and the last 2,048 frames of the mono mix for the spectrum.
  - `Player`: a thread with the queue's two songs (the one playing, the next). The next song is
    decoded into the same pipe when its format is the same (gapless, E5-D7); another format
    reopens the device once the first has played. Events: `started` (also for a song that began
    by itself), `ended`, `failed`. It decodes ahead *before* opening the device, so a song starts
    with itself, not with silence (found here: ALSA's file plugin showed the first frames empty).
  - `Spectrum`: Web Audio's analyser over what the device took (Blackman window, 1,024 points,
    smoothing 0.8, -100 to -30 dB, 0-255), with a radix-2 FFT written here (E5-D5).
  - `DeviceOutput` (feature `device`): `cpal` 0.18 for shared mode everywhere. On macOS `cpal`
    sets the device's rate and physical format to the song's, and exclusive is hog mode through
    `coreaudio-rs`'s safe helpers, taken before the stream and given back after. On Linux,
    exclusive opens a `hw:` device directly. On Windows, exclusive is WASAPI's exclusive mode with
    the `wasapi` crate (24-in-32, 32 or 16 bits, whichever the device takes first for the song's
    depth). There is no `unsafe` code in the crate.
  - `CaptureOutput`: what a device would be given, as bytes, for the tests.
  - The route says how faithful the way is: `bit-perfect` (exclusive, the song's rate, a format
    that carries its depth), `mixer` (shared: untouched while nothing else sounds and the volume
    is at its top) or `converted`.
- **`src-tauri/src/audio.rs`**: commands `audio_devices`, `audio_configure`, `audio_load`,
  `audio_next`, `audio_play`, `audio_pause`, `audio_seek`, `audio_stop`, `audio_status` and
  `audio_spectrum`; the player's events go to the interface as `audio` events. Files are named
  by root and path, as every other command: the app's own folders, and the folders of its own
  service (S2), on the same machine.
- **The interface**:
  - `src/lib/native-audio.ts`: the native player shaped like the `<audio>` element.
  - `src/lib/player.ts`: drives either output. The loaded track's element is chosen per track:
    native for a desktop folder's or the local service's track unless *Embedded* is chosen. The
    next track is handed to the native player for gapless playback; when it starts by itself the
    queue moves on to it without reloading. A track the native player cannot play (no device, a
    rate the device does not take) continues in the embedded output where it was, and the
    preferences say why.
  - `src/lib/output.ts` and `src/helpers/output.ts`: the choice (native shared by default; native
    exclusive; embedded) and the device, kept between visits.
  - The preferences: *Output* and *Device*, and what plays now ("Now: FLAC 24 bit · 96 kHz → USB DAC
    · 96 kHz · bit-perfect"). The display: "24/96 bit-perfect" before the time.
  - Switching between native and embedded while a song plays goes on from the same second.
  - The family library's songs stay embedded: they are put together as they leave (F1's head
    and blob).

### Checks

- `cargo test -p jackbird-audio` (no devices, 15 tests):
  - The three FLAC fixtures (24/96 twice, 16/44.1) decode to the PCM whose MD5 is in their
    STREAMINFO and that `ffmpeg` decodes; MP3 to its length without the encoder's delay and padding.
  - Through the player and the capture output, a 24/96 FLAC reaches the "device" byte for byte;
    two songs of the same format arrive in one opening, sample after sample.
  - Another format reopens at its rate; a 16-bit device is said to convert; floats carry 24 bits
    exactly.
  - Also covered: seeking, pausing, replacing the next song after it was decoded ahead, a missing
    file, and the spectrum's peak at the tones played.
- `tests/loopback.rs` (feature `device`, Linux, each when its variable is set):
  - ALSA's default device pointed at a file (`.asoundrc`): the 24/96 FLAC is written sample for
    sample. Verified here.
  - ALSA's loopback card: played exclusively to `hw:Loopback,0`, recorded from `hw:Loopback,1`,
    compared sample for sample. CI only: this container has no kernel modules.
- `npm test`: `helpers/output.ts` covered.
- The desktop check now also plays `hires-24-96.flac` natively. Here, under xvfb with ALSA's file
  device, it started (96 kHz, I24, shared, through the mixer), ended, and its spectrum moved.
  Without a device it fails with the system's reason, which the check accepts on macOS and
  Windows (runners have no sound card). On Linux CI the file device is set up, so it must play
  (`JACKBIRD_CHECK_NATIVE=required`).
- macOS and Windows code is checked here with `cargo clippy --target` (aarch64-apple-darwin,
  x86_64-pc-windows-gnu); `audio.yml` builds and tests it on the real systems.

### Left to the owner, by hand

On the Mac with the DAC, open a folder with a 24/96 (or higher) FLAC and check these:

- The preferences show "bit-perfect" in *Native, exclusive* and "through the system's mixer" in
  *Native, shared*.
- The DAC's own display shows 96 kHz.
- Audio MIDI Setup shows the device switched to 96 kHz while it plays.
- An album of the same rate plays without gaps.

**Summary**: For `59919d4`, the following are green:

- `audio.yml` ([run 42685636531](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/42685636531)):
  - `crates/audio` with its devices: lints and tests on macOS, Windows and Linux.

    as the check allows.

The first two runs failed. The fixes:

- Clippy 1.99's `chunks_exact_to_as_chunks` in the tests: fixed in `d268f96`; the toolchain here
  is now 1.99.1 as well.
- The loopback job asked for `linux-modules-extra-7.0.0-1017-azure`, which does not exist.

**Correction to the walkthrough**: the check through ALSA's loopback card (`snd-aloop`, exclusive
on `hw:Loopback`) **did not run**. The runner's Azure kernel neither carries the module nor has a
package for it. The step leaves a warning and skips. The exclusive paths (ALSA `hw:`, macOS's hog
mode, WASAPI's exclusive mode) are built and linted on their systems but have not played to a real
device. The owner's run on macOS with the DAC is the first that will.

## Extension — 2026-11-09 21:24 — E6's spike starts: how each question is answered

**Summary**: The owner said to go ahead (*"Ok, dale"*). E6's spike was approved at 19:37 with
19:55's answers: the phone as a client of the family instance first; local files only weighed;
OpenSubsonic weighed beside it. The four questions of E6-D1 are answered in this order, each
with something that runs:

1. **The app on the phone**:
   - `src-tauri` builds for Android and iOS. The desktop-only parts stay out of mobile builds:
     the service it carries, the native player, the local media server and the folder commands.
   - The interface asks for the instance's address the first time. From then on it is a client of
     the instance, as the web is, with S3's sign-in and a bearer token.
   - The service already answers the app's origins with CORS: Android's is
     `http://tauri.localhost` and iOS's is `tauri://localhost`.
2. **Checked in CI**: an unattended check built into a debug build (the instance's address given
   at build time).
   - On Android it runs in an emulator; the service on the runner is reached through
     `adb reverse`.
   - On iOS it runs in the simulator, which shares the host's network.
   - The check connects, lists the library, plays a song a few seconds, and posts its report to a
     small receiver on the runner.
3. **Background playback and the lock screen**: measured on top of (1).
4. **OpenSubsonic and local files**: last, then the record with the recommendation and E6b's plan.

## Extension — 2026-11-10 01:29 — E6's spike: the app runs on Android and iOS as a client of an instance (CI passes); background playback, first measurement

**Summary**: `mobile.yml` passes for `d8a3313`
([run 42726466105](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/42726466105)).

- **What the check does**: on Android 15's emulator and on iOS's simulator (iPhone 17 Pro), the
  app (a debug build made for the check) connects to a jackbird service on the runner, signs in
  as its owner (an open instance with one person), opens a folder of that machine, lists its five
  songs and plays a FLAC (16/44.1).
- **Android, in the background**: with the app sent home, the webview **pauses** the song. The
  position stays put for the ten seconds in the background, and the song goes on when the app
  comes back. Playing in the background on Android needs a foreground service with a media
  session; the webview alone stops.
- **iOS, in the background**: not measured yet. Launching Settings over the app never hid the
  page in the simulator, so the samples stayed visible throughout. Another way to send it to the
  background is needed.
- **OpenSubsonic**: the service's `/rest/` answers (`2672c93`, `docs/opensubsonic.md`) pass
  `service.yml`.

### What it took (fourteen runs)

Most failures were the check or the CI emulator, not the app.

- **The build**:
  - Tauri's mobile builds call `npm run tauri`, which `package.json` lacked.
  - On iOS, `tauri ios build --target aarch64-sim` is needed, not `xcodebuild` alone: Xcode's
    Rust phase asks the running Tauri command for its options.
  - The simulator needs `simctl bootstatus` before the first launch.
- **The check's player** asks for its media with CORS (`crossOrigin = "anonymous"`), as the app's
  player does. Without it, the instance refuses a cross-site request with no origin (403).
- **Android's emulator on GitHub's runners**:
  - On the `google_apis` image, a dying Play Services process took the app down.
  - On API 34 and 33 (AOSP), the whole emulator went away a few seconds into the app's run,
    whatever the app was doing. Ruled out: memory (over 10 GB free, nothing in the kernel's log),
    the GPU emulation (guest rendering), `adb reverse` (now the emulator reaches the runner at
    10.0.2.2 through a small proxy), and the sound card.
  - API 35 runs.
- **Kept for diagnosis**: the page's steps reported to the runner as it goes, a heartbeat, a
  30-second limit per step, the phone's log on failure, and screenshots as artifacts.

### Left in the spike

- Background playback on iOS (another way to background it) and the lock screen on both systems.
- Local files weighed in writing.
- An OpenSubsonic client tried against the instance.
- The record with the recommendation and E6b's plan.

## Extension — 2026-11-10 07:28 — The APK and the IPA on the release (proposal)

**Summary**: The owner does not see an APK or an IPA on the release. None is made: `release.yml`
calls `build.yml`, `desktop.yml`, `service.yml` and `ship.yml`, never `mobile.yml`, and
`mobile.yml` has no `workflow_call` and uploads only the check's screenshots. Its builds are made
for the check (debug, x86_64 for the emulator, the simulator's `aarch64-sim`, with
`VITE_JACKBIRD_PHONE_CHECK` baked in), so they would not install on a phone anyway. E6-D5 said
nothing of the spike ships; attaching the apps is a change to that decision, proposed here.

### What it takes on each system

- **Android**: a release APK for phones (`arm64-v8a` and `armeabi-v7a`, one universal APK; x86_64
  too for emulators), without the check's switch. Android installs only a signed APK, and an update
  installs over the old one only when both are signed with the same key. A debug key is made anew
  on each runner, so it would break every update: the release needs **one keystore**, kept as the
  repository's secrets (`ANDROID_KEYSTORE` in base64, `ANDROID_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD`,
  `ANDROID_KEY_ALIAS`, `ANDROID_KEY_PASSWORD`). The owner makes it once with `keytool`; it costs
  nothing. Losing it means the next APK installs only after uninstalling the app.
- **iOS**: an IPA installs on an iPhone only signed by Apple's chain.
  - With a **paid Apple Developer account** (99 USD a year): a distribution certificate and an ad
    hoc profile (devices registered by their UDID) as secrets, or TestFlight. A free account's
    certificates last 7 days and need Xcode on the owner's Mac: not usable in CI.
  - Without one: an **unsigned IPA**, which installs only through a tool that re-signs it with the
    person's own Apple ID (AltStore, Sideloadly), every 7 days with a free ID.

### Proposed

1. `mobile.yml` gains `workflow_call` (`ref`, and `release: true`). With `release`, beside the
   checks (unchanged), two jobs build for phones without `VITE_JACKBIRD_PHONE_CHECK`:
   - `tauri android build --apk --target aarch64 armv7 x86_64`, signed with the keystore from the
     secrets (Gradle's `signingConfigs` written by the job into `src-tauri/gen/android`, which is
     not committed); the job fails if a secret is missing, rather than ship an APK that cannot be
     updated. Uploaded as `mobile-android`: `jackbird-vX.Y.Z-android.apk`.
   - `tauri ios build --target aarch64` unsigned (`CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO`), the `.app` put in
     `Payload/` and zipped as `jackbird-vX.Y.Z-ios-unsigned.ipa`. Uploaded as `mobile-ios`.
2. `release.yml` calls it (`needs: version`), the release waits for it and attaches
   `mobile-*` beside the desktop bundles and the service's binaries.
3. The header comments of both workflows say so, and this record's walkthrough when it is done.

### Questions for the owner

1. **Ship the phone apps now**, while E6 is a spike (no background playback on Android yet), or
   wait for E6b?
2. **Android's key**: a keystore in the repository's secrets (recommended), or a debug-signed APK
   for now, knowing each release will need the app uninstalled first?
3. **iOS**: an unsigned IPA for AltStore or Sideloadly (recommended until there is an account), or
   a paid Apple Developer account with ad hoc signing?

## Extension — 2026-11-10 08:32 — E6's spike goes on: background playback, measured with sound

**Summary**: The owner said to go on (*"Sigamos"*). Next in the spike is E6-D1 (c): background
playback. Three findings from 01:29's run and Tauri's sources change how it is measured:

- **iOS did go to the background in that run** (a correction to 01:29): the page was hidden from
  26 s, and the song was **paused** at that moment, as on Android. Only three samples fell in the
  background, because Settings took 22 s to come up and the samples stopped at 30 s.
- **The check played muted**, which both webviews pause when hidden whatever the app does: a
  muted element is not "playing audio" to the system. Tauri builds both webviews with autoplay
  allowed (wry's `autoplay`, on by default: `setMediaPlaybackRequiresUserGesture(false)` on
  Android, `mediaTypesRequiringUserActionForPlayback = none` on iOS), so the check can play with
  sound.
- **What each system lacks**:
  - On iOS, the app has no `audio` background mode (`UIBackgroundModes`), without which iOS
    suspends any app that leaves the foreground. WebKit sets the audio session to *playback* by
    itself while a media element plays with sound.
  - On Android, Tauri's activity (wry's `WryActivity`) calls `WebView.onPause()` when the app
    leaves the foreground, and nothing keeps the process in the foreground (no foreground
    service), so Android may freeze it.

### This step

1. `src-tauri/Info.ios.plist`, which Tauri merges into the app's `Info.plist`: `UIBackgroundModes`
   = `audio`. `mobile.yml` prints the generated `Info.plist`'s background modes, to see that it
   was merged.
2. The check's background measurement plays **with sound** (muted only if the webview refuses,
   and the report says which), samples for 45 s instead of 30, and the app stays in the background
   for 15 s instead of 10.
3. Measured on both systems. If Android still pauses, the next step is the foreground service with
   a media session (a small Kotlin plugin), which E6-D1 (c) foresaw; if iOS still pauses, an
   `AVAudioSession` of category *playback* set by the app itself (Swift).

  that, whenever it happens (up to 90 s), instead of after a fixed time.

  page counts how much has played across the loops.

  after, since the page's timers may be held back while hidden) and how much played meanwhile.

## Extension — 2026-11-10 09:14 — Two minutes away: Android plays on; iOS suspends the page; no media session from the page on Android

**Summary**: `mobile.yml` passes for `68dcb46`
([run 42786645815](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/42786645815)).

| System | Away | Played meanwhile (as measured) | `navigator.mediaSession` | What the system says |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Android 15 (emulator) | 121.1 s | 117.9 s | **absent** (WebView, Chromium 124) | the process stayed in state 15 (*last activity*), never frozen; **no media session** of the app's |
| iOS 18.7 (simulator) | 138.1 s | 70.0 s (see below) | present | not asked |

- **Android plays on for two minutes**, but only as the last app used. Android protects that one;
  once another app is opened, the process becomes *cached* and is frozen, so a phone in use
  would stop the song. A **foreground service** keeps it, and it needs a notification, which the
  media session's controls go in. WebView does not give the page `navigator.mediaSession`, so
  the lock screen's and the notification's controls must be made natively (a Kotlin plugin,
  Android's `MediaSession` driven by the page's player). E6-D1 (c) had foreseen this.
- **iOS suspended the page's JavaScript while the app was away**: not one sample between 51.3 s
  (hidden) and 187.3 s (shown again), where 01:29's run, 18 s away, still had one every ~2 s.
  The count read 120.096 s, exactly two passes of the 60 s file, because the loop's wraps
  could not be counted while the page was suspended. So it cannot say whether the song went on
  to its end and stopped, or kept looping.
  - Either way, what matters for a player follows: **with the page suspended, nothing of the page
    runs in the background on iOS**. The next song in the queue would not be loaded, and the
    played counts would not be sent, until the app comes back. If that holds on a phone, playback
    on iOS has to move out of the page for a queue to go on: AVPlayer, or AVQueuePlayer fed with
    the queue's next songs.

### Changed for the next run

- The silence is five minutes long (`fixtures/audio/silence-300s.flac`, 60 KB, replacing
  `silence-60s.flac`; the other fixtures came out byte-identical from `make.mjs`), with no loop.
  It is longer than any stay away, so the position read on return says how far it went.

played the 20 s silence as a queue does (on `ended`, the next media URL from the instance, then

Correction to 09:30: "06:09's suspension" there means 09:14's.

| System | Away | Played meanwhile | Changes of song while hidden |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Android 15 (emulator) | 120.5 s | 37.0 s | the first song ended at 20 s, and the change **never finished**: no URL, no `play()` settled, and still paused 2 s after the app was back |

**Summary**: `mobile.yml` passes for `2337f7c`
([run 42795309288](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/42795309288)). With the
proxy's log and one change of song in the foreground first:

| System | Change | Ended (hidden?) | URL | `play()` | What reached the instance |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Android | 1 | 20.1 s (shown) | 20.6 s | 20.6 s | the `stat`, 200 in 2 ms |
| Android | 2 | 41.9 s (**hidden** since 26 s) | **never** | — | **nothing**, while hidden or in the 9 s after the app was back |
| iOS | 1–3 | 20.2, 40.6, 62.5 s (shown) | +0.1 to +0.9 s | +0.2 to +1.5 s | — |
| iOS | 4 | 84.6 s (**hidden** since 80 s) | 84.7 s | 86.9 s | — |

After change 4, iOS **suspended the page at 97 s, while that song was playing**, and resumed it
only when the app was back, at 240 s (the simulator took 54 s to put Settings in front, and 2.5 min
to bring the app back).

### What the spike has found about background playback (E6-D1 c)

1. **A song that has started plays to its end** in the background on both phones, with sound.
   That takes, on iOS, the `audio` background mode (`src-tauri/Info.ios.plist`, now in the app).
2. **The page cannot start the next one** reliably:
   - **Android's WebView holds back the page's requests while the app is in the background**: the
     next song's `stat` never left the phone. The page's JavaScript still runs (timers every 1 to
     48 s). Asking for the next song's URL ahead of time might get around this, but nothing else
     would keep going: the app has no foreground service, so Android freezes it once it is no longer
     the last app used (09:14), and it has no media session.
   - **iOS suspends the page in the background, sooner or later**: 5 s into a gap between songs
     (09:59), or 17 s into a song (this run), or never in two minutes (09:30). While it is
     suspended no `ended` handler runs, so nothing done ahead of time helps.
3. **The lock screen and the notification**: Android's WebView gives the page no
   `navigator.mediaSession`, and the app has no media session (09:14). iOS's WKWebView has
   `navigator.mediaSession`. Whether its controls show on the lock screen was not seen (the
   simulator's lock screen is not captured here), and they would not help while the page is
   suspended.

### Recommendation for E6b (to be approved with E6b's plan)

**Playback on the phones is native, with the queue handed to it; the page drives it and shows it**:

- **Android**: Media3 (ExoPlayer) in a `MediaSessionService`: the foreground service, its
  notification, the lock screen's and the headset's controls, and the queue it plays through by
  itself. A Tauri plugin in Kotlin.
- **iOS**: `AVQueuePlayer` with the queue's next songs given ahead, `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` and
  `MPRemoteCommandCenter` for the lock screen. A Tauri plugin in Swift.
- **In the page**, a third *output* beside E5-D4's two (embedded and the desktop's native): load
  a queue (the media URLs, with tokens long enough for the queue), play, pause, seek, next, and
  events for the position, the song that is playing, and its end. The played counts (F3) are sent
  from what the output reports when the app is back, since the page may be suspended meanwhile.
- **The media tokens** must outlast the queue they are handed with, or the native player must
  ask for new ones itself: a point for E6b's plan, with S3's token lifetimes.

### Left in the spike

- Local files, weighed in writing (E6-D1 d).
- An OpenSubsonic client tried against the instance (E6-D4).
- The spike's closing record: the recommendation above with the rest, and E6b's plan.

## Extension — 2026-11-10 12:29 — E6-D4: an outside OpenSubsonic client against the instance; E6-D1 (d): local files, weighed

**Summary**: The owner said to go on (*"ok, sigue"*). Two of the spike's three remaining points.

### E6-D4: an outside client (`scripts/opensubsonic-client-check.py`)

**The client**: [py-opensonic](https://github.com/khers/py-opensonic) 10.4.1, a Python client of the
OpenSubsonic API written by others. Its calls read our answers into its own types, so an answer it
cannot read fails the check. It is a library, not an app on a phone. An app in the emulator would
have to be driven through its screens by `adb`, which is fragile for an unattended check and
says no more about the API.

**The check**: it starts with an open instance on the runner. It imports a folder of the tag
fixtures into the library and makes the owner a key, both through jackbird's own API. Then it
runs, through the client:

- **Sign-in**: `apiKey`; the key as a "legacy" password (`p=enc:…`, as the apps that only ask for
  a user and a password send it); a salted token refused with error 41.
- **Browsing**: `getLicense`, `getOpenSubsonicExtensions`, `getArtists`, `getArtist`, `getAlbum`,
  `getSong`, `getAlbumList2` (three kinds), `getRandomSongs`, `getGenres`, `search3` with an empty
  query, `getIndexes`, `getPlaylists`.
- **Playing**: `stream` (whole, and a range: 206), `getCoverArt`.
- **One's own**: `star`, `getStarred2`, `unstar`, `scrobble`.
- An album through the legacy sign-in.

**Results** (run here against the service built from this branch; added to `service.yml` on
Linux):

- **All pass**, with one exception that is the client's bug, not ours: py-opensonic reads
  `getMusicFolders`' `musicFolders` as the list itself, where the specification nests it as
  `musicFolders.musicFolder`, as jackbird answers. The check reads that answer's shape directly
  instead.
- The synchronous form of the client cannot read binary answers (it hands back aiohttp's
  response), so `stream` and `getCoverArt` are asked by plain HTTP with the same key.
- Noticed on the way: `serverVersion` answers `0.1.0` while `/api/info` answers `0.1.12`. The
  first comes from the Cargo workspace's version, which `release.yml` does not bump (it bumps
  `package.json`). Not changed here: it touches the release.
- **Which apps sign in this way** was not checked against each app. From the specification and
  servers' documentation (LMS), the apps without an API key field take the key as the password,
  and those that fall back to the plain password after error 41 do so too. An app that only sends
  salted tokens cannot sign in. That is said in `docs/opensubsonic.md`, and stays to be seen app by
  app on a phone.

### E6-D1 (d): local files on the phone, weighed

| | Android | iOS |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Picking a folder** | the Storage Access Framework (`ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT_TREE`), with a permission kept across restarts | `UIDocumentPickerViewController` for a folder, kept as a security-scoped bookmark |
| **What `crates/core` gets** | `content://` URIs, not paths: listing goes through the `ContentResolver`, one query per folder, slow on large trees (seconds to tens of seconds for thousands of files, by others' reports; not measured here). Reading and writing go through a file descriptor (`openFileDescriptor`, "r" or "rw"), which Rust can use as a `File` | a real path while the access is held (`startAccessingSecurityScopedResource`): `crates/core` works as it does on a computer. Files in iCloud Drive may be placeholders, to be downloaded first (`NSFileCoordinator`) |
| **Renaming, creating** | `DocumentsContract` (`renameDocument`, `createDocument`), not the file system | the file system, within the folder |
| **The shortcut** | "All files access" (`MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`) gives real paths under `/storage/emulated/0`. Google Play allows it only to a few kinds of apps (file managers, backup), but **an APK installed from the release is not bound by that**. With it, `crates/core` works on paths, as on a computer | the app's own folder, shown in the Files app (`UIFileSharingEnabled`, `LSSupportsOpeningDocumentsInPlace`), where music is copied from a computer or Files: real paths, no picker |
| **Tauri today** | `tauri-plugin-dialog` picks files, **not folders**, on the phones; on iOS it copies each picked file into the app's cache. Either system's way would be a plugin of our own (Kotlin, Swift) | |

**Assessment**:

- Local files are possible on both phones, at the cost of a native plugin per system.
- On Android, "All files access" for the APK, or SAF for a store build. On iOS, a folder of the
  user's with a bookmark, or the app's own folder.
- None of this is needed for the client of an instance, which the owner put first (19:55). It
  also brings nothing to the native playback that 10:16 found necessary: the native players play
  `file://` paths as readily as the instance's URLs, so local files can come later on the same
  player.
- **Recommended**: not in E6b. Weigh it again once the phone plays from the instance, with the
  owner's real use (a phone with music on it, or always the instance).

### Left in the spike

The closing record: the recommendation, and E6b's plan for the owner to approve.

| (b) A client of the instance | It connects to an instance by its address, signs in, opens a folder of it, lists and plays a FLAC. | 01:29 |
| (c) Background playback, lock screen | A started song plays to its end in the background (iOS with the `audio` background mode). **A queue does not go on**: Android's WebView holds back the page's requests, and iOS suspends the page. | (d) Local files | Possible on both, each with a plugin of its own (SAF or "All files access"; a bookmarked folder or the app's own folder). Not needed for the client. | 12:29 |
| E6-D3 Offline songs | **Weighed, not measured.** `/api/library/download` gives a song with the family's tags, and media URLs take ranges, so a song can be fetched whole or resumed. Where it is kept: the app's own storage on both systems (Android's app-specific files; iOS's *Application Support*, not *Caches*, which iOS may empty). The native players read local files as readily as URLs, so offline songs are a list of downloaded files the queue prefers. How much a phone may keep is the phone's free space; the app sets its own limit. | here |
| E6-D4 OpenSubsonic | The service answers it (`docs/opensubsonic.md`), and an outside client (py-opensonic) reads it all, signing in with a key or the key as a password. | 12:29 |

### Recommendation

1. **Our own app, as a client of the instance**, with **native playback** on each phone, driven by
   the page. The interface stays the web app, as on the desktop.
2. **OpenSubsonic stays as it is**: an open door for the apps that already do the phone's work
   (Symfonium, Amperfy, Tempus), for whoever prefers them. It is already built and costs little to
   keep. It does not replace our app, which edits tags, renames and imports, as no other app does.
3. **Local files later**, on the same native player, once the owner's use asks for them.
4. **Offline songs in E6b**: the native player makes them cheap (a downloaded file is one more
   source for the queue). Without them, a phone away from home without coverage plays nothing.

### E6b: decisions (proposed)

| # | Proposal | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| E6b-D1 | **A native player plugin per phone**, in `src-tauri` (Tauri's mobile plugin API). **Android**: Media3 ExoPlayer in a `MediaSessionService`: the foreground service, its notification, the lock screen's and the headset's controls. **iOS**: `AVQueuePlayer`, the audio session of category *playback*, `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` and `MPRemoteCommandCenter`. Both take **the queue** (URLs, titles, artists, cover URLs), not one song, and play through it by themselves. | 10:15: the page cannot keep a queue going in the background |
| E6b-D2 | **The phone's output in the page**, a third beside E5's two: the player (`src/lib/player.ts`) hands it the queue and the position, and asks play, pause, seek, next, previous. It listens for the song playing, the position, its end, and errors. On a phone it is the only output; embedded `<audio>` stays the fallback if the plugin fails. | E5-D4's interface, extended to a queue |
| E6b-D3 | **The played counts (F3) and the position** are reported by the native player as they happen, kept by it while the page is suspended, and handed to the page when it is back, which sends them to the instance. The queue and the position also survive the app being closed. | the page may be suspended for minutes (10:15) |
| E6b-D4 | **Media tokens for a queue**: today a media token lasts 12 h (`MEDIA_SECONDS`), which covers a long queue. When one runs out, the native player asks the page for new URLs when it is back; if the page is suspended, it stops at that song and says why. A token that never expires is not proposed. | S3's tokens kept as they are |
| E6b-D5 | **Offline songs**: "Keep on this phone" on a song, an album or a playlist downloads it through `/api/library/download` into the app's own storage, with a limit in the settings. The queue plays the kept file when there is one, the instance's URL otherwise. A list in the settings shows what is kept and its size, and frees it. | E6-D3 weighed above |
| E6b-D6 | **The instance's address**: asked the first time, as now; then **sign-in through the system browser** and back to the app by a link of its own (S3's plan for the app), since passkeys in the webviews are uneven. Until that is built, the code by email in the app. | S3, 2026-11-04 |
| E6b-D7 | **Checks in CI**: `mobile.yml`'s check grows to the native player. It plays a queue of three 20 s songs with the app in the background for two minutes and checks that every change of song happened while hidden (the measurement of 09:44, now required). It checks the media session on Android (`dumpsys media_session`: the app's session, playing) and the now-playing info on iOS. **By hand, the owner**: the lock screen and a headset on a real phone of each. | the spike's harness, now a test |
| E6b-D8 | **Builds for phones on the release** (07:28's plan, held until E6b): a signed APK and, per the owner's answer, an unsigned IPA or a signed one. The questions 2 and 3 of 07:28 come back below. | the owner's question that started this session |

Local files on the phone; CarPlay and Android Auto; casting (Chromecast, AirPlay to other devices);
downloads in the background while the app is closed (they go on while it is open); the stores

## Extension — 2026-11-11 19:58 — E6b approved: the owner's answers, Android's key, and step 1's plan

**Summary**: The owner approved E6b (*"sigamos"*) and answered 12:37's five questions. This
extension takes the answers in, says how the owner makes Android's key, and details step 1 before
it is built.

### The owner's answers

1. **Offline songs are in E6b** (E6b-D5 stands).
2. **Android first**, then iOS (the steps' order of 12:37 stands).
3. **Android's key**: a keystore in the repository's secrets; the owner asked how to make it (below).
   It is needed only at step 5 (the builds on the release).
4. **iOS**: an **unsigned IPA** for now (AltStore, Sideloadly).
5. **Phones checked by hand**: a **Pixel 10**, a **CMF Phone 2 Pro** and a **OnePlus 9 Pro**; later
   an **iPhone XS**. Their system versions are to be noted when checked (the Pixel 10 ships with
   Android 16; the CMF Phone 2 Pro with Nothing OS on Android 15; the OnePlus 9 Pro's last major
   update was Android 14; the iPhone XS's last iOS is 18).
   - Nothing's and OnePlus's systems are known for stopping apps in the background more readily
     than Pixel's: the foreground service (E6b-D1) is what keeps the player alive there, and these
     two phones are the check of it. If they still stop it, the app may have to ask to be left out
     of the battery optimisation (`REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS`), decided then.
   - The iPhone XS (A12) runs iOS 18, the version CI's simulator runs.

### Android's key, for the owner

Made once, on any computer with a JDK (`keytool` comes with it; Android Studio carries one in
`jbr/bin/`):

```bash
keytool -genkeypair -v -keystore jackbird-release.jks -alias jackbird \
  -keyalg RSA -keysize 4096 -validity 36500 -storetype PKCS12
```

It asks for a password (PKCS12 uses the same one for the store and the key) and a name, which may
be anything. Then four secrets in GitHub (*Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → New
repository secret*):

| Secret | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `ANDROID_KEYSTORE` | the file in base64: `base64 -w0 jackbird-release.jks` (Linux), `base64 -i jackbird-release.jks` (macOS) |
| `ANDROID_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD` | the password |
| `ANDROID_KEY_ALIAS` | `jackbird` |
| `ANDROID_KEY_PASSWORD` | the same password |

The `.jks` file and its password are kept somewhere safe besides GitHub (a password manager): a
secret cannot be read back, and without the key, an APK signed with a new one installs only after
the app is uninstalled. The file is never committed.

### Step 1's plan: Android's native player

**1a. The plugin and the queue in the background** (this extension's first change):

- `crates/player`: a Tauri plugin crate (`tauri-plugin-jackbird-player`, the plugin
  `jackbird-player`), registered by the app on phones only. Its Rust side only registers the
  Kotlin class; the page calls its commands directly (`plugin:jackbird-player|…`, which Tauri
  hands to the Kotlin plugin), allowed by a capability for Android and iOS
  (`src-tauri/capabilities/phone.json`). On the desktop it builds and does nothing.
- `crates/player/android`: a Gradle library in Kotlin.
  - `PlayerService`, a Media3 `MediaSessionService` with an ExoPlayer: the foreground service
    (`mediaPlayback`), its notification, the lock screen's and the headset's controls, all
    Media3's own.
  - `PlayerPlugin`, the Tauri plugin, which drives it through a `MediaController`.
  - Commands: `set_queue` (the songs: a key, a media URL, title, artist, album, cover; the one to
    start at, from which second, playing or not), `play`, `pause`, `seek`, `skip_to`, `stop`,
    `status` (the key and index playing, whether it plays, the position and the duration).
  - Events: `state` (on every change: what plays and whether), `advance` (the next song started by
    itself), `ended` (the queue's end), `failed` (a song it cannot play).
- `src/lib/phone-audio.ts`: the page's side, shaped like `NativeAudio` (E5): the element's words
  (`src`, `paused`, `currentTime`, `duration`, `play`, `pause`, the same events), plus the
  queue's songs ahead given at once (`setUpcoming`), since the page may not be able to ask for
  them in the background.
- **The check** (E6b-D7's first part): the background measurement plays a queue of three 20 s
  silences through the plugin instead of the page's `<audio>`, sends the app away for two minutes,
  and **fails unless both changes of song happened while hidden** and Android lists a media
  session of the app's, playing. The rest of the check is unchanged.

**1b. The player's phone output** (after 1a passes in CI): `lib/player.ts` plays through
`PhoneAudio` on a phone (E6b-D2), handing it the current song and up to 50 after it (each opened
by the root's `openMedia`, which gives a media URL with a 12 h token). An `advance` moves the queue
on as E5's gapless advance does; a change of the queue sends the songs ahead again; a song the
plugin cannot play falls back to the `<audio>` element. The lock screen shows the title, artist,
album and cover the library has.

**Not in step 1**: the played counts while the page is suspended (step 3), offline songs (step 4),
iOS (step 2), the release's APK (step 5).

## Extension — 2026-11-11 20:24 — What the owner wants to try, and Android's bit-perfect DAC output (proposal)

**Summary**: Step 1a's first run, and two requests from the owner: the four ways they want to use
jackbird (below), and **bit-perfect output to a DAC on Android**, to be added to E6b. Both change
E6b's plan of 12:37 (2026-11-10): proposed here, for the owner to approve.

### Step 1a's first run

`mobile.yml` failed for `3beb5a1`
([run 43093449558](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/43093449558)), with a finding:

- **The native player worked**: the plugin's commands reached Kotlin, Media3's session was listed
  by Android as `PLAYING`, and it went through the queue by itself, on the third song 30 s after
  the app left the screen.
- **Then Android stopped the service**, between 30 s and 60 s away. The app's process stayed
  (state 10, then 15), not a foreground service (state 4). Media3 makes a service foreground only
  for a session it knows of. It knows of a session once a `MediaController` asks for it, or once
  the session is added by hand. `PlayerPlugin` uses the player directly and never adds the session.
  So there was no notification, no foreground service, and Android stopped the service like any
  other started in the background.
- Fixed in `767ce77`: `PlayerService` adds its session (`addSession`). To be confirmed in CI.

| 3 | **The same, from outside, through intergate** | the service with sign-in on, its address set in **Sign-in → The instance's address**, intergate's client beside it (`docs/install.md`, *From outside home*) | possible today; worth a walkthrough by hand |

So 2 and 3 can be tried now. 4 and 1 need an APK on a real phone soon. **Proposed changes to

### E6b-D10: bit-perfect output to a DAC on Android (proposal)

**What Android gives**: Android mixes every app's sound in one mixer at its own rate (48 kHz, as a
rule) and resamples into it, so nothing played the usual way is bit-perfect. **Android 14
(API 34)** added a mixer behaviour for USB devices, `AudioMixerAttributes.MIXER_BEHAVIOR_BIT_PERFECT`.
An app asks for it with `AudioManager.setPreferredMixerAttributes(…)` for media on a given USB
device, at a rate, depth and channel count the device lists (`getSupportedMixerAttributes`). From
then on, while that app plays at exactly that format, the mixer passes its samples through
untouched. Meanwhile the system's other sounds are not mixed in, and the volume is the DAC's own.
Below Android 14 there is no way to it short of a USB driver of one's own, which is not proposed.

**What the player does** (`crates/player`'s Kotlin):

- An **output setting**, like the desktop's (E5): *shared* (the default: Android's mixer) or
  *bit-perfect when a USB DAC allows it*. Shown on Android 14 and later.
- **With bit-perfect on and a USB DAC connected**:
  - before each song, if the DAC lists the song's rate and depth, the player asks for that format
    with the bit-perfect behaviour;
  - ExoPlayer outputs the decoded samples at the file's own depth: 16 bits as they are, 24 bits in
    a 24- or 32-bit integer, or float when that is all the DAC lists (float holds 24-bit samples
    exactly). Today ExoPlayer turns 24-bit audio into 16 bits by default.
  - A song whose format the DAC does not list plays shared, and says so. When the DAC is unplugged
    or the setting is off, the preference is cleared.
- **What sounds is shown**, as on the desktop (`output.sounding`): the file's format and the way to
  the DAC: *bit-perfect*, *resampled by Android*, or *16 bits*. The phone's player reports it with
  each song. The faceplate and the preferences already show this for E5.
- **Checks**: the choice of format (a song's format against the device's list) is a pure function
  with unit tests. CI's emulator has no USB DAC, so the path is **checked by hand by the owner** on
  the Pixel 10 (Android 16) with a DAC, and noted per phone (the CMF's and the OnePlus's systems may
  not offer it).
- **Estimate**: 2 d.

### Questions for the owner

1. **The changes above**: local files on Android in E6b (E6b-D9), bit-perfect output (E6b-D10),
   and the new order (the APK right after step 1)?
2. **Which DAC** will be used with the Pixel 10, and its formats (up to 24/96, 24/192, 32 bits)?
   This is for the check by hand.

## Extension — 2026-11-11 20:52 — The owner approved 20:24; step 1b pushed; step 2's plan: the APK

**Summary**: The owner approved 20:24's changes (*"Dale"*): local files on Android (E6b-D9) and
bit-perfect output (E6b-D10) are in E6b, in the new order. 20:24's second question (which DAC)
is still open, for step 4. Step 1b is pushed (`6148a7f`): `lib/player.ts` plays through
`PhoneAudio` on Android and hands it the next 50 tracks. `mobile.yml`'s runs for `767ce77` and
`6148a7f` are still queued on GitHub's side, behind a run whose iOS job has not ended, so step 1 is
not confirmed yet.

### Step 2's plan: the APK

- **A workflow of its own, `phone-release.yml`**, instead of `release: true` on `mobile.yml`
  (07:28, 2026-11-10). The release must not wait for the phone checks (8 to 40 minutes, the
  emulator and simulator). And run by hand (**Actions → phone-release → Run workflow**) on any
  branch, it gives the owner an APK to try before a release, as the run's artifact. It is
  `workflow_call` (from `release.yml`, with the tag and the version) and `workflow_dispatch`.
- **The build**: `tauri android init --ci`, then `tauri android build --apk --target aarch64 armv7
  x86_64` (one universal APK, release, without the check's switch).
  - `http://` addresses are allowed: Tauri's generated Gradle file sets `usesCleartextTraffic` to
    `false` outside debug builds, and the job sets it to `true`. The job fails if the line is not
    found, rather than ship an app that cannot reach `http://192.168.…`.
- **Signing**: `zipalign`, then `apksigner`.
  - With the four secrets of 19:58, the APK is signed with the owner's key:
    `jackbird-vX.Y.Z-android.apk`.
  - Without them, it is signed with a key made for that run: `jackbird-vX.Y.Z-android-testkey.apk`,
    with a warning in the run. It installs, but each new one installs only after the old app is
    removed.
- **`release.yml`** calls it (`secrets: inherit`), waits for it, and attaches the APK beside the

## Extension — 2026-11-11 22:47 — Step 3 grows: a library's sources, a folder or a server (proposal)

**Summary**: The owner said to go on with step 3 (*"Dale, sigue con el paso 3"*) and asked for a
change to it: **choosing a folder becomes choosing a source for the library**, in the app and on
the web. A source is **a folder** or **a remote server**. This also answers the question of
2026-11-11 (owner): the desktop app cannot reach a remote instance today, only the phone can.
Since this changes how the app talks to services, it is proposed here before any of it is built.

### How it is today

- **One service at a time.** `src/lib/sources/service.ts` keeps one address (`base`), one token
  and one media token. Every root of a service, the account (`lib/account.ts`), people, the family
  library's own features (imports, suggestions, favourites, counts) go through it. Which service
  is fixed when the page starts:
  - the page's origin, when the service serves it;
  - on the desktop, its own installed service (S2);
  - on a phone, the instance typed in the first dialog (`views/instance-dialog.html`), which cannot
    be closed.
- **"Add a folder"** (`pickSource`) opens whatever that runtime has: the browser's picker, the
  system's dialog, or the service's folder chooser (`views/folder-dialog.html`). A phone has no
  folders of its own.

### Proposed (E6b-D11): a library's sources

- **"Add to the library…"** replaces "Add a folder" and "Open a library". It asks which source,
  then that source's own steps:

  | Source | Browser (static) | Web served by a service | Desktop app | Android app |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | **A folder of this device** | the browser's picker (as now) | a folder of the server's machine (as now) | the system's dialog (its own backend, or its installed service, as now) | the phone's folders, with "All files access" (E6b-D9, below) |
  | **A jackbird server** | not in E6b (see question 2) | not offered: it is a server already | its address, then sign in | its address, then sign in (replaces the first dialog) |

  A server added that way then offers **its family library**, and **its machine's folders**
  (for its owners and admins, as on its own web).
- **Several servers at once**, alongside the device's folders. `service.ts` becomes a client per
  server: the address, the token (kept per address) and the media token. Each root holds the
  client of its server. The page's own origin and the desktop's installed service are clients
  like any other.
- **The account follows the active root**: sign-in, profiles, people, and the family library's
  features (import, suggestions, favourites, counts) speak to the server of the root in front. The
  preferences' "Account" says which server it is. Signing in is asked when a server is added.
- **What the session keeps**: a server's root is kept with its address (`saved` holds the address
  and the path, and its id carries the address), so the same path on two machines is two roots,
  and each reopens on its own server after a restart. A phone that kept an instance from the
  current first dialog reopens it as a server source, so nothing is lost on update.
- **Phones start with an empty library** and "Add to the library…". There is no blocking address
  dialog any more.
- **Web on a static host** (GitHub Pages) reaching a remote server needs that server to allow the
  page's origin (CORS). The service allows only the apps' origins today. So it is not offered in
  E6b; see question 2.

### E6b-D9, local files on Android (unchanged in what it does, now one of the sources)

- **The permission**: "All files access" (`MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`). The app asks for it when
  "A folder of this device" is chosen and it is not granted: Android's own settings page for it
  opens, and the app checks again on its return. A small Kotlin command in `crates/player`'s
  plugin (renamed for what it now does: the phone's native side) checks and asks.
- **The folder chooser**: the same view as the service's (`views/folder-dialog.html`), over the
  phone's storage from `/storage/emulated/0`. The app lists folders with a command of its own on
  `crates/core`.
- **Opening it**: the desktop app's commands (`open_root`, `walk`, `inspect`, `save`, `rename`…)
  work on Android as they are, on real paths: tags, checksums and renames as on a computer.
- **Playing**: the native player takes the file's `file://` path. The page's `<audio>` fallback
  takes Tauri's asset URL.

### Steps (estimates)

1. `service.ts` as a client per server; roots and the session keep their server; the account
   follows the active root. No visible change yet. Checks: the unit tests of the new helpers
   (the saved form, a root's id), `npm test`, the desktop and service checks as now (1.5 d).
2. "Add to the library…" with its two sources, on every runtime, and the phone's first dialog
   replaced (1 d).
3. Android's folders: the permission, the chooser, the native player on `file://` (1 d).
4. **Checks in the emulator**: `phone-check.mjs` copies the audio fixtures into the emulator's
   `/sdcard/Music` and grants the permission (`appops`). The check opens that folder as a source,
   lists it, and plays a FLAC from it through the native player; it also adds the server, as now
   (0.5 d).
5. The walkthrough, and `docs/usage.md` (the sources).

### Questions for the owner

1. **Several servers at once** (recommended: it costs the same once each root has its client, and
   a phone may use the home server and another), or one at a time?
2. **The web on a static host** reaching a server: later (recommended), or in E6b? It needs a
   list of allowed origins in the service's settings, for its owners to fill.

## Extension — 2026-11-11 23:06 — Step 3, parts 1 to 3: a client per server, "Add to the library…", the phone's own folders

**Summary**: Step 3's first three parts (22:47) are built. Parts 1 and 2 pass the service's three
browser checks here. Part 3 is checked in CI's emulator. One decision differs from 22:47, below.

### 3.1: a client per server (`2f3ab0d`)

- `src/lib/sources/service.ts`: a `ServiceClient` per address, with its token (kept under
  `jackbird.serviceToken:<address>`; the home server still reads the old key once) and its media
  token. `serviceAt(address)` gives it, one per address.
  - Every root of a server holds its client (`RootSource.server`). Its `saved` form carries the
    address unless it is on the home server: `jackbird-server:<address>#<path>`
    (`savedOnServer`, `parseSaved` in `src/helpers/instance.ts`, with tests). `restoreSource`
    reopens it on its own server.
- **The server spoken to now** (`currentServer`): the account, people, imports and the family
  library's features go through it. It follows the root in front (`followServer` in
  `lib/account.ts`, started by `followActiveRoot` in `lib/session.ts`). Coming to a server where
  the session is gone asks to sign in there.
  - A refusal from a server that is not the one in front is its roots' own error; it no longer
    reloads the page.
  - A play of a library's song is counted on that song's own server.
- The family library of "the account" is the current server's (`kind === "library"` and its
  server), no longer `saved === "@library"`.

### 3.2: "Add to the library…" (`6467c6a`)

- `src/lib/adding.ts` and `src/views/sources-dialog.html`. The sources each runtime offers:
  - **"A folder of this device"**: the browser's picker when no service serves the page; the
    desktop app's dialog; an Android phone's folders (3.3).
  - **"A folder of {server}"**: for the owners of the server in front. It is the page's own server
    on the web, or one the app added.
  - **"A jackbird server"**: the apps only. Its address is checked as before (`/api/info`). The
    sign-in follows there if the server asks. Then its family library and the folders its owners
    opened join the library, the first in front.
- With one source only, the button goes straight to it, as before: the web served by a service
  opens its folder dialog, and the browser opens its picker.
- The sign-in dialog can be closed for a server the person added: the account goes back to the
  home server. The home server's sign-in still cannot be given up.
- The phone's blocking address dialog (`views/instance-dialog.html`) is gone. A phone starts with
  an empty library. The phone check now adds its instance through `addServer`, as a person would.

### A change from 22:47: no carried-over instance on phones

22:47 said a phone that kept an instance from the first dialog would reopen it as a server. It does
not, and `lib/instance.ts` is gone:

- **No APK was shipped before today**, so no phone has such an instance to keep.
- **Keeping it would make two kinds of roots ambiguous**: the phone's own folders and the
  instance's folders would both be kept as bare paths.

A phone has **no home server**: every server is a source, kept with its address.

### 3.3: the phone's own folders (this extension's commit)

- **The permission**: `storage_access` and `ask_storage_access` in `crates/player`'s Kotlin plugin.
  - Android 11 and later: "All files access" (`MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`), through Android's page
    for it.
  - Before Android 11: the storage permission, and `requestLegacyExternalStorage` for Android 10.
  - The page asks again once it is back in front (`src/lib/device-storage.ts`).
  - The crate keeps its name (`tauri-plugin-jackbird-player`), although it now does more than
    play.
- **The chooser**: `device_folders` lists a folder's subfolders, hidden ones left out, from
  `/storage/emulated/0` (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`). `lib/folders.ts`'s dialog takes where its
  folders come from.
- **Opening**: the desktop app's own commands (`open_root` and the rest), on the real path.
- **Playing**: the phone's player opens a `file://` URL (`fileUrl` in `src/helpers/output.ts`,
  tested). The page's `<audio>` fallback keeps the asset URL.
- **The check** (`mobile.yml`, Android):
  - the fixtures are pushed to `/sdcard/Music/jackbird-check`, and "All files access" is granted
    with `appops`;
  - the page checks that access is granted, that the folder is listed, opened and walked
    (3 songs or more), and that a FLAC of it plays through the native player from its file;
  - the check fails otherwise.

## Extension — 2026-11-11 23:29 — Step 4's plan: bit-perfect output to a USB DAC on Android (E6b-D10)

**Summary**: The owner said to go on with step 4 (*"Ok, sigue con él"*), before trying the APK.
How E6b-D10 (20:24) is built:

- **Where the format is chosen**: in Media3's audio sink, the one place that knows the exact PCM
  format an `AudioTrack` will be made with, just before it is made. `crates/player`'s player wraps
  its sink (`ForwardingAudioSink`). In `configure`, with bit-perfect on, Android 14 or later and a
  USB device connected, it looks among the device's mixer attributes
  (`getSupportedMixerAttributes`) for one with the bit-perfect behaviour and the same rate,
  channels and encoding. If one is found, it is set for media (`setPreferredMixerAttributes`);
  otherwise it is cleared, and the song plays through Android's mixer.
- **Depth**: the player decodes to float (Media3's float output; Android's FLAC decoder gives float
  from Android 7). Float holds 16- and 24-bit samples exactly, so 24-bit songs keep their depth
  instead of being cut to 16. The bit-perfect match is then a float mixer format at the song's
  rate.
  - **The risk**: a DAC that offers only integer formats for bit-perfect (16, 24, 32 bits) gets no
    match. The player then reports what the DAC offers, and the next step is integer output (a
    sink of our own for 24 and 32 bits). The FiiO KA11 on the Pixel 10 will tell.
- **What sounds**, as on the desktop (`output.sounding`, E5):
  - the codec, the song's depth and rate (Media3's track format);
  - the DAC's name;
  - the way: `bit-perfect`, or `through the system's mixer`;
  - when it is not bit-perfect, the formats the DAC offers for it.

  Shown on the faceplate's badge and in the preferences.
- **The setting**: in the preferences on Android: "Bit-perfect to a USB DAC" (off by default),
  kept on the phone. It takes effect from the next song. Below Android 14 it is shown as not
  available.
- **Checks**:
  - the choice of mixer format is a pure function in Kotlin, small enough to read;
  - the page's side is checked by `npm test` (`describeFormat` without a depth);
  - CI's emulator has no USB DAC, so the check there is only that playback still works with the
    setting on;
  - **by hand**: the owner on the Pixel 10 with the FiiO KA11, with 16/44.1 and 24/96 FLAC files,
    looking at the badge.

## Extension — 2026-11-11 23:33 — Step 4 built; the app never asks for all files; the phone's folders through Android's folder picker (proposal)

**Summary**: Step 4 is built (`a530378`) and waits for CI and the owner's check with the DAC.
While it was being built, the owner tried the 3.3 APK and **refused "All files access"**: the app
must never ask for it, only for the folder the person chooses. This extension takes 3.3's
permission out, and proposes how the phone's own folders come back.

### Step 4 as built (`a530378`)

- `crates/player/android/src/main/java/BitPerfect.kt`:
  - `BitPerfectSink` wraps Media3's sink and calls `BitPerfect.prepare` with each AudioTrack's PCM
    format before it is made;
  - `prepare` sets a bit-perfect mixer format that matches (rate, channels, encoding), or clears
    it;
  - it reports the way: the DAC's name, whether bit-perfect, and the formats the DAC offers
    bit-perfect.

  Checked against Media3 1.5.1's sources:
  - `buildAudioSink`'s signature;
  - the decoder asked for float output when the sink takes float;
  - the FLAC track's format carrying its own depth.
- The player decodes to float (`setEnableFloatOutput(true)`).
- The plugin:
  - `set_output` (the setting) and `output_info` (Android 14 or later, the USB DAC connected);
  - a `sounding` event: the codec, the song's depth and rate, and the way, as the desktop's
    `started`.
- The page:
  - `PhoneAudio` turns `sounding` into `started`, so `output.sounding`, the faceplate's badge and
    the preferences' line show it as on the desktop;
  - the preferences on Android: "Bit-perfect to a USB DAC", what is connected, and what the DAC
    offers;
  - `describeFormat` and `shortFormat` leave out a depth that is not known (tested).

### "All files access" taken out (this extension's commit)

- **Taken out**:
  - `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, the legacy storage permissions and `requestLegacyExternalStorage`
    from the plugin's manifest;
  - `storage_access` and `ask_storage_access`;
  - `device_folders`;
  - `src/lib/device-storage.ts`;
  - the phone check's device folder.
- **"A folder of this device" is not offered on phones** until the folder picker below is built.
  No build of this branch asks for all files from now on.
- **The APKs built before** (`phone-release` runs 1 and 2) still ask for it. A new run replaces
  them as the one to try.

### Proposed: the phone's folders through Android's folder picker (E6b-D9, revised)

- **Choosing**: Android's own folder picker (`ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT_TREE`). The person picks one
  folder, and the app keeps a lasting permission for **that folder alone**
  (`takePersistableUriPermission`, read and write). No permission is asked in Android's settings.
- **What the app gets**: a `content://` address for the folder, not a path. The folder is kept by
  that address in the session, and reopened by it after a restart, while the permission lasts. The
  person can withdraw the permission in Android's settings.
- **Listing and small files**: in Kotlin (`DocumentsContract`), as the plugin's commands:
  walk (one query per folder), list, stat, read and write small files (`.meta/`, playlists),
  rename, move, delete, mkdir. The page gets a `RootSource` like the others
  (`src/lib/sources/phone.ts`).
- **Tags and checksums**, still by `crates/core`, streamed, without the audio passing through the
  page:
  - Kotlin opens the file (`openFileDescriptor`) and hands Rust the open file as
    `/proc/self/fd/<n>`, a path Rust can open (no `unsafe`);
  - `crates/core` gains `inspect` and `save` on such a path (today they go through a `Root`);
  - a save that fits in the old tags' room writes in place;
  - a save that does not fit writes a new document beside the old one, checks its audio, and puts
    it in the old one's place, as `save` does on a computer.
- **Playing**: the native player plays the `content://` address itself (Media3 reads it). The
  page's `<audio>` fallback is not offered for these files.
- **Checks in the emulator**: the picker cannot be driven from the check. So the check grants the
  folder the way the picker would (`adb shell content` and the `DocumentsUI` intent are
  unreliable), or calls the source's commands with a folder granted in a debug-only way. This is to
  be settled when built. The owner's check by hand on the Pixel is the real one.
- **Estimate**: 2 d, in two parts: playing and reading tags first, then saving tags, renaming and
  playlists.

### Question for the owner

1. **The folder picker** as above? In particular, **two parts** (first play and read, then edit
   and rename; recommended), or both at once?

## Extension — 2026-11-12 00:31 — The phone's folders through Android's folder picker (E6b-D9, first part); the app clear of the phone's bars

**Summary**: The owner approved 23:33's folder picker, with a key in the "Folder" tab that opens the
system's dialog (*"habrá un botón que levante el diálogo de sistema para elegir la carpeta a la que
dar acceso"*). Its first part is built: walking, reading tags and checksums, playing, and the
small files (`.meta/`, playlists). Saving tags is the second part: until then it says so. While
it was being built, the owner saw the top of the app under the status bar and the selfie camera
on the Pixel 10; that is fixed too.

### The folder picker

- **Kotlin** (`crates/player/android/src/main/java/Folders.kt`, the plugin's `folder_*` commands):
  - `pick_folder` opens Android's picker (`ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT_TREE`, at `initial` if given)
    and keeps the permission for the folder chosen (`takePersistableUriPermission`, read and
    write); `folder_check` reopens it while the permission lasts.
  - `folder_walk`, `folder_list`, `folder_stat`, `folder_read`, `folder_write`,
    `folder_delete`, `folder_rename` (rename and move), `folder_mkdir`: by the path in the
    folder, through `DocumentsContract`, document ids cached by path.
  - `folder_address`: a file's `content://` address, which the native player plays.
  - `folder_open` and `folder_close`: a file opened for Rust.
- **Rust**: `inspect_fd` (Android only) reads `/proc/self/fd/<fd>`, through
  `jackbird_core::inspect_file` (new; tested on Linux through a descriptor's own path, on every
  tag fixture, against `inspect`). The tag engine reads the file Android opened, streamed, with
  no `unsafe`.
- **The page**: `src/lib/sources/phone.ts`, a `RootSource` kept by its `content://`
  address. `pickSource` and `restoreSource` use it on a phone. `save` refuses, saying it comes
  next.
- **The dialog**: the "Folder" tab has a key per folder source, "Choose a folder…" first (the
  device's own dialog), with a line saying jackbird reaches that folder and nothing else.
- **The check** (`mobile.yml`, Android):
  - the fixtures and a tagged FLAC (`fixtures/tags/flac-full.flac`, titled "Before") are pushed
    to `/sdcard/Music/jackbird-check`;
  - the page opens the picker there, and `phone-check.mjs` taps "Use this folder" and "Allow"
    (read with uiautomator);
  - the page walks the folder, reads the FLAC's title through Rust, and plays a FLAC from its
    `content://` address;
  - the check fails unless all of that happened.

### Clear of the phone's bars

Android 15 draws apps edge to edge, and the webview's `env(safe-area-inset-*)` was not relied on.

- The plugin reads what covers the page from the system (the bars and the camera's cutout,
  `WindowInsetsCompat`), in CSS pixels: `safe_area`, and an `insets` event on each change.
- `src/lib/safe-area.ts` sets them as `--mm-safe-top`, `-right`, `-bottom` and `-left`.
- The faceplate starts below the status bar, with the chassis behind it. The footbar and the
  phone's sheets keep clear of the navigation bar, and the sides keep clear of a cutout in
  landscape.

Seen in Chromium as an Android phone, with 48 px of status bar given.

## Extension — 2026-11-12 00:48 — The folder picker works in the emulator; reading tags through /proc/self/fd is refused (a question for the owner)

**Summary**: `mobile.yml` for `8440ad8`
([run 43127928701](https://github.com/jgermade/jackbird/actions/runs/43127928701)) fails on
Android. Everything else passes there and in the other workflows, and the APK is built.

- **What works**:
  - Android's folder picker opened at the folder; the check tapped "USE THIS FOLDER" and "ALLOW",
    and the app got the folder;
  - the server source;
  - the native queue in the background, 3 of 3 changes of song while hidden.
- **What failed**: reading a file's tags: `/proc/self/fd/225: Permission denied (os error 13)`.
  Opening `/proc/self/fd/<n>` does not use the descriptor Android's document provider opened. It
  opens the file again by its real path, under `/storage/emulated/0`. Without access to all files
  (which the owner refused, rightly), the app may not open that path. 23:33's plan assumed
  otherwise.

### How Rust can read the file instead (for the owner to choose)

1. **Rust takes the descriptor itself** (recommended):
   - the plugin hands over the descriptor (`detachFd`), and Rust makes a `File` of it
     (`File::from_raw_fd`): `crates/core`'s `inspect` and `save` work on it as on any file,
     streamed;
   - this is how Tauri's own file plugin does it on Android;
   - it is one line of `unsafe` (Rust cannot know that a number is a file it may own), in one
     function of `src-tauri`, Android only. The workspace forbids `unsafe` today, with one
     exception (`crates/tags-wasm`'s exports), so this would be a second, written down beside the
     first.
2. **Rust reads ranges given to it**, as the tag crates already do (the head and the last bytes):
   - the page asks the plugin for the ranges and Rust for the layout and the tags, round after
     round, the bytes crossing as base64;
   - the plugin hashes the file in Kotlin;
   - saving writes the splice from Kotlin;
   - no `unsafe`, but three times the code, slower, and a second way of reading and writing files
     beside `crates/core`'s.
3. **A copy in the app's cache**, inspected by path and then removed: no `unsafe` and little
   code, but every song is read twice and written once, and checking a whole library's checksums
   copies all of it.

Until the owner chooses, the branch's `mobile.yml` stays red on this one point.

## Extension — 2026-11-13 07:45 — E6b-D9's second part: saving tags, renaming and playlists in the phone's folders

**Summary**: The owner asked to take it up (2026-11-13, *"dale caña … a E6b-D9 -segunda parte-"*).
Built as 23:33's plan said, on the descriptor Android's document provider opens (01:03's
option 1). Renaming and playlists already went through the plugin's `folder_rename` and
`folder_write` (the root's VFS); what was missing was saving tags, and a check of all three.

### Saving tags

- **`crates/core`**: `save_open(file, shown, edit, beside)` is `save` on a file already open for
  reading and writing. It uses the same splice and safeguards:
  - when the new tags fit, they are written in place (`Saved::Done`);
  - when they do not, they go into the empty file `beside()` gives, with the old audio copied
    after them and checked (`Saved::Beside`); the caller puts that file in the old one's place;
  - either way, the audio region hashed afterwards must match the source's.

  Tested on every fixture with three edits: the same bytes as the engine's `write`, both routes
  taken, and a refusal changes nothing (`save_open_gives_what_save_gives`).
- **The plugin** (Kotlin, `Folders.kt` and `PlayerPlugin.kt`), two commands that only Rust calls
  (not among the plugin's permissions, like `folderDetach`):
  - `folderBeside`: a new hidden file beside the song (`.<name>.jackbird-save`, or the name the
    provider gives it to keep it unique), opened `rw`, its descriptor given up;
  - `folderReplace`: the old file removed, then the new one renamed to its name. If the old one
    cannot be removed, the new one is removed and nothing has changed. If the rename then fails,
    the new file is kept, since it is the only copy, and the error names it.
  - Also fixed: `rename` failed on a provider that keeps a document's id when it renames it
    (`renameDocument` then gives no new address).
- **Rust**: `tauri_plugin_jackbird_player::{open_beside, replace_file, remove_file}`, and the app's
  `phone_save` (Android only):
  - it opens the song `rw` and runs `save_open`, with `open_beside` as the file beside;
  - after `Beside`, it calls `replace_file`;
  - on an error before that, it removes the file beside.
- **The page**: `src/lib/sources/phone.ts`'s `save` calls `phone_save`. The text
  `sources.phoneSaveSoon` is gone.

### The check (`mobile.yml`, Android)

`phone-check.mjs` also pushes `fixtures/tags/mp3-no-tag.mp3` to the folder. After reading and
playing, the page:

- saves a title into `flac-full.flac` (its padding takes it, in place);
- saves a title into `mp3-no-tag.mp3`: it has no tags, so the new ones cannot fit; they go beside
  and that file takes the old one's place;
- reads both back: the title, the same audio checksum, the file still at its path, no
  `.jackbird-save` left;
- renames the MP3 into a folder (`renamed/Grown on the phone.mp3`) and reads its title there;
- writes `check.m3u8` and reads it back.

The check fails unless all of that happened.

### Checked here

- `cargo test -p jackbird-core`;
- `cargo clippy -p jackbird -p tauri-plugin-jackbird-player --target aarch64-linux-android -- -D
  warnings`, which checks the Android-only Rust;
- `npm test`.

The Kotlin is not compiled here (no Android SDK): `mobile.yml` builds it and runs the check on the
next push. The owner's check by hand on the Pixel 10 remains the real one.

Commit: `feat(phone): save tags in the phone's folders, in place or beside; the check renames and writes a playlist there too`

## Extension — 2026-11-13 10:00 — The service's checks drew ports that browsers refuse

**Summary**: `release.yml` for `d88b599` failed in "Service on macos-latest" with `signin-check:
the service did not start`. The service had started and printed its address, `localhost:5060`.
The same commit passed on the push's own `service.yml`, on another port.

**The cause**:

- The checks picked their port as 4646 plus a random number below 1000, and the mail sink's port
  as that plus 1000.
- Those ranges hold 5060 and 5061 (SIP's ports), and, for the sink, 6000 and 6566. All four are
  in the Fetch standard's list of bad ports, which Node's `fetch` and Chromium refuse at once
  ("bad port").
- Reproduced here: with the service up on 5060, `fetch("http://localhost:5060/api/info")` fails
  with `bad port`, and the service never sees the request.
- So any check that drew one of those ports failed, on any system: about once in 250 runs for
  each port drawn.

**Fixed**:

- `scripts/free-port.cjs`: `freePort()` asks the system for a free port on 127.0.0.1. It keeps
  the port only if it is free on ::1 too (or the machine has no ::1) and is not one of the Fetch
  standard's bad ports. It is synchronous, so the checks keep their ports as constants.
- `service-check.cjs`, `signin-check.cjs` and `library-check.cjs` take their ports, and the mail
  sink's, from it.
- `service.yml` runs again when the helper changes.

**Checked here**: the three checks pass on the release build of the service.

Commit: `fix(checks): the service's checks take free ports from the system, never ones browsers refuse`

## 2026-11-12 21:45 UTC — Bit-perfect on Android applied at once; refusals logged

The owner's question of 2026-11-12, with a picture of a phone with a FiiO KA11 connected (Android
14+, the switch on, the DAC offering 16-bit/44.1 among others, an MP3 at 16-bit/44.1 playing "by
the system's mixer"): "can we enable bit-perfect on Android?".

It is there (E6b-D10, `BitPerfect.kt`): the player's sink asks Android for the mixer's format
(`setPreferredMixerAttributes`, `MIXER_BEHAVIOR_BIT_PERFECT`) each time it makes an AudioTrack.
Why it was not taken, read from the code (no device here):

- **The setting waited for a new AudioTrack**, and Media3 reuses the one it has while the format
  does not change: turned on in the middle of a song, it did not reach that song, and very likely
  not the next ones of the same format either. Now `setOutput` (`PlayerPlugin.kt`) marks it
  pending and seeks to where the song is; the seek flushes the sink, which lets the AudioTrack go,
  and `BitPerfectSink.flush()` asks for the mixer's format again before the next one is made. The
  setting's hint no longer says "from the next song" (both catalogues).
- **A refusal was silent**: an exception from the call was swallowed. It is logged now
  (`jackbird` in logcat), as is a format the DAC does not offer and Android answering false.
- `AndroidManifest.xml`: `MODIFY_AUDIO_SETTINGS`, a normal permission granted on install, in case
  the device wants it for the mixer's preference.

Not built nor tried here (no Android SDK in this container): to be checked on the phone with the
DAC, the switch turned on while a song plays (the display and Preferences should say bit-perfect),
and `adb logcat -s jackbird` if not.

Commit: `fix(android): bit-perfect applied at once, not from a new AudioTrack; refusals logged`

## 2026-11-12 23:55 UTC — The service's media handler: ServeFile's infallible error

The owner's report of 2026-11-12 (pull request #21's CI): clippy, with warnings as errors, fails

as before.

crate but the desktop app and its player plugin (they need GTK, not installed here); the
service's tests. Pushed with `[ci tags] [ci service]` (the pull request is a draft).
