## Extension — 2026-11-13 03:15 UTC — Disc, album artist and compilation: read, edited and saved

**Summary**: The owner missed fields in the tag editor (*"número de pista, número de disco, marcar
como recopilación, etc"*; the track number was already there). Three fields join the engine's six,
end to end, and the owner's idea for the compilation box (*"un checkbox 'recopilación' en el input
Artista del Álbum"*) is how the editor shows it.

- **The engine** (`crates/tags`): `Field::Disc`, `Field::AlbumArtist`, `Field::Compilation`,
  appended to `Field::ALL` (the wire only grows at its end).
  - MP3: `TPOS`, `TPE2` and iTunes' `TCMP` (v2.2's `TPA`, `TP2`, `TCP` read).
  - FLAC: `DISCNUMBER` with `DISCTOTAL`/`TOTALDISCS` as the track number's total is kept;
    `ALBUMARTIST` (or `ALBUM ARTIST`, written back to the key it was read from); `COMPILATION`.
  - The compilation is the file's text: `1` marks one (`isCompilation`), anything else does not.
  - Two tests used `TPOS` as a frame no field touched; they use `TCOM` now. The fixtures already
    held these frames: `cases.json`'s expectations gain them (`make.mjs` says the same; the binary
    fixtures are not made again).
- **The wire** (`crates/tags-wasm`, `src/helpers/tagwire.ts`): nine fields.
- **`crates/api`, `crates/store`**: the fields with `serde(default)`, so `.meta/`, an older
  interface's save and a catalogue imported before them read as empty; the layers carry them;
  OpenSubsonic gives `discNumber`.
- **Saving without losing**: a library read before these fields holds them empty. `editFor` (and
  `edit_for` in Rust) send the six first fields whole as before, and the three new ones **only
  when they change**, so a save from an old cache never empties a file's disc.
- **Reading them again**: a `.meta/` record without them is read again on the next opening (no
  checksum given, so the tags come back even for an unchanged file): `readsEveryField`,
  `withEveryField`.
- **The library**:
  - the disc of a track is its tag's number, else a `CD2`/`Disc 2` folder;
  - albums group by album artist (a compilation's tracks of many artists together);
  - Compilations lists albums a track marks as one, or with several artists and no album artist;
  - Albums by artist puts each album under its album artist ("Various artists" for a compilation);
  - Songs shows "Artist - Album" beside each title (the owner's request of the same message).
- **The editor**: Title; Artist and Album artist (with "Compilation" inside it, at its right, as
  the "Override" box is: ticked, unticked, or mixed across a selection); Album and Year; Track and
  Disc; Genre. Info lists the three.

Checked here: `npm test` (416); `cargo fmt`, clippy with warnings as errors and the tests of the
tag crates, `api`, `core`, `store` and `service`; in the browser (the landing's embedded app, the
sample music): the editor's fields, a save marking a compilation and a disc, read back by Info.

## Extension — 2026-11-13 04:40 UTC — Bit-perfect to a DAC that takes only integers (E6b-D10's risk)

**Summary**: The owner: *"dale con la salida en enteros para el DAC"*. 23:29 (2026-11-11) foresaw
it: the player decodes to float, and a DAC that offers bit-perfect only in integer formats (the
FiiO KA11 on the Pixel 10: 16-bit/44.1 among others) never matched, so songs went through
Android's mixer.

What Media3 1.5.1 allows (read in its sources): `DefaultAudioSink` writes an AudioTrack in 16-bit
integers or in float only; a 24- or 32-bit integer input is cut to 16 bits (`ToInt16PcmAudioProcessor`)
unless float output is on. But it takes an `AudioTrackProvider`, and float and 32-bit integers
are both 4 bytes a sample.

- **The choice** (`BitPerfect.pick`, pure): among the formats the DAC offers bit-perfect at the
  song's rate and channels: float first (as before); else **32-bit integers** for a song of up to
  24 bits (exact: float holds those samples exactly, and `round(x * 2^31)` gives each back,
  shifted into 32 bits); else **16-bit integers** for a song of up to 16 bits (a lossy one counts
  as 16, its decoder's usual depth); else the mixer, as before.
- **The song's own depth**: the sink only sees float, so a renderer of ours (`PlayerService`,
  `MediaCodecAudioRenderer.onInputFormatChanged`) notes the file's sample (Media3's FLAC
  extractor gives its bits per sample) before its samples reach the sink.
- **The sink** (`BitPerfectSink`): for 32 bits, the inner sink stays configured for float (its
  float path changes no sample) and gets the converted samples; `IntegerTracks`, the AudioTrack
  provider, makes that track `ENCODING_PCM_32BIT`. For 16 bits, the inner sink is configured for
  16-bit input, which it writes as it is. The converted buffer is kept until the inner sink has
  taken it all (Media3 wants the same buffer back), and the decoder's buffer moves on with it. The
  spectrum still reads the decoder's float. The setting turned on while a song plays: the flush
  that applies it now also configures the inner sink anew.
- **What it reports**: the way's sample is now what reaches the DAC (`32-bit`, `16-bit`, `float`).
- **Not covered**: a DAC that offers 24-bit packed only (3 bytes a sample: Media3's sink cannot
  carry it this way; logged); and, with a 16-bit-only DAC, a 24-bit song played gaplessly right
  after a 16-bit one of the same rate, which Media3 does not configure again (it plays at 16 bits,
  as the mixer would have).

Not built here (no Android SDK): pushed with `[ci mobile]`; to be tried by the owner on the Pixel
10 with the KA11 (`adb logcat -s jackbird`, and the display's badge).

## Extension — 2026-11-13 03:33 UTC — Each person's music, shared with whom they choose (plan, with the owner's answers)

(A correction first: the two extensions above headed 04:10 and 04:40 UTC were written at about
03:20 and 03:30 UTC; their times were wrong.)

**Summary**: The owner: *"al conectar a un servidor jackbird, vería mi carpeta y opcionalmente,
las que hayan compartido conmigo, pudiendo seleccionar por usuario que comparte (o la mía)"*, and
*"compartir es algo que decide cada usuario con su carpeta, y elige con quién la comparte"*. F1
made one family library everyone sees; this splits it into one folder ("shelf") per person, each
shared by its owner with whom they choose. The owner's answers (2026-11-13): the songs already in
the library become the instance owner's, shared with everyone; in a folder shared with you, you
listen and keep your own version (F3's layer), and its tags are its owner's; each folder opens as a
tab of its own.

### Decisions

| # | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| SH-D1 | **A shelf per person**: every song belongs to one person's shelf. In the catalogue its path is `@<person>/<path>`; the interface never sees the prefix: each shelf is a root of its own, with the paths it had. Small files (`.meta/`, playlists) are per shelf the same way (`files/@<person>/`). |
| SH-D2 | **The library as it is now** goes to the instance's first owner's shelf, shared with everyone (a migration on opening: paths and small files moved under `@<owner>/`). |
| SH-D3 | **Sharing**: a shelf's owner shares it with everyone (newcomers included) or with chosen people; kept in the catalogue (`shares`). Changed in Preferences ("Share my music"). |
| SH-D4 | **Rights in a shared shelf**: play, download, favourites, playlists of one's own, one's own layer of a song. Tags (the shelf's layer, F1's "family" layer), renames, deletions, uploads and imports are its owner's (with the role editor or more, as before). The safety tools (trash, export, scrub) stay the instance's owners' and admins', over every shelf. |
| SH-D5 | **The same audio in two shelves**: one blob on disk (named by its audio, as before), one song in each shelf; within a shelf, as before (a suggestion, the importer's layer). The trash deletes a blob only when no song uses it any more. |
| SH-D6 | **The interface**: one's own shelf opens as the server's library ("My music"); the sources panel lists "Shared with me", one entry per person whose shelf one may see, each opening as a tab ("Ana's music"). |
| SH-D7 | **OpenSubsonic**: one's own shelf and those shared with one. |
| SH-D8 | **Not in this step**: one's own playlists listing songs of other shelves (a playlist's paths are its root's); sharing a single folder of a shelf. |

### Steps

1. `crates/store`: shelves (the prefix, the migration, the blob no longer unique), shares, per
   shelf listing; tests.
2. `crates/service`: the library's routes take the shelf (`shelf=<person>`, one's own by
   default), its rights, the shares' routes, OpenSubsonic; tests.
3. The interface: the shelves as roots, "Shared with me", the sharing preference; texts in both
   languages.
4. Checks, the walkthrough, CI (`[ci service]`).

## Extension — 2026-11-13 04:20 UTC — Each person's music, shared: built (walkthrough)

**Summary**: The plan of 03:33, steps 1 to 3, built and tried end to end.

- **Store** (`crates/store`, `138306e`): shelves (`shelf_path`, `on_shelf`); the catalogue made
  again without `blob ... unique` (its rows and ids kept), the same audio on two shelves as two
  songs and one blob, which the trash deletes once no song uses it; `shares` (everyone, or
  people); `into_shelves`, the migration onto the first owner's shelf, shared with everyone, done
  once. `Viewer::on(shelf)`: one's own folder ("My playlists") is in every shelf one looks at, its
  Favourites and Recently played listing that shelf's songs (written back, they set that shelf's
  favourites, the others' kept); one's own playlists stay on one's own shelf.
- **Service** (`757ca16`): every library route takes `shelf` (one's own by default); `Shelf` maps
  the shelf's paths to the catalogue's and back, refuses a shelf not shared with one (404,
  `not-shared`) and changes to someone else's (403, `not-yours`), save for one's own layer.
  `library/shelves`, `library/shares`; the trash shows the shelf's path and its owner;
  OpenSubsonic lists one's own and shared shelves. The router's tests now say the new rules (the
  owner's music changed by its owner alone; stale saves by the same person in two windows).
- **Interface** (this commit): a library root carries its shelf (`RootSource.shelf`), and each
  song's call passes it (layers, suggestions, favourites, plays, downloads); favourites are kept
  per shelf. "Shared with me" in the sources' dialog opens someone's music as a tab ("Ana's
  music"), kept by the session (`@library:<id>`); the key to open it shows for listeners too. In
  shared music the editor saves one's own version and the menus offer no rename or removal.
  Preferences: "Share my music", with everyone or with the people ticked, saved at once.
- **Tried here**: a service with three people (Owner, Ana as owner, Luis as listener) and the web
  build: Ana imports two songs (onto her shelf; Luis sees nothing), shares with Luis in
  Preferences (the API then says so), Luis opens "Ana's music" from "Shared with me" and sees
  her songs; through the API, Luis plays a range (206), is refused saving for everyone (403),
  saves his own title (Ana still sees hers), and the Owner, not chosen, gets 404.
- **Checks**: `cargo test` of the store (21) and the service (35), clippy with warnings as errors,
  `npm test` (416).

Not done: one's own playlists listing songs of several people's music (SH-D8); sharing part of
one's music.

(Correction: the extension above headed 04:20 UTC was written at 03:51 UTC.)
